# flai CLI

`flai` is a single static Go binary. It creates and imports projects, manages work items, validates a repo against the standard, prints metrics, and runs the dashboard. It is designed to be used by humans at a terminal and by agents through non-interactive flags.

Source: `./flai` in this monorepo. Module `github.com/bytepunx/system-flow/flai`.

## Configuration

`~/.flai/config.json`, created on first run with defaults. Overridable with `FLAI_CONFIG` and `--config`.

```json
{
  "template": {
    "repo": "https://github.com/bytepunx/system-flow-template",
    "ref": "main"
  },
  "dashboard": {
    "image": "ghcr.io/bytepunx/flaiover",
    "tag": "latest",
    "port": 4242,
    "bind": "0.0.0.0",
    "allow_unsigned": false,
    "push_key": "",
    "push_known_hosts": "",
    "allowed_hosts": ""
  },
  "cache_dir": "~/.flai/cache",
  "author": "alex",
  "worktrees": {
    "relative_paths": false
  }
}
```

`host_actions`, `agent`, `checks`, `forges`, and `import_roots` live in the same file, are absent until set, and are not among `config.Keys`: `flai serve` subcommands manage them (see the `flai serve` rows below). Every key is documented for users in [docs/users/flai.md](../../docs/users/flai.md#configuration).

Template repo cloning goes into `cache_dir/templates/<hash of repo+ref>`, one clone per repo and ref. `template.Source.Ensure` fetches a cached clone at a branch again before each use (`git fetch --depth 1` of the ref and a hard reset to it); a clone at a tag or a commit, its HEAD detached, is reused. When the fetch fails, as offline, it returns `template.ErrStale` and flai warns and uses the clone it has. `flai template update` discards the clone and clones it again. Which ref `flai new`, `flai import`, and `flai upgrade` use is in [Which version is applied](template.md#which-version-is-applied) ([ADR-0103](../adrs/0103-flai-new-import-and-upgrade-follow-the-template-s-releases-the-newest-version.md)). A local path in `template.repo` is used directly without cloning, which is how this monorepo develops against `./template`.

## Commands

| Command | Purpose |
|---------|---------|
| `flai new <dir>` | Create a new conforming monorepo from the template. Prompts for variables when stdin is a terminal; `--var k=v` sets them, `--defaults` never prompts. `--template` and `--ref` override the config source. With no `--ref`, `releaseRef` makes a git template's project at its newest `vX.Y.Z` tag when the config's `template.ref` follows releases (empty, the template's default branch, or a release tag; `template.ListRemote` reads them with one `git ls-remote`), records it as `template.ref` and `template.version` and in the lock, and says "the template's newest release" (`newest_release` in `--json`); another branch or a commit, a template with no version tags, and a local directory are used as given, and when the tags cannot be listed flai warns and uses the ref as given. `--ref` is used as given, except that one spelling a release's version, such as `1.0.60`, names its tag `v1.0.60` ([ADR-0103](../adrs/0103-flai-new-import-and-upgrade-follow-the-template-s-releases-the-newest-version.md)). `--layout key=name` renames a folder, `--force` overwrites, `--no-git` skips `git init`. A required variable left empty, `--var` included, is refused before anything is written; the values are recorded in `system-flow.lock.yaml` (S-0185). |
| `flai import [dir] [--commit] [--trailer]` | Analyse an existing repo, propose the layout, prompt for folder names, create missing structure, offer to move existing markdown into it, write `system-flow.yaml`. The template ref is chosen by `releaseRef` as for `flai new` ([ADR-0103](../adrs/0103-flai-new-import-and-upgrade-follow-the-template-s-releases-the-newest-version.md)), the proposal names it when it is the newest release, and `--json` has it in `template` (`repo`, `ref`, `version`, `newest_release`). `--commit` (S-0098, [ADR-0035](../adrs/0035-repositories-under-folders-the-operator-names-can-be-imported-from-the-board.md)) then runs the repository's tests and commits the import when they pass; see Import flow. |
| `flai serve import add\|remove\|list <folder>` (S-0098, ADR-0035) | The folders whose git repositories without a manifest `flai serve` offers the board for import, kept in the host's configuration as `import_roots` (not among `config.Keys`). `list` shows what `serve.FindCandidates` finds there now: the folders three levels deep, not into a repository, hidden folders, or build output, each with its key (`import-` and the folder's name made safe, unique among the served projects' keys). The git repositories there that have a manifest are served by `flai serve` instead (S-0120, [ADR-0045](../adrs/0044-a-system-flow-repository-under-a-folder-named-for-import-is-served-whether-or.md)). |
| `flai serve project add [folder]`, `remove <key\|folder>`, `list` (S-0121) | The registry of served projects (`serve/projects.json`) managed on its own, with why a project is not showing. `add` requires `system-flow.yaml` in the folder itself (the current one by default), resolves the dashboard address as `flai dashboard` does (`dashboardSettings`), and registers through `registerProject`, which `flai dashboard`'s `connectServe` and `connectServeFolder` call too: the main checkout as the root, the shared agent credential made when missing, then `serve.Dir.Register`; it starts `flai host` when none runs. `serve.Dir.Register` refuses a key another root already has, naming that root, so every writer of the registry (these, `flai dashboard`, the board's import) refuses it. `remove` finds the entry by key first, then by folder (a folder inside a project or a worktree of it names the project), calls `serve.Dir.Unregister`, as `flai dashboard stop` does, and touches no file of the project and not the container; a project served only because `flai serve` was started above it, or because it is below a folder named for import (S-0120), is found among `projectsBelow` (the running `flai serve`'s `folder_projects`, `import_projects`, and `unserved`, and `serve.FindBelow` over `import_roots` either way), and its root is added to the list of removed projects (`serve/removed.json`, `serve.Dir.RemoveRoot`, S-0123); a registered one below such a folder (`servedBelow`: `importRootOf`, or a running `flai serve`'s `folder`) is unregistered and listed too, so it is not served from there instead. `add` takes the folder off the list (`serve.Dir.RestoreRoot`); when it is below a folder `flai serve` serves from, it is served from there again and not registered (`served_below` in `--json`), otherwise it is registered as usual. `serve.Place` takes the list as a set (`serve.Dir.RemovedSet`, read at every tick by `offers.folderProjects`, so a change is served within a second): a listed root is not served, with `removed: true` and a reason naming `flai serve project add`; a registered root is the registry's whatever the list says. A list that does not read is logged and served as empty. Before `serve.Run` drops the connection of a project that left the registry or is now served under another key, it sends the notification `removed` (`serve.Removed`), so the dashboard's switcher drops it; a project that is only unavailable, or a changed entry with the same key, is not removed. `serve.Entry.Unavailable` says why a registered project cannot be served (the folder is gone, not a folder, no manifest, a manifest that does not load or has no key, an unreadable credential); `serve.Run` skips such an entry, logs `registered project not served` once and `registered project can be served again` once, and writes the reasons to its state as `unavailable` by root. `flai serve status` works the reasons out itself, running or not (`unavailable` in `--json`). `list` (and `--json`, `projectListing`) shows each registered project, each served below the folder `flai serve` was started in, and each served below a folder named for import (`from`: registry, folder, or import), with key, root, dashboard address, and `state`: connected with `since`, not-connected with `last_error`, connecting, unavailable with `reason`, or not-running. Then the import candidates (`serve.FindCandidates` over `import_roots`) and the projects below the folders that `flai serve` does not serve, with why (`belowImportRoots`: its state's `unserved` while it runs, `serve.Place` otherwise). `flai dashboard`, `flai import` (`serveImported`), and `add` all register through `registerProject`, which builds the entry with `serveEntry`. |
| `flai check [dir] [--strict] [--story S-nnnn[,S-nnnn...] [--record-issues]]` | Validate manifest and layout, every item in kanban and archive (front matter, ID and file name, parents and children, state history and sequence, acceptance criteria, sections, and a story's `after:`: `story.after` is an error for an entry that names no story, the story itself, or anything but a story, and for a cycle, reported once on its lowest story, S-0130), the claims of open stories (S-0468, [ADR-0188](../adrs/0188-a-task-takes-no-folder-touch-the-planner-s-folder-touch-on-a-story-needs-the.md): `touches.folder`, an advisory warning `--strict` passes over, on the file of a story in progress whose claim, narrowed by its tasks, keeps a folder touch, an entry naming a folder of the tree that holds files and lies outside the shared paths, naming the folder, its file count, and the ready stories it holds, and saying to name the files with `flai touches` or to say under `### Planning` why files may be added there; `wip.after-hub`, an advisory warning on the file of a story not done or cancelled that three or more stories in backlog, ready, or in progress name in `after:`, naming them, and saying that `after:` names only a change the story needs on main and the pull order sets a preference), a story's draft flag (`story.draft`, a warning on a draft story in ready or later, naming `flai edit <id> --no-draft`; a `draft`, `cost_of_delay`, `forecast`, or `finalized` that `Validate` refuses is `item.front-matter`, S-0199, [ADR-0074](../adrs/0074-work-items-carry-planning-data-a-story-s-draft-flag-an-epic-s-or-story-s-cost.md), and S-0201, a `finalized` block on an epic or task, without `by` or a UTC `at`, or on a story still a draft), narratives and index (`narrative.state`, S-0271: a warning, on the section's heading, on the narrative of a story in progress or in review whose `## Current state` or `## Next steps` is empty or the template's placeholder, by the test the close-out's `narrative` step applies, or whose `## Next steps` is not a list, naming `flai stream state` and the MCP tool `stream_state`), the strategic agents' activity documents, which are not narratives (`wip/agents/planner.md`, `orchestrator.md`, `analyzer.md`, and `curator.md`: `activity.document` is an error on one that does not parse, `activity.front-matter` an error on a negative total, and `activity.totals` a warning on the line of a total that disagrees with the log, each saying to restore the document from git since flai writes it, S-0206, [ADR-0079](../adrs/0079-the-planner-the-orchestrator-and-the-analyzer-each-log-their-activities-in-one.md)), board limits and order, design/docs front matter including ADRs, and the conventions folder (front matter, unique order, marker and project additions, length, README index), and design/issues (schema, unique IDs, file names, summary freshness, and `issues.no-story`, a warning on the `first_reported` line of an open issue older than `issues.story_after` that no open story links, naming `flai issue story <id>`; an invalid `issues.story_after` is one `manifest.issues` error on `system-flow.yaml` and turns the warning off, S-0198), design/experiments (`experiments.document`: a results document's `title`, `updated`, `status`, and `story` front matter, its `<S-nnnn>-<slug>.md` name agreeing with `story`, and its Hypothesis, Success measure, What was done, Results, and Recommendation sections, the last saying adopt, adapt, or drop; `experiments.story`: the story exists, a warning when it is not an experiment; `experiments.duplicate`: one document per story; the folder's `README.md` and `template.md` are left alone, ADR-0066), design/analysis (S-0223, [ADR-0099](../adrs/0099-the-analyzer-runs-behind-the-analyze-host-action-and-writes-one-report-under.md), `checker.analysis` with `analysis.Validate`: `analysis.report` is an error, on its field's line, for a report not named `<date>-<focus>.md` with a real date and one of `bottlenecks`, `intent`, `risk`, and `all`, with no front matter or front matter that does not parse, missing `title`, `updated`, `status`, `focus`, `from`, or `to`, or with an `updated` neither a date nor a timestamp, a `status` none of `draft`, `active`, and `deprecated`, a `focus` none of the four or not the file's, a `from` or `to` not a date, or a window that ends before it starts; `analysis.index` is a warning on a report the folder's `README.md` does not link and on a row that links a report not there; the folder is left out of the design documents' own front matter rules, and a project without it has nothing checked), design/digest (S-0428, [ADR-0191](../adrs/0191-the-digest-is-one-markdown-file-per-entry-under-the-design-folder-s-digest.md), `checker.digest` with `digest.Validate` on every `.md` file but the folder's `README.md`: `digest.entry` is an error, on its field's line, for an entry not named `D-nnnn-<slug>.md`, with no front matter or front matter that does not parse, missing `id`, `title`, `kind`, `date`, or `status`, with an `id` not `D-nnnn` or not the file's, a `kind` none of `decision`, `answer`, `recommendation`, and `cause`, a `status` none of `active`, `absorbed`, and `compacted`, a `date` not `YYYY-MM-DD`, no `sources`, a source that is not a work item, thread, or issue ID or names none in the project or its archive, a topic that is not a word, an `absorbed_by` set without `status: absorbed` or missing with it, not a path from the project root, or naming no file, or an `asked_again` or `cited` entry of the wrong kind of ID; `digest.secret` is an error on each line with text the shape of a private key block, an API key, a GitHub, Slack, or AWS key, a JSON web token, or a long value assigned to a word naming a key, token, secret, or password; the folder is left out of the design documents' own front matter rules, and a project without it has nothing checked), the design token file (S-0381, [ADR-0148](../adrs/0148-a-design-token-s-colour-is-in-the-srgb-colour-space-with-its-hex-or-in-oklch.md), `checker.designTokens` with `designsystem.Load`: `design.tokens` is an error for each problem, on the token's line, as `<token path>: <problem>`, or the problem alone when it is the whole file's, such as JSON that does not parse; a project with no file at the manifest's `design.tokens` has nothing checked, and contrast is never a finding; see [Design system](#design-system)), the generated design stylesheet (S-0385, [ADR-0159](../adrs/0159-the-generated-design-stylesheet-puts-the-dark-values-under-data-theme-dark-and.md), `checker.designStylesheet` and `checker.designStale`: `design.stylesheet` is an error on `system-flow.yaml`, at the key's line, when the manifest's `design.stylesheet` lies outside every sub-project; `design.stale` is a warning on both the stylesheet and the token file when the stylesheet is missing or differs from what `flai design css --write` would write, CRLF and LF counting as the same, naming that command, so that a branch that changed only the token file stops at its close-out ([ADR-0152](../adrs/0152-agents-build-ui-from-a-tailwind-v4-stylesheet-flai-generates-from-the-tokens.md)); neither is reported when the manifest names no stylesheet, and `design.stale` not when there is no token file, the token file has problems, which `design.tokens` reports, or the stylesheet lies outside every sub-project), component examples (S-0443, [ADR-0164](../adrs/0164-a-component-s-examples-are-named-sets-of-props-in-one-json-file-per-component.md), `checker.componentExamples`: `components.examples` is a warning on each `*.examples.json` under the design folder's `components/` that does not fit the format, at the problem's line; on a file in no sub-project's folder, of no supported sub-project, or of no component its catalogue lists, at line 1; on a file whose component's props could not be read, once, at line 1; and on each example whose props do not fit the component's, read with `node` once per sub-project with examples, at the prop's or the example's line, as [component-library.md § The check](component-library.md#the-check) gives; with no such file nothing is checked, and with no supported sub-project, or no `node` on `PATH`, it adds one advisory note on the folder, which `--strict` passes over, the format and the components still checked without `node`), a front-matter field this flai does not know on an item, thread, or issue (`item.unknown-field`, `threads.unknown-field`, `issues.unknown-field`, an error on the key's line: the listing paths read past it, check does not, S-0181), a thread still open on an archived item (`threads.archived`, a warning on the thread's `status` line: `<TH-nnnn> is <status> but <id> is archived; resolve it with flai thread resolve <TH-nnnn>`, since threads have no archive folder; acceptance and `flai archive` resolve such threads since S-0277, [ADR-0109](../adrs/0109-archiving-an-item-resolves-the-threads-still-open-on-it.md), so it finds those a hand edit or an older flai left), the conversations under `wip/messages` (S-0330, [ADR-0120](../adrs/0120-agents-of-two-open-stories-message-each-other-in-conversations-kept-under-wip.md); each file but `README.md` is read on its own, so one that does not parse is reported and the rest still are): errors `messages.front-matter` (a file that does not parse or whose front matter `Validate` refuses, such as an ID not `MS-nnnn`, a `from` or `to` not a story ID, the two the same, a status not `open` or `closed`, or a time not UTC), `messages.duplicate-id`, `messages.filename` (a name that does not start with the ID), `messages.story` (a `from` or `to` naming a story that does not exist), and `messages.unknown-field`, and warnings `messages.closed` (stored `open` while one of its stories is archived; it says to close it as `flai archive` would) and `messages.entries` (no dated entries), the manifest (`manifest.key` warns when `system-flow.yaml` has no `key`, which API responses use to name the project, ADR-0024; `manifest.planning` is an error on the `planning` line for a currency that is not three capital letters, an hour rate below zero, or a cycle that is not a duration longer than zero, S-0199, or a `default_duration` that is not one, S-0210; `manifest.analysis` is an error on the `analysis` line for an `analysis.schedule` that does not parse and an `analysis.agent` that is not valid, S-0223; `manifest.digest` is an error on the `digest` line for a `digest.agent` that is not valid, S-0431), a story no release could plan (`story.component-tag` warns on an open feature, improvement, or remediation story whose touches, with its open tasks', reach two or more components, read as `Holds.Claim` reads them, while no tag of its own or its epic's names one of them, and gives the `flai edit --tag` that adds one, I-0024), and the clone itself: `git.relative-worktrees` warns when `extensions.relativeWorktrees` is set and the git on `PATH` is older than 2.48, read from `.git/config` because that git refuses the repository (ADR-0022), and the markdown under the wip folder: `markdown.MDnnn` warns on each finding of the project's markdownlint configuration in every `.md` file there, archive included, with markdownlint's own message (S-0179, [ADR-0061](../adrs/0061-flai-lints-the-markdown-it-writes-in-wip-with-its-own-implementation-of-the.md)), for the rules ADR-0061 names, MD007, unordered list indentation, added when a story made from the dashboard indented its list (S-0240), and MD038, spaces inside a code span, added when a task body with a space before a code span's closing backtick reached main (S-0262, I-0072), and MD056, table column count, which splits a row on every unescaped pipe, inside a code span too, added when a table row's code span of pipes reached a close-out (S-0289, I-0077); without a configuration (`.markdownlint-cli2.jsonc` or `.yaml`, `.markdownlint.jsonc`, `.json`, `.yaml`, or `.yml` at the root) nothing is linted, and one that cannot be read is `markdown.config`. `markdown.conflict-marker` is an error on each line that opens or closes a merge conflict, `<<<<<<<`, `\|\|\|\|\|\|\|`, or `>>>>>>>` followed by a space or the line's end, but not `=======`, which alone on a line is a setext heading underline, in every `.md` file under the design, docs, and wip folders, archive included, and at the root, without a markdownlint configuration as with one: a conflict git-added unresolved reached main in a design document and nothing caught it, and an error fails CI's check, which runs without `--strict` (S-0253, I-0066). One `path:line: level: rule: message` per finding; an item's front matter is one `item.front-matter` finding per problem `Validate` returns, on its key's line, whatever its message holds (S-0248). Errors exit 1; `--strict` makes warnings exit 1, except `board.wip-limit` for review over its limit, which only acceptance clears (S-0243, [ADR-0073](../adrs/0073-a-full-review-holds-the-pull-and-flai-check-strict-passes-over-review-over-its.md)), and `epic.lags-stories`, a warning on the `status` line of an open epic that its stories put further on than its status, naming the `flai move`s that catch it up, or `flai accept E-nnnn` when its stories are all done, which only the operator clears (S-0200, [ADR-0076](../adrs/0076-an-epic-follows-its-stories-to-ready-and-in-progress-with-the-first-to-review.md), [workflow.md](workflow.md#an-epic-follows-its-stories)), and `narrative.state`, since a narrative is unwritten from `flai stream open` until its agent first writes it and the close-out stops a story that reaches it so (S-0271): `--json` counts all three in `advisory` as well as `warnings`, and the summary line adds `(N that --strict passes over: review over its limit and an epic behind its stories, which only the operator clears, or a narrative not yet written, which flai stream state writes, or component examples left unchecked for want of node, a Svelte sub-project, or a sub-project's svelte)`. Ready and in-progress over their limits still fail `--strict`. `--story S-nnnn` scopes the run to a story (S-0249, [ADR-0085](../adrs/0085-a-close-out-s-flai-check-reports-findings-outside-the-story-as-notes-and.md), `check.ScopeToStory`): a finding is inside it when it is on the story's item file or one of its tasks', its narrative, a thread anchored on the story or one of its tasks, a conversation under `wip/messages` the story is one of the two of (S-0330), a path its branch changes against the main branch or uncommitted in its worktree (`storygit.StoryChanges`); every other finding is outside it. A `wip.overlap` whose `stories` name the story is outside it too, and one between two other stories is left out (S-0279, [ADR-0115](../adrs/0115-a-close-out-records-no-wip-overlap-and-notes-only-an-overlap-naming-its-story.md), `namesStory`). A `touches.folder` on another story, and a `wip.after-hub` whose hub is another story, are left out too, their counts taken back, and `--record-issues` records neither; on the story itself both are kept (S-0468, [ADR-0188](../adrs/0188-a-task-takes-no-folder-touch-the-planner-s-folder-touch-on-a-story-needs-the.md)). An `item.archive` outside the story is left out as well, its counts taken back, since it never names a story in progress and only `flai archive` in the main checkout clears it (S-0280, [ADR-0122](../adrs/0122-a-close-out-records-no-item-archive-and-a-check-scoped-to-a-story-leaves-out.md)). So is a `board.wip-limit` outside the story, advisory included, its counts taken back, since it is on `wip/kanban/board.md`, whose counts are the main checkout's state, which no story branch changes, and names no story; only the operator's acceptances, cancellations, or a raised limit clear it, and without `--story` it is reported as before (S-0348, [ADR-0133](../adrs/0133-a-close-out-records-no-board-wip-limit-and-a-check-scoped-to-a-story-leaves-out.md)). So is an `issues.no-story` outside the story, its counts taken back, since it is on an open issue's file and names that issue, not the story; only the operator's choice of a story for that issue clears it. One on an issue file the story's branch changes, or uncommitted in its worktree, is inside and reported as before, and without `--story` every one is reported (S-0365, [ADR-0136](../adrs/0136-a-close-out-records-no-issues-no-story-and-a-check-scoped-to-a-story-leaves-out.md)). So is a `threads.archived` outside the story, its counts taken back, since it is on the `status` line of a thread anchored on an archived item and names that thread, not the story; only the operator or the thread's opener clears it, with `flai thread resolve` in the main checkout, where threads are written. One on a thread anchored on the story or one of its tasks, or on a thread file the story's branch changes or uncommitted in its worktree, is inside and reported as before, and without `--story` every one is reported (S-0366, [ADR-0137](../adrs/0137-a-close-out-records-no-threads-archived-and-a-check-scoped-to-a-story-leaves.md)). So is the `components.examples` note for examples left unchecked for want of `node`, on the examples folder, or of a sub-project's svelte, on that sub-project's folder under it, its counts taken back, unless a path the story's branch changes, or uncommitted in its worktree, is under the note's folder: only installing the tool clears it, which no story branch does. When one is, the story's own examples went unchecked and the note is inside it, advisory as before, and without `--story` both notes are reported (S-0446, [ADR-0171](../adrs/0171-a-sub-project-whose-svelte-is-not-installed-gets-one-components-examples-note.md)). So is a `markdown.*` finding on the narrative, `wip/agents/<id>.md`, of another story the repository holds that is neither done nor cancelled, its counts taken back, since that story's own close-out counts its narrative as inside and stops on it; a markdown finding on any other file outside the story, a thread, a task, an archived narrative, or a done or cancelled story's narrative, stays outside (S-0318, [ADR-0123](../adrs/0123-a-close-out-records-no-markdown-finding-on-another-open-story-s-narrative-and-a.md)). So is a `narrative.state` finding on such a narrative, its counts taken back, since only that story's agent writes its `## Current state` and `## Next steps` and its own close-out stops on them; any other finding on another story's narrative stays outside (S-0323, [ADR-0125](../adrs/0125-a-close-out-records-no-narrative-state-finding-on-another-open-story-s.md)). A finding outside keeps its level, is printed with `(outside S-nnnn)`, and fails the run neither as an error nor under `--strict`. The summary line adds `; N outside S-nnnn, notes the run passes over`, and `--json` marks each such finding `outside` and counts them in `outside`. `--record-issues`, refused without `--story`, records each rule's findings outside the story, save `wip.overlap`, which it never records (`recordOutside`, ADR-0115), and `item.archive`, `board.wip-limit`, `issues.no-story`, `threads.archived`, the `components.examples` note for examples left unchecked, and a markdown or `narrative.state` finding on another open story's narrative, which the scoped run has left out (ADR-0122, ADR-0133, ADR-0136, ADR-0137, ADR-0171, ADR-0123, ADR-0125), in the open issue titled ``flai check finds `<rule>` outside the story at close-out``, opening it with class `efficiency` and count 1 when none is open and bumping it otherwise, with an instance naming the story and each finding's path and message; an instance for the same story and findings is written once, so a rerun does not inflate the count. It writes in the checkout the run reads, the story's worktree at close-out, regenerates `summary.md`, prints `recorded <rule> outside S-nnnn in I-nnnn (<outcome>)`, the outcome `opened`, `bumped, count N`, or `already recorded, count N`, and adds `recorded` to `--json`; it records whether or not the run passes, and a failure to record is an error. The close-out passes both (`CLOSE_OUT_STORY`, read by `scripts/check.sh`); without `--story`, as in CI, every finding counts. `--story` takes several stories, comma-separated or repeated, as the merge queue's batch names the stories it verifies (S-0464, [ADR-0183](../adrs/0183-the-merge-queue-s-batch-and-each-bisection-step-run-their-tiers-with-merge.md), `check.ScopeToStories`): a finding is inside when it is inside any of them and outside only when it is outside every one, printed with `(outside S-0001, S-0002)`; a `wip.overlap` is outside when it names any of them, and a markdown or `narrative.state` finding is left out on the narrative of an open story that is none of them. `--record-issues` with more than one story is refused, since a batch records no issue; `scripts/check.sh` passes the batch's `MERGE_QUEUE_STORIES` to `--story` alone. |
| `flai test [path\|package]... [--all] [--max n] [--json]` (S-0273) | Runs the project's test and lint tiers, the manifest's `tests` ([project-manifest.md](project-manifest.md); `Manifest.TestTiers`, read from the checkout by `verify.CheckoutTiers`), in the checkout of the working directory, a story's worktree or the main checkout (`git rev-parse --show-toplevel`, else the folder of `system-flow.yaml`). Arguments are files or folders, relative to the working directory or absolute, inside the checkout; a Go package is given as its folder. With none, the paths are what the checkout changed against the main branch, its commits and what it has not committed. `verify.Test` selects each tier whose `paths` match a path, runs the selected tiers in the manifest's order, cheapest first, with `{packages}`, `{importers}`, or `{files}` replaced by what each selected, `{importers}` being the changed packages and every package of the module that imports one (S-0364), and stops at the first that fails, the rest `not-reached`; an `all_only` tier runs only under `--all`, which runs every tier for the whole checkout, with `all_command` where a tier has one. A selected tier that another selected tier covers, through the manifest's `covers` (S-0342), is not run: it is listed in its place as `skipped`, covered by the nearest selected tier that covers it and is not covered itself, never fails the run, and stays `skipped` after a failure. Each tier's `format` turns its output into findings (`go-test-json`, `vitest-json`, `golangci-json`, `gofmt-list`, or `plain`, which reads only the exit status), each a name, a path, a line, and a message; the answer holds at most `--max` of them across the run (5 by default) and counts the rest in the tier's `omitted`, never the whole output. Text prints a line per tier, its state, name, and duration, a skipped tier as `skipped <tier>: covered by <tier>` with no duration, then each finding as `path:line name: message`; `--json` prints `verify.Result`, `{passed, paths, tiers}`, each tier with `name`, `command`, `dir`, `state` (`passed`, `failed`, `not-reached`, or `skipped`), `exit_code`, `duration_ms`, `duration`, `findings`, `omitted`, and a skipped tier's `covered_by`. Exit 0 on pass, 1 when a tier failed, 2 when it could not answer: an argument outside the checkout, a `--max` under 1, tiers the manifest does not declare validly (`flai check` reports them as `manifest.tests`), no main branch to count changes against, or a run stopped by a signal, which ends each tier's whole process tree. A manifest without `tests` runs `scripts/test.sh` as its one `plain` tier when the file exists. Every tier runs with `FLAI_ROLE=verify`, added after the inherited environment so it wins whatever role ran `flai test`, so a test that runs flai's writes in process is not refused by the orchestrator's checks (S-0311, `verify.runTier`). The MCP tool `test` (`internal/mcpserver/test.go`) answers the same result for `{story, paths, all, max}`, in the story's worktree or the main checkout, and the host method `test.run`, behind the `checks` host action, runs `flai test --json` there ([dashboard-host-channel.md](dashboard-host-channel.md)). `flai guard` lets a sub-agent run both. The conventions and the story agent's prompt send an agent to it for the tests of what each task changed, the whole suite and lint staying the close-out's, which runs `flai verify` (S-0270). |
| `flai verify <story> [--json] [--record-issues] [--max n] [--last] [--sync-only] [--fresh] [--all]` (S-0270, S-0341, [ADR-0110](../adrs/0110-a-story-s-agent-runs-the-close-out-which-runs-flai-verify-itself-before-review.md); S-0405, [ADR-0174](../adrs/0174-while-the-merge-queue-can-batch-flai-verify-and-the-close-out-leave-the-test.md)) | Verifies a story before review, in its worktree under `.flai-cache/worktrees`, from any checkout of the project; an ID that is not a story, or a story with no worktree, is refused, naming `flai stream open`. `verify.Verify` (`internal/verify/story.go`) runs the steps in order, cheapest first, and stops at the first that fails, the rest `not-reached`: `rebase` (no rebase left unfinished in the worktree), `sync` (the branch contains the main branch, the branch the main checkout has, but for commits that change only paths under the manifest's `wip` folder none of which the branch changes: the paths `git diff --no-renames --name-only HEAD...<main>` lists, a rename as both of its paths, against the branch's changed paths; the step then passes with a `note` naming how many commits it passed over and their short hashes, S-0347, [ADR-0135](../adrs/0135-the-sync-step-of-flai-verify-and-the-close-out-s-last-check-pass-over-commits.md)), `narrative` (the main checkout's `wip/agents/<story>.md` has `## Current state` and `## Next steps` written, `verify.Unwritten`), `check` (`flai check --strict` scoped to the story, run in process as `--story` scopes it: a finding in the story fails the step, and a finding outside it is a note, [ADR-0085](../adrs/0085-a-close-out-s-flai-check-reports-findings-outside-the-story-as-notes-and.md)), then one step per tier the branch's changes against the main branch select (`verify.SelectStory`): those `flai test` selects for the paths, and each `all_only` tier whose `paths` select one of them or that has no `paths`, with its `all_command` where it has one. A selected tier that another selected tier covers is not run, as in `flai test`: its step is `skipped`, covered by the tier that runs in its place, never fails the run, and stays `skipped` after a step before it failed. While the merge queue can batch for the project, each selected tier the manifest marks `queue: true` is left to the queue's batch, which runs it ([ADR-0174](../adrs/0174-while-the-merge-queue-can-batch-flai-verify-and-the-close-out-leave-the-test.md)). The queue can batch when the `merge-queue` host action is on for the project in the configuration this flai reads, read without creating it, and the main checkout's manifest has `orchestration.permissions.accept_reviews` on and local acceptance (`mergequeue.Batches`, `queueBatches` in `cmd/verify.go`); a hold does not count, and a configuration or manifest that cannot be read is warned of and leaves no tier out. `verify.SelectStoryLeavingQueue` then answers the `queue` tiers apart, and the rest are covered as before but by none of those: a tier that only a `queue` tier covers runs. Each tier left out is a step after the rest, in the state `queued`, whatever the steps before it did, and the run passes without it. `--all` leaves no tier to the queue, and neither does `--sync-only`, which runs no tier. When the queue cannot batch, `queueOff` in `cmd/verify.go` answers why, the condition that is off and what turns it on: the host action off for the project in the configuration it names, a configuration or project that cannot be read, `accept_reviews` off, or pull-request acceptance. A run that is neither `--all` nor `--sync-only` and selects a tier marked `queue` then stores the reason, with those tiers' names, as the report's `queue_off`, `tiers <a> and <b> ran here, not left to the merge queue: <why>`, and the text prints it as a line before the notes, so a close-out that runs the `queue` tiers for a configuration without the host action says so (S-0466, I-0148). The tiers are the worktree's manifest's (`verify.CheckoutTiers`) and run with `CLOSE_OUT_STORY` set to the story, which `scripts/check.sh` and the tests that check the repository read to scope themselves to it: `TestMonorepoIsClean` notes a check finding outside the story, and `TestRepositoryLintsClean` (`internal/mdlint/repo_test.go`) and `scripts/lint-md.sh` leave out the markdown under `wip/` that the story does not change, which the branch holds as the main branch last committed it, so a line another agent commits to main's `wip/` fails no story's integration or smoke tier (S-0345, I-0117); without the variable, as in CI and `make lint-md`, they count every file. They run with `FLAI_ROLE=verify` too, whatever role ran `flai verify`, so the orchestrator's `flai verify` on a story in review does not have the story's tests refused as the orchestrator (S-0311). The report (`verify.Report`) has `story`, `commit` (the worktree's HEAD), `base`, `base_commit` (the commit `base` had, left out when git cannot read it), `merge_base` (`git merge-base HEAD <base>`) and `patch` (`git patch-id --stable` of the diff from `merge_base` to HEAD, leaving out the files a sync's rebase writes again, `design/issues/summary.md` (the paths `issues.Generated` names, [ADR-0192](../adrs/0192-flai-verify-s-patch-id-leaves-out-the-files-a-sync-s-rebase-writes-again-so-a.md)), taken by `verify.BranchPatch` only when the worktree has nothing uncommitted, [ADR-0156](../adrs/0156-a-close-out-that-main-outran-syncs-and-verifies-again-at-most-three-rounds-and.md)), `fingerprint` (`verify.fingerprint`: a SHA-256 of the manifest's tiers as declared, each selected tier's name, `dir`, and argv, and the names of the tiers left to the merge queue, so a run that leaves tiers to the queue reuses nothing from one that ran them, nor the other way round), `ran_at`, `duration_ms` and `duration`, `passed`, `stopped_at`, `paths` (what the branch changed), `steps`, `notes`, and `queue_off`, left out when empty. A step has `name`, `tier` for a tier, `state` (`passed`, `failed`, `not-reached`, `reused`, `skipped`, or `queued`), a skipped tier's `covered_by`, `duration_ms`, `duration`, `findings` (each a `name`, `path`, `line`, and `message`), `omitted`, a passing step's `note`, printed indented under it and never recorded in an issue, a tier's `command`, `dir`, and `exit_code`, and a reused tier's `reused_from`; a note has `rule`, `level`, `path`, `line`, and `message`. `--max` caps the findings across the run, 5 by default. Every run's report is stored, passed or not, at `.flai-cache/verify/<story>.json` in the project's cache (`verify.SaveReport`); `--last` prints the stored one and runs nothing, `null` with `--json` when there is none, exits 0 whatever it holds, and is refused with `--record-issues`. `--sync-only` runs the `rebase` and `sync` steps alone, prints and exits as a full run does, and stores no report, so the stored one stays the last full run's; it is refused with `--last` and with `--record-issues`. A run resumes from the stored report when its `commit` is the head, its `base_commit` the main branch's commit now, and its `fingerprint` this run's, and `--fresh` is not given (`verify.reusable`): the four steps before the tiers always run, and the tiers before the first the report shows `failed` or `not-reached` are `reused`, keeping that run's `command`, `exit_code`, and duration, with `reused_from` the `ran_at` of the run that ran them, or the time a tier reused again already had; a tier another selected tier covers stays `skipped` there and does not end what is reused (S-0342). Across a clean rebase onto a newer main branch, where `commit` and `base_commit` differ, the run reuses the same way when its `patch` is the report's and not empty, its `fingerprint` and tiers are the report's, and both merge bases are known. It reuses only up to the first tier that the paths the main branch brought in select: those `verify.BetweenPaths` lists from the report's `merge_base` to this run's, but for paths under the `wip` folder that the branch does not change, selected with `verify.SelectStory` (`{importers}` included), together with the tier that covers one of them. That tier runs again with every tier after it, and the `note` of the first tier run again names the tiers main's paths select. The first reused tier's `note` says the tiers were reused across the rebase, or, without a rebase, that main moved on (S-0374, [ADR-0156](../adrs/0156-a-close-out-that-main-outran-syncs-and-verifies-again-at-most-three-rounds-and.md)). A report that stopped before the tiers reuses none, one that passed them all reuses all, and one with no `fingerprint` or `base_commit`, from an older flai, gives a full run. The report a resumed run stores holds every step. `--fresh` has no effect with `--last`; the MCP tool and `verify.run` take no `fresh`. `flai test` never resumes. The text is a line per step, `<state> <name> (<duration>)`, or `reused tier <name> (from <RFC3339 UTC>)`, with `tier` before a tier's name, a skipped tier as `skipped tier <tier>: covered by <tier>` and a queued one as `queued tier <tier>: left to the merge queue`, both with no duration, the failing step's findings indented under it, then the notes under `outside S-nnnn, notes that do not fail it:`, and last `verify: S-nnnn passed every step` or `verify: S-nnnn stopped at <step> (<exit N, failed, or interrupted>)`, either followed by `, <a>, <b> and <c> left to the merge queue` when it left tiers to the queue, and then by `(reused N tiers from <time>)`, the earliest `reused_from`, when the run reused tiers (`1 tier` for one). `--record-issues` records the notes in `design/issues` of the story's worktree with `flai check --record-issues`'s recorder (`recordOutside`), prints `recorded <rule> outside S-nnnn in I-nnnn (<outcome>)`, and adds `recorded` to `--json`. It commits nothing. Exit 0 when every step passed, 1 when a step failed, 2 when it could not answer: a usage error, no worktree, tiers the manifest does not declare validly, notes it could not record, or a run stopped by a signal. The MCP tool `verify` answers the same report, and its `all` is `--all`; `flai mcp` and `flai mcp start` give it `queueBatches`. The host method `verify.run` runs it behind the `checks` host action, and `verify.status` reads the stored one ([dashboard-host-channel.md](dashboard-host-channel.md)); the review page shows it ([flaiover-dashboard.md](flaiover-dashboard.md)). `scripts/close-out.sh`, here and in the template, runs `flai verify "$story" --record-issues` for its checks, after checking that the worktree is on the story's branch, and then commits, checks again with `flai verify "$story" --sync-only` that the branch contains the main branch under the `sync` step's rule, and checks that the worktree is clean, so the close-out and `flai verify` never disagree. When that last check stops at the `sync` step, the close-out runs `flai stream sync "$story"` and, when the sync ends clean, the verify, the commit, and the check again, at most three rounds, printing `close-out: main moved during the run; flai stream sync S-nnnn (round N of 3)` before each, and naming the round in its last line when there was more than one. A sync that fails stops it at `flai stream sync`, and a check that still fails after the third round stops it at the sync check ([ADR-0156](../adrs/0156-a-close-out-that-main-outran-syncs-and-verifies-again-at-most-three-rounds-and.md)). It passes no `--fresh` and no `--all`, so it leaves the `queue` tiers to the batch while the queue can batch, and a second close-out at an unchanged head resumes, a round after a clean sync reuses what main's new paths do not reach, and the last line carries verify's `(reused N tiers from <time>)`. A commit it makes changes the branch's own change, so the run after is full. `flai guard` lets a sub-agent and the orchestrator run it without `--record-issues`. |
| `flai epic new`, `flai story new --epic E-0001`, `flai task new --story S-0004` | Create an item from the body template, allocate the next ID, link to parent. `--body-stdin` reads the body below the heading from standard input (S-0059), and creation is then one step (`flai/internal/itemnew`): `flai check` before and after, anything the item introduces refuses it with exit 4 and the findings as `{ refused }`, the file removed, the parent restored, and the ID not spent, but a `wip.overlap`, which is advisory (ADR-0019) and comes back in `warnings`, as `flai edit` keeps it (S-0451). A title loses the trailing punctuation markdownlint rejects in a heading (`workitem.CleanTitle`, MD026's `.,;:!` and their full-width forms; a question mark stays), here, in `flai edit --title`, and in `flai thread new`. `Repo.Create` lints the document it is about to write and refuses one the project's markdown lint rejects, writing nothing, with an `*mdlint.Error` naming each rule and line (`Repo.LintGuard`, S-0179); `itemnew` turns it into the `{ refused }` above, its findings `markdown.MDnnn`, so `item_new`, `flai story new`, and the dashboard's form are refused alike, and `flai edit` and `item_edit` are refused through the check they run. `--autocommit` commits the item and its parent, path limited, as `chore: [ID] create <type>: <title>` with `--trailer` lines, unless `dashboard.autocommit` is false. `--print-body` prints the body the project's template gives the type and creates nothing. A story gets the project's default agent, with `--harness`, `--model`, `--provider` (S-0349, [ADR-0129](../adrs/0129-a-story-s-agent-names-a-provider-beside-its-harness-and-model-and-the-providers.md)), and `--agent-config key=value` (repeatable) laid over it (S-0103); another type given them is refused. `--topics` (stories and epics, S-0135) sets what the item is about: `workitem.CleanTopics` trims, drops repeats, and refuses what is not one word (`workitem.ValidTopic`, which `topics.Valid` shares); a task has no such flag, and `Item.Validate` refuses a task that carries topics. `flai story new --draft` makes a draft (S-0199), which `flai move` refuses to ready until it is finalized; another type has no such flag. Since S-0244 `flai task new` reports a claim the new task grew into another story in progress's, as `flai touches` does. |
| `flai move <id> <state>` | Transition an item with rule validation. Besides forward, an item moves back one column from ready, in-progress, review, or cancelled, and never out of done ([ADR-0055](../adrs/0055-a-story-moves-back-one-column-from-ready-in-progress-review-or-cancelled-and.md), S-0167). `--reason` required for `cancelled` and for review to in-progress; `--by` defaults to `FLAI_AGENT` when it is set, else the config author, as for `flai block`, `unblock`, and threads, so an agent's moves are recorded as the agent's (S-0058). A story's epic follows its move in the same write (S-0200, [ADR-0076](../adrs/0076-an-epic-follows-its-stories-to-ready-and-in-progress-with-the-first-to-review.md); the rule is in [workflow.md](workflow.md#an-epic-follows-its-stories)): `workitem.Repo.Follow`, called from `TransitionAll`, walks the epic with the story's actor and time and validates every step before anything is written; the command prints `E-nnnn → <state>, following S-nnnn`, indented, under the item's line and `--json` returns the walk as `followed` (`workitem.Followed`: `id`, `type`, `title`, `from`, `to`, `story`), and a cancellation's list, `--json`, and dry run say it too. Warns on WIP limit breaches, and on a task moved to in-progress while a task its `after` names is open (S-0176, `Plan.Waits`), and moves it. A move to `cancelled` cancels everything open under the item too (ADR-0028): the items are listed first, a terminal is asked unless `--yes`, `--dry-run` lists and changes nothing, `--json` answers `{ id, status, cancelled[], left_behind[], warnings[] }`, and the narratives, story branches, and worktrees of cancelled stories are named and left as they are (S-0070). Moving a story from review to done is acceptance and runs the `flai accept` flow with its flags (S-0046), `--verified` and `--evidence` among them since S-0221. Its refusal is `flai accept`'s too (I-0145): `move` returns `acceptItem`'s error through `acceptRefusal`, so under `--json` a refusal for lint the fix left or for a path the branch undoes of main's change exits 4 and prints the same `{"refused": {...}}`, with `findings` or `undoes_main`, and without `--json` it is the text and exit 1 it was. The host write `item.move` maps exit 4 to `Refused` with that object as data, as `accept.run` does. A story moves to review only with nothing uncommitted in its worktree (S-0140): the rule is in `workitem.Repo.Move`, which asks `git status --porcelain` there through `Repo.Git` (the command's `execx.Runner`, set by `a.project()`; the MCP server sets its own), refuses with the paths and the branch, and warns and moves when git cannot read the worktree. A story with an unticked acceptance criterion moves to review with a warning that counts them, and is refused only at done (S-0282, [ADR-0089](../adrs/0089-acceptance-criteria-are-ticked-through-flai-by-number-by-the-story-s-agent-once.md)). A draft story is refused to ready with "finalize it first" unless the move finalizes it (S-0199, `workitem.MoveOptions.Finalize`): `--yes` on a move to ready is the operator's say, and the move clears `draft` in the same save as the transition, records the move's `by` and time in the story's `finalized` block (S-0201, [ADR-0077](../adrs/0077-a-story-that-was-a-draft-records-who-finalized-it-and-when-and-the-dashboard.md)), prints that the story is finalized, and answers `finalized: true` in `--json`. Under `FLAI_ROLE=orchestrate` a move to ready, here and through the MCP tool `item_move`, is refused, as `rule:`, unless `orchestration.permissions.promote_to_ready` is on, the item is a story `flai promote --candidates` lists, and the ready column is under its WIP limit (`Repo.Promotable`, S-0219); a story that is not a candidate is refused with each reason the candidates give it. |
| `flai show <id>` | One item with history, blocks, and children. For a story, its topics and where each came from (S-0135, [ADR-0047](../adrs/0047-an-agent-is-primed-with-what-its-story-s-topics-claim-and-links-select.md)): `topics.ForStory` reads the story, its epic, and the active items, and `topics.OfStory` gives its own and its epic's `topics`, then per sub-project in manifest order the name, tags, and kind of each one a tag names or a claim entry reaches (the story's `touches` and its open tasks', an entry reaching a sub-project when it is the name or a tag, or when `workitem.PathsOverlap` says it and the path overlap), `code` for each of those that is not the template, and `all`, each topic once with every `Source` (`kind` own, epic, tag, claim, code, all; `item`; `project`; `via`). The text prints one line per topic with `StoryTopic.Summary`, each sub-project once; `--json` adds `topics` beside `item` and `children` with every source. An epic prints its own `topics`. A story with tasks gets its task plan (S-0176), `workitem.PlanOf` (`internal/workitem/taskplan.go`) over its children: per task, in ID order, a state (`ready` when not started and every task its `after` names is done or cancelled, `waiting` with `waiting_for` while one is open, `in-progress` for in progress or review, `done`, `cancelled`), and `layers`, the tasks that are not cancelled grouped by the longest chain of `after` steps before each, IDs in ID order, layer 0 waiting for none. An `after` entry that names no task of the story is skipped, a cancelled task holds no one and is in no layer, and a task on a cycle or after one is in none. The text prints each task's state with `for` and what it waits for, or `after` and its `after`, then the layers numbered from 1. `--json`, the MCP `item_get`, and the host's `item.get` add `plan: {tasks: [{id, state, after?, waiting_for?}], layers: [[id]]}` beside `item` and `children` (MCP: beside its fields), only for a story with tasks. It lives in `workitem` because `NewBoardView` builds the cards there. An item's planning data (S-0199), a story's `draft` and `forecast` and a story's or epic's `cost_of_delay`, is printed after its agent, one line each, with `finalized by <by> at <at>` for a story that was a draft (S-0201), amounts in `planning.currency` per week, with who set each block and when; `flai edit --show` prints the same lines. A story whose pull request flai serve has read gets a pull request section (S-0394, [ADR-0167](../adrs/0167-flai-serve-reads-each-pull-request-s-required-checks-and-reviews-into-kept.md)), from the kept state and never the forge ([Reading the pull request back](#reading-the-pull-request-back)): `pull request: #<n> <url> at <head>, read <time>`, then one line per required check, `check <name>: <state> <log> (<summary>)`, and per review, `review <author>: <state> <link>`, each link and summary left out when the forge gave none. `--json` adds `pull_request`, the kept `forge.Status` without its `events`. A kept file that cannot be read is logged as a warning and left out, and the item still shows. |
| `flai block <id> --reason`, `flai unblock <id>` | Open and close blocked intervals. |
| `flai board [--all]` | Stories per column with nature, age in column, blocked flag, WIP counts, and the pull order, with `backlog` and `ready` laid out in it (S-0057); `--all` adds epics and tasks. A held ready story (S-0128, [ADR-0046](../adrs/0046-a-ready-story-whose-claim-overlaps-an-open-story-s-is-held-yellow-and-with-its.md)) is flagged `HELD` with the reason on the line under it; `--json` carries `held: {code, reason}` on its card. On a hold on overlap alone, `held` adds `asked` (S-0338): for each holding story whose agent flai serve asked about the hold since the story last entered ready (see `share` under `flai message`), the newest such message as `{by, conversation, at}`, the holding story, the conversation's ID, and when, in ID order; a holder not asked is left out. `Repo.Conversations` reads the asks, entries by `flai` written for one of an open conversation's two stories, with the shares in force, in one pass (`internal/workitem/share.go`), and `Repo.Holds` gives both to `Holds` (`WithShares`, `WithAsks`). A story card in ready, in progress, or in review carries `shared`: each share in force on which it is the held story (`Holds.SharesWith`), `{holder, held, paths, split, by, at, conversation}`, `conversation` filled from the conversation's `id` and never written to its front matter. flai serve's activity for a held story carries the same `hold`. The text prints neither. `workitem.NewBoardView` takes the manifest's sub-projects and marks each ready story with `workitem.Holds.Of` (`internal/workitem/hold.go`): the claim is the story's `touches` and those of its open tasks, a sub-project's name or tag read as its path (`Holds.Claim`); stories in progress or in review are open; overlap is `workitem.PathsOverlap`, which `flai check` shares; an empty claim on either side is `no-touches`. A ready story that names in `after:` a story not done is `after` (S-0130): `Holds.Of` names each with its state (`no such story`, `cancelled`, `in backlog`, …) and joins an overlap after `; also`. `Repo.Holds(items)` is `NewHolds` with a lookup of a named story missing from `items` through `Repo.Get`, so `flai serve` and `Repo.Move`, which read only the active items, find an archived story done or cancelled. A story with tasks carries `tasks: {ready, waiting, in_progress, done, layers}` on its card, `Plan.Summary` of its task plan (S-0176, see `flai show`), cancelled tasks uncounted and `layers` the number of layers, and the text prints it under an open story's card; `board.get` and the MCP `board` give the same view. Since S-0195 ([ADR-0067](../adrs/0067-accepted-work-reaches-the-remote-only-when-it-is-published-and-agents-publish.md)) the view carries `unpublished`, the IDs of accepted items no release has covered yet (`release.PendingIDs`, sorted), as information, and the text prints them as `accepted, not yet published: …` with "publishing is the operator's: git fetch, then flai release --pending"; it no longer carries `unpushed` or tells anyone to push. Since S-0398 the view carries `queue`, the merge queue as `flai queue --json` gives it ([ADR-0173](../adrs/0173-a-merge-queue-in-flai-serve-with-no-model-runs-behind-the-merge-queue-host.md)): `mergequeue.OnBoard` puts it beside the board as `mergequeue.Board`, the one builder for `flai board --json`, the MCP `board`, and `board.get`, timed as the phase `merge.queue`. A queue that cannot be read is logged as a warning and left off, and the board is still served. The text adds one line under the review column, `queue: S-0001, S-0003; batch: none`, or `queue off under acceptance.mode pull-request`; the reasons a story is not eligible are `flai queue`'s to give, save `dirty_main`: since S-0402 (`heldBackLines` in `cmd/board.go`, [ADR-0178](../adrs/0178-the-merge-queue-lands-each-story-in-the-main-checkout-with-git-s-own-check.md)) one line under the queue's names each story it holds back, `held back: S-nnnn by the main checkout's uncommitted <files>; free it: <command>`. |
| `flai board limit <column> <n>` | Sets the WIP limit of `ready`, `in-progress`, or `review` in `wip_limits` of `board.md`, `0` for none (S-0167); any other column, a negative number, or not a number is refused. `--json` answers `{ column, limit, wip_limits }`. The host channel's `board.limit` (`{column, limit}`, 0 to 99) runs it for the board's lane menu |
| `flai queue [--json]` (S-0398, [ADR-0173](../adrs/0173-a-merge-queue-in-flai-serve-with-no-model-runs-behind-the-merge-queue-host.md)) | The merge queue as it stands, read-only: it enqueues, verifies, merges, and moves nothing; the writes are `flai queue add`, `remove`, `hold`, and `resume` (below). `mergequeue.Compute` (`internal/mergequeue/eligible.go`) gives each story in review every reason it is not eligible, each a stable `kind` with a sentence saying what fails and what to do: `verify_missing` (no `flai verify` result), `verify_failed` (its last failed, naming the step it stopped at), `verify_stale` (its last passed at a commit other than its branch's head, read with `git rev-parse` in the main checkout), `branch_unread` (that head cannot be read), `criteria_unticked` (with `criteria`, their numbers), `thread_open` (with `threads`, those not resolved on the story or one of its tasks), `undone_main` (with `paths`, those its record at `.flai-cache/undone/` says its branch undoes of main's change, [ADR-0155](../adrs/0155-a-rebased-story-branch-that-undoes-main-s-change-to-a-path-is-recorded-at-sync.md); a story with no record runs no git), `dirty_main` (since S-0402, [ADR-0178](../adrs/0178-the-merge-queue-lands-each-story-in-the-main-checkout-with-git-s-own-check.md): its change, `main...story/S-nnnn`, would overwrite an uncommitted or untracked file in the main checkout, with `paths`, those files, and `command`, `mergequeue.FreeCommand`, `git stash push -- <files>` with each path quoted for the shell; the checkout's status is read once per computation and, only when it has such files, each story's change, so the reason clears once they are clean), and `unreadable` (its verify result or that record cannot be read). A story with none is eligible. Both lists are ordered by when each story last entered review, from its last transition to review, oldest first, then by ID. `--json` is `mergequeue.Queue`: `mode`, `eligible` and `ineligible` as `{story, title, entered_review, head, enqueued, reasons}`, `enqueued`, `held` with `held_by` and `held_at` when the queue is held (S-0399), `batch` as `mergequeue.Batch` (left out when there is none), with `heads`, `bisect`, and `landing` since S-0400 ([The merge queue pass](#the-merge-queue-pass)), and `outcomes`. The text lists `eligible (N)` numbered in queue order and `not eligible (N)` with each reason under its story as `kind: text`, each story with `in review since <time>` and `enqueued` when it is; then `batch: none` or `batch: S-0001, step verifying go-test`, with the batch's result under it once it has one, `result: passed` or `result: failed at <tier>`, when it finished, and its summary, and `dropped by <who> at <time>: <reason>` when it was dropped (S-0399), a line `held back: S-nnnn, by the main checkout's uncommitted <files>; free it: git stash push -- <files>` for each story in the landing's `held_back` (S-0402, `printBatch`), and `outcomes` when there are any. A held queue is first, `held by <who> at <time>: no batch is built until flai queue resume`. With no story in review it says `no story is in review`. The state is `.flai-cache/merge-queue/state.json` in the main checkout (`StatePath` and `ReadState` in `state.go`, the only reader): the stories enqueued with `by` and `at`, the batch and its step, and each story's last outcome. A missing file is an empty queue, with no batch and no outcomes; the writers are in the next row. Under `acceptance.mode: pull-request` the queue is `off` with `off_reason` (`mergequeue.OffUnderPullRequest`), lists no story, and reads no state; the text is the one line `merge queue off under acceptance.mode pull-request: <reason>`. Items, threads, or a state file that cannot be read are an error. |
| `flai queue add <story> [--by] [--verified <commit> --evidence <file>] [--json]`, `flai queue remove <story> [--json]`, `flai queue hold [--json]`, `flai queue resume [--json]` (S-0399, [ADR-0173](../adrs/0173-a-merge-queue-in-flai-serve-with-no-model-runs-behind-the-merge-queue-host.md)) | The queue's writers, in `internal/mergequeue/state.go`. `mergequeue.Update` is the only writer of `state.json`: it takes the lock `state.json.lock` beside it, waiting up to 10 seconds for another writer, `flai serve`'s batch or a shell's command, reads the state, applies one change, and writes it whole with `atomicfile.WriteFile`, or not at all when nothing changed. `add` (`mergequeue.Add`) enqueues a story at the end of the queue, recorded with `by`, `FLAI_AGENT` or else the configuration's `author`, and `at`. It refuses, with a `*mergequeue.Refusal` (`{story, text, reasons}`) and nothing written, any story under `acceptance.mode: pull-request`, saying the queue runs under local acceptance only; an item that is not a story; a story not in review; and a story not eligible, with every reason `Compute` gives it, printed as `kind: text`. A story enqueued already keeps its place and who enqueued it, and the change says nothing changed. `add --by orchestrator` is the orchestrator's enqueue in place of its acceptance (S-0403, [ADR-0093](../adrs/0093-with-accept-reviews-on-the-orchestrator-accepts-a-story-in-review-through-flai.md)), with `--verified <commit>`, the commit its verifier checked, and `--evidence <file>`, `-` for standard input, as `flai accept --by orchestrator` takes them. `mcpserver.EnqueueByOrchestrator`, which the MCP tool `queue_add` shares, refuses as `rule:`, writing nothing: `accept_reviews` off (`Repo.OrchestratorPermits`); a missing commit or evidence; evidence with a heading line or that `preview.ParseEvidence` cannot read. It then enqueues through `mergequeue.AddWith`, whose `Vouch` runs `preview.AcceptWith` as the orchestrator with that commit and evidence and refuses, as a `*mergequeue.Refusal`, on each of its `orchestrator_blockers`, the code as the reason's `kind`. The enqueue records `verified` and `evidence` beside `by` and `at` in the state's `Enqueued`. `--verified` and `--evidence` are refused without `--by orchestrator`, and under `FLAI_ROLE=orchestrate` any other `--by`, or none, is. `remove` (`mergequeue.Remove`) takes the story out and marks the batch that holds it `dropped`, with the reason, who, and when, so that the queue lands no story no one has enqueued; a story not enqueued is refused. `hold` (`mergequeue.Hold`) sets `held`, `held_by`, and `held_at`: no batch starts until `resume` clears them, and a batch under verification finishes and waits. Both leave a queue already so as it is, `hold` keeping who held it first. Each prints one line saying what changed, or that nothing did and why, and logs `merge queue changed` when something did; `--json` is `mergequeue.Change`: `{action, story, changed, by, at, state}`, `story` left out for `hold` and `resume`. `flai accept` of a story (`leaveQueue` in `cmd/accept.go`, `mergequeue.AcceptedByHand`) takes it out of the queue before the move to done, drops the batch that holds it, and records the outcome `accepted_by_hand`; `flai accept --batch` records `landed` instead, with `mergequeue.Landed`, and keeps the batch (S-0400), and for a story the orchestrator enqueued with evidence and given no `--evidence`, takes that evidence and writes `- Enqueued verified: <commit>` in its notes (S-0403); the story is merged by then, so a state it cannot write is a warning and the acceptance goes on. The dashboard's writes are `queue.add`, `queue.remove`, and `queue.hold` ([flaiover-dashboard.md](flaiover-dashboard.md#writes-s-0075-adr-0029)) |
| `flai order <story> --before <id> \| --after <id> \| --top \| --bottom [--placed-by <name>]` | Places a ready or backlog story in its column's pull order (S-0057) and rewrites `order` in `board.md`: ready stories, then backlog stories, stale names dropped, the unplaced tail of the backlog left unnamed. Refuses epics, tasks, other states, and a reference in another column, with the reason. Since S-0219 ([ADR-0088](../adrs/0088-board-md-records-who-placed-a-story-by-hand-and-when-and-a-policy-s-order-keeps.md)) it records the placement in `board.md`'s `placed` map, after `order` (`Board.RecordPlacement`, `workitem.Placed`): by story, `by` and `at` in UTC, replacing the story's earlier entry, written only when the map has an entry and sorted by ID. `by` is `--placed-by`, else `FLAI_AGENT`, else the config author (`app.placedBy`), and `orchestrator` whatever they say under `FLAI_ROLE=orchestrate`; the dashboard's drag runs this command with `FLAI_AGENT=flaiover`. `flai move` drops a story's entry whenever it changes the story's column (`Board.RemoveFromOrder`). `--json` prints `{ id, status, sequence, order, placed }`. Rules in [workflow.md](workflow.md#the-pull-order-s-0057). |
| `flai order --by <cod\|wsjf\|throughput\|fifo> [--apply] [--keep-placed <duration>] [--json]` (S-0217) | Computes the ready column's order by a policy and prints each story with its position and the figure it was ordered by. This, `flai promote --candidates`, and `flai release --evaluate` are the orchestrator's arithmetic, kept in flai so that the operator and the dashboard get the same answer as the orchestrator, with no agent; the orchestrator itself makes only the judgement calls (the designer's choice of 2026-10-02). `workitem.OrderByPolicy` takes the ready stories in their pull order: `cod` orders by the cost of delay `value` per week, highest first; `wsjf` by that value over the forecast duration in hours, highest first; `throughput` by the forecast duration, shortest first; `fifo` by `created`, oldest first. A forecast duration is read as a Go duration, and one that does not parse or is not over zero counts as none. A story without the figure its policy needs goes after the stories with it, in its current order, and `missing` names what it lacks (`cost of delay value`, `forecast duration`, both for `wsjf`, or `created`); ties keep the current order. `--apply` writes the computed order to `board.md` through `Board.ApplyReadyOrder`, which places each story at the bottom of the ready column in turn with `Board.Place`, as dragging it would; it commits nothing. Without `--apply` it writes nothing. `--by` refuses `--before`, `--after`, `--top`, `--bottom`, `--placed-by`, and a story ID, and `--apply` or `--keep-placed` without `--by` is refused. Since S-0219 ([ADR-0088](../adrs/0088-board-md-records-who-placed-a-story-by-hand-and-when-and-a-policy-s-order-keeps.md)) the order keeps hand placements (`Repo.ReadyOrderKeeping`, `workitem.KeepPlaces`): a ready story whose `placed` entry is by anyone but `orchestrator` (`IsOrchestrator`) and within `--keep-placed` of now (`Board.HandPlaced`), `24h` unless given (`DefaultKeepPlaced`), keeps its position in the column, and the other stories fill the remaining positions in the policy's order; `--keep-placed 0` keeps none, and a negative one is refused. The listing ends a kept story's line with `(kept: placed by <by> at <at>)`. `--apply` records no placement. Under `FLAI_ROLE=orchestrate`, `--apply` is refused unless `orchestration.permissions.order_ready` is on (`Repo.OrchestratorPermits`). `--json` prints `workitem.ReadyOrder`: `{ policy, keep_placed?, applied, stories: [{ position, id, title, figure, unit, text, missing, kept? }], order }`, `keep_placed` the window as a Go duration and `kept` the placement `{ by, at }` a story kept its place by, where `figure` is the number ordered by (`per week`, `per hour`, `hours`, or `unix seconds`, as `unit` says), `text` the figure as a person reads it, rounded to two places, and `order` the board's whole pull order, after the apply when there was one. The hostapi read `order.by` (`{policy?}`, the manifest's `orchestration.policy` when empty, `fifo` when that is unset) and the MCP tool `order_by_policy` (`{policy?}`, the same default) answer with the same JSON, with the default window, and never apply. `flai guard` passes `order --by` without `--apply` to a sub-agent, the planner, and the orchestrator as a read; with `--apply` it is a write, which the orchestrator makes only with `order_ready`. |
| `flai promote --candidates [--limit N] [--json]` (S-0217) | Lists the backlog stories that could go to ready, ordered by the project's `orchestration.policy` (`fifo` when unset) as `flai order --by` orders the ready column, each with its figure, and every other backlog story with each reason it is not a candidate (`workitem.PromotionCandidates`). A backlog story is a candidate when it is not a draft; meets the definition of ready: a `## Goal` with words, an `## Acceptance criteria` section with a checkbox, and an epic that is not cancelled; would not be held if it were ready (`Holds.Of`: it declares touches whenever a story is in progress or in review, those touches overlap no such story's, and every story its `after:` names is done); and has a forecast duration and a cost of delay `value`. The others' reasons are `draft: finalize it first`, `no goal`, `no acceptance criteria with a checkbox`, `its epic E-nnnn is cancelled`, the hold's own reason, `no forecast duration`, and `no cost of delay value`, every one that applies. Backlog stories are taken in their pull order, so candidates whose figures tie keep it, and the others are listed in it. `--limit` caps the candidates listed, `0` for all; those beyond it are not listed at all. It writes nothing: moving a candidate to ready is `flai move`. `--json` prints `{ policy, candidates: [{ position, id, title, figure, unit, text }], others: [{ id, title, reasons, held? }] }`, `held` being the hold the story would have in ready. The hostapi read `promote.candidates` (`{limit?}`) and the MCP tool `promote_candidates` (`{limit?}`) answer with the same JSON. `flai guard` passes `promote --candidates` to a sub-agent and the planner as a read. |
| `flai promote --drafts [--json]` (S-0219) | Lists each draft story in the backlog, taken in its pull order and ordered by `orchestration.policy` as the candidates are, each `complete` or `incomplete` with every thing it lacks to be finalized (`Repo.Drafts`, `workitem.FinalizableLacks`): `no "## <section>" section` for each level-two section of the project's story template it lacks, `no goal`, `no acceptance criteria with a checkbox`, `no touches`, `no forecast duration`, `no forecast delivery`, `no cost of delay value`, and `its epic E-nnnn is not found` or `is <done\|cancelled>`. A story with no epic lacks none for it. That is what arithmetic can judge; whether the criteria, touches, and forecast describe the same work is for whoever finalizes it. It writes nothing: finalizing is `flai edit --no-draft`. Exactly one of `--candidates` and `--drafts` is given, and `--limit` is refused with `--drafts`. `--json` prints `workitem.Drafts`: `{ policy, drafts: [{ position, id, title, text?, complete, lacks }] }`. The same check holds the orchestrator when it finalizes (`Repo.Finalizable`, see `flai edit`). No MCP tool or hostapi read gives it; the orchestrator runs it in its shell, and `flai guard` passes it to a sub-agent, the planner, and the orchestrator as a read. |
| `flai push --pending [--publish] [--dry-run]` | On the host, push what is merged and not pushed (S-0063, ADR-0026). Since S-0195 ([ADR-0067](../adrs/0067-accepted-work-reaches-the-remote-only-when-it-is-published-and-agents-publish.md)) it is the operator's shell tool, outside the workflow: publishing (`flai release --pending`, or the board's Publish) is the one way accepted work reaches the remote, no dashboard method runs or previews it, and neither `flai board`, `inbox`, nor the MCP server's instructions mention it. It releases nothing unless the `auto-publish` host action is enabled for the project in the configuration it runs with (S-0144, [ADR-0048](../adrs/0048-pushing-accepted-work-releases-nothing-unless-the-auto-publish-host-action-is-enabled.md)): off, which is the default, what is accepted waits to be released together by `flai release --pending` or the board's Publish. On, before deciding what to push it tags: everything `release.Pending` finds accumulated and unreleased since each component's last tag (the same computation `flai release --pending` uses) is applied (version file, changelog, committed) and tagged on HEAD (S-0094); acceptance alone tags nothing (S-0087). `--dry-run` only previews the plan, and shows one only when it would tag. Tags made on commits the remote already has (an acceptance pushed before it was released) go on their own, three to a push and without the branch (`pending.TagsOnly`, `Unpushed.Pushes`); `flai release --pending` does the same. It then pushes the branch and every tag reachable from it and not from the remote-tracking branch (a release just tagged here, one a prior `flai release --pending` tagged but could not push, or both) with the host's credentials. "nothing pending" otherwise; ordinary commits are left to `git push`; refuses when the two have diverged; never forces. The question is asked offline (`flai/internal/pending`): commits ahead, the items accepted, the tags reachable from the branch and not from the remote-tracking branch, commits behind; until S-0195 `flai board` printed it and `board --json` and `inbox` carried it as `unpushed`. Tags go three to a push and the branch with the last of them (`pending.Batches`, I-0026): GitHub creates no tag events when one push carries more than three tags, so a workflow that releases on a tag never ran; the branch goes last because unpushed tags are found through it, so an interrupted push is still reported. `--publish` (S-0078) also publishes each template component whose `template.yaml` differs between the remote-tracking branch and the checkout, asked before the push because nothing is ahead afterwards, as `flai template push --tag` does and never forced; a publish that fails after the push names the command to finish it. With `auto-publish` on, a clone whose release tags lag the remote's (S-0174), whose remote branch has commits it lacks (S-0195), or that cannot ask the remote, is refused before anything is tagged, dry run or not, as `flai release --pending` refuses it. A remote that has moved exits 3 (`exitPushDiverged`) with a `conflict:` message, so a caller that is not a person tells it from a failure |
| `flai stats [--since 30d] [--type story] [--by nature\|type\|parent] [--bucket hour\|day\|week] [--json]` | The aggregates in [metrics.md](metrics.md) as a table; `--json` adds per-item values, weekly throughput, burn-up (whole set and per parent), cumulative flow, aging, and usage, with what was spent on each item type over time, one point per `--bucket` (S-0163, [ADR-0053](../adrs/0053-usage-is-charted-as-spend-over-time-in-buckets-for-every-item-type-at-once-and.md)). Since S-0272 it counts the story agents' empty wakes, the `wait_for_events` calls that timed out with nothing to report, which flai serve counts from the agents' logs into each story's `usage.empty_wakes` when it measures usage: `items[].usage.empty_wakes`, `waiting.empty_wakes` (`count`, `mean` per item with usage), `waiting.weeks[].empty_wakes`, and an `empty wakes` line at the end of the text report's waiting section ([ADR-0105](../adrs/0105-a-story-s-empty-wakes-the-wait-for-events-calls-of-its-agents-that-timed-out.md)). Since S-0396, under pull-request acceptance, it splits out the parts of review spent on the required checks and on the reviewers, read from the kept forge events under `.flai-cache/forge/status` ([metrics.md](metrics.md#waiting), [ADR-0168](../adrs/0168-under-pull-request-acceptance-flai-stats-reports-a-checks-wait-and-a-reviewers.md)): `items[].wait_checks_seconds` and `items[].wait_reviewers_seconds`, `waiting.weeks[].checks` and `.reviewers`, the `waiting.longest[]` kinds `checks` and `reviewers`, and in the text report, below `in review`, the lines `on checks` and `on reviewers` (`printWaiting`, given whether the manifest accepts through pull requests). In `local` mode it prints neither line, and the weeks carry 0. Since S-0293 it reports the story agents' turns by class (`ceremony`, `test_runs`, `empty_wakes`, `hand_edits`, `work`) from each story's `usage.turns`, every story counted, archived ones included, whatever `--type`: under `turns` in `--json` (`classes`, `total`, `days` with every UTC day of the window, `stories`), and in a section after waiting that prints only when there are turns ([metrics.md](metrics.md#turns-s-0293), [ADR-0116](../adrs/0116-when-flai-measures-a-story-s-usage-from-its-logs-it-classifies-each-turn-of-the.md)). Since S-0337 it reports `coordination`, per ISO week of the window, every story counted whatever `--type`: the conversations between stories opened, agreed, escalated, and closed with their story, from `wip/messages`; the conflicts a trial merge found against those a rebase met at sync and at acceptance, from the conflict log; and the stories started on a share with the hold each saved, from the board's transitions and the conversations' `shares[]` (`metrics/coordination.go`, [metrics.md](metrics.md#coordination), [ADR-0138](../adrs/0138-flai-stats-reports-per-week-the-conversations-between-agents-and-how-they-ended.md)). `--json` has `coordination.logged` and `coordination.weeks[]`, each week with `conversations`, `conflicts` (absent when the log cannot be read), and `shares`. The text report ends with a coordination section, the window's totals then each week that counts any (`printCoordination`), left out when nothing is counted and the log was read.
