The conflict log is `.flai-cache/conflicts.jsonl` in the main checkout's cache (`storygit.ConflictsPath`, `AppendConflict`, `ReadConflicts`, `RecordConflict` in `internal/storygit/conflicts.go`), one JSON line per event with `at`, `story`, `other` on a trial line, `where` (`trial`, `sync`, `accept`), and `paths`, the files a sync settles itself left out. It is append-only and never trimmed, and a failed write is a warning; a missing log counts no conflicts, and one that cannot be read is warned of on stderr, `logged` is false, and the section's conflicts line says so. Since S-0404 it reports `queue`, per ISO week of the window, from the merge queue's event log (`metrics/queue.go`, [metrics.md](metrics.md#merge-queue-s-0404), [ADR-0177](../adrs/0177-flai-stats-reports-per-week-the-stories-the-merge-queue-landed-or-sent-back.md)): the stories it landed and sent back with their mean and longest queue wait, the batches it built with their mean and largest size, and its bisections, under `queue.weeks[]` in `--json`, with each item's `items[].queue_wait_seconds`. The text report prints a merge queue section after waiting (`printQueueStats`): the window's totals, then each week that counts any. Without the log, or with one that cannot be read, which is warned of on stderr, `queue` is absent and the section is not printed. |
| `flai stream open <story-id> [--no-branch]`, `flai stream log <story-id> "<entry>"` | Create a narrative from the template and, in a git repository, branch `story/<id>` from the main branch checked out in a worktree under `.flai-cache/worktrees/<id>` (ADR-0019; `wip/` is written in the main checkout from anywhere), with the host's name as the narrative's `host` (`workitem.ThisHost`). A story in `ready` is started first by `storystart.Begin`, the code `flai story start` moves a story with ([ADR-0153](../adrs/0153-flai-stream-open-starts-a-ready-story-as-flai-story-start-does-moving-it-to-in.md), I-0097): it is refused, exit 4 and nothing changed, when the board holds it, and otherwise moved to `in-progress` as `FLAI_AGENT`, its epic following, printed as `S-nnnn → in-progress` before the narrative (`--json`: `moved`); a story in any other state is opened unmoved. A story whose narrative exists and whose worktree does not, such as one begun on another host, is reopened (S-0177, [ADR-0064](../adrs/0064-a-story-in-ready-or-in-progress-with-no-agent-run-on-this-host-is-started-here.md)): `Repo.ReopenStream` keeps the narrative and records the agent, session, and host opening it now, and `openStoryBranch`, after `git worktree prune` drops the registration of a worktree removed without git, checks out this clone's `story/<id>`, else the remote's (`storygit.Remote`: `origin`, else the only remote) when `git ls-remote --heads` lists it, fetched into a local branch of the same name (`storygit.FetchBranch`), else a new one from the main branch. It says `reopened`, and where the branch came from (`--json`: `reopened`, `from`: `local`, the remote's name, or `main`). A remote that cannot be asked refuses it, saying how to go on. It is refused, as `StreamExistsError`, when git knows its worktree too, with `--no-branch`, or outside git. A start a host crash cut leaves what git cannot repair (S-0375, I-0137), and `flai stream open` repairs it: a narrative that is empty or holds only whitespace counts as none and is written afresh from the template, logged at info, while one with any content is never replaced. `storygit.Open`, which `flai story start` uses too, removes the story's own `story/<id>` loose ref when it resolves to no commit, since git refuses to delete, replace, or make again a broken ref, and its reflog when that is empty too; a reflog with entries is kept. It takes a folder at the worktree path as the worktree only when git knows it (`storygit.IsWorktree`: `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` in it names the folder). A folder git does not know is removed and made again when the branch is missing or broken, or when every file in it is empty; any other may hold work and is refused, naming the folder and saying to check it holds no work, remove it with `rm -rf`, and run `flai stream open <id>` again. Each removal is logged at warn, and `git worktree prune` after it drops a registration whose files are empty. The branch is made with `git -c core.fsync=reference worktree add`, so its ref is on disk before git returns. Append a timestamped log entry, refused with the rule and line when it would bring a finding of the project's markdown lint (`Repo.LintGuard`, S-0179). With the config key `worktrees.relative_paths` on and git 2.48 or newer the worktree is created with `--relative-paths`; on an older git flai warns and creates an ordinary one (ADR-0022). `FLAI_AGENT` and `FLAI_SESSION` identify the writer. `index.md` is regenerated from the active narratives after every open, log, move, and archive. `Repo.ActiveNarratives` returns the streams of the `S-*.md` files that do not parse apart from the narratives, and `Repo.WriteIndex` lists each in its place as unreadable, with its story's status, rather than failing (I-0129, S-0373); `flai check` keeps `narrative.front-matter` as its one finding ([agent-narrative.md](agent-narrative.md#files)). A story's agent does not open a ready story's stream with it: `flai story start` moves the story and opens its stream in one call (S-0274); `flai stream open` stays for taking up a story already in progress. |
| `flai stream answer <story-id> "<question>" "<answer>" [--by]` | Answers a hand-written bullet under a narrative's `## Open questions` (one mirrored from a thread has its own `flai thread reply` instead, S-0090): removes it, exactly as `<question>` reads (an inbox `question` entry's own title), and records it with `<answer>` under `## Decisions` ([agent-narrative.md](agent-narrative.md): "Answered questions move to Decisions"). Refuses if none matches. Where the bullet had a blank line above and below, it leaves one, and it refuses, writing nothing, an answer the project's markdown lint rejects, as `flai stream log` does (S-0376, I-0139). `--by` defaults through `FLAI_AGENT`, then the config author, same as `flai thread reply`. |
| `flai stream state <story-id> [--current "<text>"] [--next "<text>"]` | Writes a narrative's `## Current state` and `## Next steps` (S-0271, `Repo.SetStreamState` in `internal/workitem/narrative.go`), the way agents write them, never by editing the file ([agent-narrative.md](agent-narrative.md#narrative-structure)). `--current` replaces what is under `## Current state` and `--next` what is under `## Next steps`; either may be left out, and its section is left as it is, but giving neither is an error. `-` reads that text from standard input, and only one of the two may be `-`. Every other section is left as it was and nothing is appended to the log, which is `flai stream log`'s. `updated`, `agent`, and `session` are stamped as `flai stream log` stamps them, and `index.md` is written again. The same text again writes nothing (`changed` false). A story not in progress or in review, one with no narrative, and text the project's markdown lint rejects are refused with exit 4, and with `--json` as `{"refused": {...}}`; a text holding a `#` or `##` heading, which would end its section, is an error. `--json` prints `stream`, `path`, `updated`, `written` (the sections given text), and `changed`. The MCP tool `stream_state` and the host channel's `stream.state` do the same. |
| `flai stream diff <story-id>` | The story branch against its merge base with the main branch (S-0041): per file the path, status (added, modified, deleted, renamed), additions, deletions, and unified hunks; binary files without hunks; a file's patch and the whole result are capped and marked `truncated`. Read with git in the main checkout, so it does not need the worktree. `--json` is what the dashboard's review page shows. |
| `flai migrate ids [--dry-run]` | Widen every work item and issue ID to four digits: rename items and narratives in kanban and archive, and issues under `design/issues`, with `git mv` (plain rename outside git) and rewrite references in the layout folders, the root markdown and yaml files, and each project's root markdown files. Idempotent. |
| `flai archive [id...] [--dry-run]` | Move done and cancelled items and their narratives to `wip/archive`; a narrative is written there with each run of blank lines outside fenced code collapsed to one, since no story owns an archived narrative to clear a markdownlint MD012 finding a hand edit left (S-0376, I-0139). Default: every closed epic whose stories are archived, every closed story with its tasks, and closed tasks whose story is gone from the board. Since S-0277 ([ADR-0109](../adrs/0109-archiving-an-item-resolves-the-threads-still-open-on-it.md)) it resolves each thread still `open` or `answered` anchored on an item it archives, with the entry `Resolved: <id> was archived` by the author (`threads.ResolveOnItems`), and commits nothing; it prints `resolved TH-nnnn, open or answered on what it archives`, `--dry-run` names them with `would resolve` (`threads.OnItems`), and `--json` lists them in `resolved_threads`. Since S-0330 ([ADR-0120](../adrs/0120-agents-of-two-open-stories-message-each-other-in-conversations-kept-under-wip.md)) it also closes each conversation under `wip/messages` still stored `open` that names a story it archives, with the entry `Closed: <id> was archived` by the author (`messages.CloseOn`); it prints `closed MS-nnnn, open with S-nnnn`, naming the other story, or `open between` both when both are archived, `--dry-run` prints `would close` for the same, and `--json` lists them in `closed_messages`. The host channel's `board.archive` (`{ids}`) runs it with the IDs the board's Archive and Archive All entries name (S-0343), and refuses an empty list, which would archive every closed item. |
| `flai dashboard [--image] [--tag] [--port] [--bind] [--no-serve] [--pull] [--attach] [--open] [--build] [--allow-unsigned]`, `flai dashboard stop\|status\|logs` | Resolve the image ([Verifying a dashboard release before it is run](#verifying-a-dashboard-release-before-it-is-run)): for `ghcr.io/bytepunx/flaiover`, `dashboard.tag` to a flaiover release and the index digest its verified digest list names, run as `ghcr.io/bytepunx/flaiover@sha256:…`, a release refused starting nothing; for `--build` or another repository, `image:tag` as given, reported unsigned. `--json` adds `image`, `signed`, `release`, and `cached_list` when it starts a container, and `status --json` adds `digest`, `release`, and `signed`. Since S-0238, once `flai serve` has judged the running image, `status` prints its verdict below the digest line (`describeServeImage`): `flai serve: dials it, signed`, with `(flaiover <release>, <digest>)` when those differ from the digest line's; `flai serve: refused it: the dashboard runs an unsigned image <image ID> (<RepoDigests> or no RepoDigests); it does not dial it`; or `flai serve: image not measured: <reason>; the release stamp alone is checked`; `--json` adds it as `serve_image`. Since S-0239 it starts the container with `FLAIOVER_ALLOW_UNSIGNED=1` when `dashboard.allow_unsigned` is in force for the project, with `--build`, or with `--allow-unsigned`, and `status` says so and names the connection's unsigned sides ([Allowing unsigned builds](#allowing-unsigned-builds)). Pull the image if missing (on an unauthorized pull from a non-Hub registry, `docker login` with `GITHUB_TOKEN`, `GH_TOKEN`, or `gh auth token` and one retry, else an error naming the `read:packages` scope; `--build` builds `flaiover:local` from `flaiover/` in the monorepo instead) and run it detached as `flaiover-<project>`, published on `dashboard.bind` (default every interface), as the host user so that it can read its two secrets, and given nothing of the project: no volume, no `PROJECT_DIR`, no git identity, excludes file, read-only git paths, or push key (S-0077, [ADR-0031](../adrs/0031-the-dashboard-s-container-holds-nothing-of-the-project-a-port-and-two-secrets.md), superseding ADR-0022, ADR-0026, and ADR-0027; `dashboard_guard.go` and `dashboard_pushkey.go` are gone). Precedence: flags, manifest `dashboard`, config. `--push-key`, `--push-known-hosts`, and the config keys `dashboard.push_key` and `dashboard.push_known_hosts` are retired but still accepted, hidden from help, so an old configuration loads and can be cleared: set, the start message (and `retired` in `--json`) names them, says they are ignored, and says that publishing from the board (Publish) runs on the host with the operator's credentials, as `flai release --pending` does in a shell (ADR-0067). Status and the already-running message inspect the real container; `status` lists a running container's mounts outside `/run/secrets` (`stale_mounts`) and says it was started by an older flai and should be restarted. Since S-0072 it also creates the agent credential (`.flai-cache/dashboard.agent-key`, mode 0600, never the login token), mounts it at `/run/secrets/flaiover_agent_key` with `FLAIOVER_AGENT_KEY_FILE`, registers the project with `flai serve` (key, root, the address to dial: the bind address, or `127.0.0.1` when bound to every interface, and the credential's path) and, since S-0106, starts `flai host` when none runs for this config, which runs `flai serve` (it starts neither `flai serve` nor `flai mcp` itself; a host running for another config is an error naming it); `--no-serve` skips both. `status`'s `host_flai` adds `host_running` and `host_pid`. The credential and, when flai's config lives in the clone, the `serve` folder are added to the read-only mounts of ADR-0027. `stop` unregisters the project and leaves `flai serve` running; `status` adds `host_flai`: running, registered, connected and since when, or the last error In a folder with no `system-flow.yaml` up the tree (S-0101, [ADR-0036](../adrs/0036-a-folder-that-is-not-a-project-is-served-whole-by-flai-mcp-and-flai-dashboard.md)) every subcommand works with the host's configuration alone. `flai dashboard` starts or reports the shared container and `flai serve`, registers the projects `workitem.FindProjects` finds below the folder, names the folder for import (`import_roots`), and records the dashboard (`serve/dashboards.json`) so that `flai serve` offers it repositories to import with no project served. `stop` unregisters nothing of its own, stops the container once no project is left, and forgets the recorded address. A manifest that does not parse is still an error. |
| `flai dashboard versions`, `flai dashboard upgrade [--tag X.Y.Z] [--published]` (S-0298, [ADR-0117](../adrs/0117-the-host-api-installs-a-release-the-dashboard-names-only-when-flai-lists-it-as.md)) | `versions` lists the published dashboard releases, newest first, marking the one the running container's image carries, the configured tag, and each with no digest list this flai can verify, `not deployable`; `--json` gives `version`, `tag`, `running`, `configured`, and `latest`, and `unverifiable`, `signature`, and `reason` for one not deployable. `upgrade --tag` swaps the container to that release the way `upgrade` swaps it to the configured one, by digest, and with `--published` refuses a tag that is not a published release, naming the published ones, or one not deployable, naming why and the deployable ones, before anything is pulled. `check` and `upgrade` compare the running image's `RepoDigests` with the release's verified list. The tag applies to that container once and `dashboard.tag` is not changed. See [Listing and installing a published release](#listing-and-installing-a-published-release). |
| `flai upgrade [--ref] [--dry-run] [--force] [--keep-all\|--replace-all] [--relock] [--var name=value]` | Bring the project from the version it is at (the lock's, else the manifest's: `projectVersion`) to the version `pickTarget` resolves ([ADR-0103](../adrs/0103-flai-new-import-and-upgrade-follow-the-template-s-releases-the-newest-version.md)). `--ref` wins, a release's version finding its tag as for `flai new`, and is recorded in `template.ref` and the lock. With no `--ref`, `chooseTarget` takes a git template to its newest version tag when the manifest's `template.ref` follows releases and `system-flow.yaml` names the same ref and version as the lock; when it names another (`manifestEdited`) that is not the newest, `settleConflict` asks a terminal (the version `system-flow.yaml` names, the newest, or change nothing), and without one or with `--yes` exits 1 naming `flai upgrade --ref <tag>` for each, while `--dry-run` says it would ask (`would_ask` in `--json`). An edit equal to the newest is applied without asking; an edited version with no release tag is refused, listing the releases. With no `--ref`, a template whose tags cannot be listed stops the upgrade, saying to check the network or pass `--ref`. `--template` with no `--ref` takes that template's default branch, a local template is used as it is, and `--relock` records at the manifest's ref. One line, `template <ref> at <version>: <reason>`, says what is applied and why; `--json` has it in `target`, and `--dry-run --json` prints `{target, plan}`. The upgrade renders each template variable from `--var`, the manifest's five standard fields, the values the lock recorded, or its default, and refuses a required one left empty before changing anything (S-0185). It adds new files, merges marker files above the marker (a project's own `topics` on the file kept, against the template's topics the lock records, S-0134), replaces files unchanged since applied (per `system-flow.lock.yaml`, ADR-0015), reports the rest as conflicts with keep, replace, or diff, and refuses a dirty tree; `--relock` records a hand-assembled project. |
| `flai prime [--cat] [--json]`, `flai prime --story S-nnnn [--budget SIZE] [--role explore\|verify] [--json\|--part N]`, `flai prime --role plan --epic E-nnnn\|--story S-nnnn [--budget SIZE] [--json\|--part N]`, `flai prime --role orchestrate\|analyze\|curate [--budget SIZE] [--json\|--part N]` | Print `design/conventions` in read order, README first, as paths or contents, so an agent or hook loads the norms in one call; the open-issues summary follows when non-empty. Without `--story`, every convention is listed whatever its roles, except that `design-system.md` is left out when the project has no design system guidance document (`context.HasDesignSystem`, `conventions.Set.WithoutDesignSystem`; [ADR-0152](../adrs/0152-agents-build-ui-from-a-tailwind-v4-stylesheet-flai-generates-from-the-tokens.md), S-0386). `--story` prints the story's context pack ([ADR-0047](../adrs/0047-an-agent-is-primed-with-what-its-story-s-topics-claim-and-links-select.md); conventions S-0136, design S-0137), fitted to a size budget ([ADR-0049](../adrs/0049-a-story-s-context-pack-fits-a-size-budget-what-the-story-names-loads-whole-what.md), S-0146). Budget: `context.Budget` takes `--budget`, else the manifest's `prime.budget`, else `context.DefaultBudget` (80 KB), each read by `context.ParseSize` (bytes, or `K`/`KB`/`KiB`/`M`/`MB`/`MiB`, 1024-based); `--budget` without `--story` is refused. Conventions: `flai/internal/context` (`ctxpack` in `cmd`) runs `topics.Parse` on the README and each convention the story's agent reads, those whose `roles` are empty or list `story` (`conventions.File.ReadBy`, [ADR-0068](../adrs/0068-a-convention-s-roles-list-every-agent-that-reads-it-the-story-s-agent-included.md), S-0196), less `design-system.md` unless the design system reaches the story (below), with `[all]` as the default and keeps a section when `context.Matches` finds `all` or one of the story's topics (`topics.ForStory`) among its effective topics; the front matter, the baseline marker, the `## Project additions` heading, and the heading line of a left-out section above a kept one always stay; with every section selected the conventions part is byte for byte `--cat`. Design: `context.LoadDocs` reads `design/system`, `design/tech`, and `design/adrs` (not their `README.md`, not `0000-template.md`) into `Doc`s cut at every heading by `topics.Parse` with no default topics, with an ADR's `id`, `refines`, and `superseded_by`. `context.Design` runs four steps on a `Selection`, which gives each loaded section to the first reason that chooses it and lists later reasons on it (`also`), and keeps one `brief` per briefed document likewise, so nothing prints twice: `Linked` over the story, its epic, and its tasks, archived or not (markdown links, a `#fragment` narrowing to that heading and the ones below it, a link that does not resolve as written matched by its tail; a document's repository path written out; `ADR-nnnn`), loaded whole (step `named`, reason `linked from S-nnnn`), except that a document named only by its path written out and larger than `Selection.BriefOver` (an eighth of the budget, `context.BriefOver`) is briefed instead, reason `named in S-nnnn`, after every link and ID has been chosen, so that a link or ID to it anywhere still loads it, and `Step` follows only the sections its topics select ([ADR-0050](../adrs/0050-a-document-a-story-names-only-by-its-path-written-out-is-briefed-when-it-is.md), S-0149); `DesignSystem` (ADR-0152, S-0386), which, when the design system guidance's front-matter `topics` reach the story (`DesignSystem.Reaches`: `all`, or one of the story's), loads the guidance and the token file whole, as if the story named them (step `named`, reason `design system: topics <matched>`), the token file read from the manifest's `design.tokens` as raw text in one section (`KindTokens`, `loadDesignSystem`), each briefed instead when larger than `BriefOver`, a document already loaded whole getting the reason in `also`; `ByTopics`, which briefs each design or tech file with a section whose topics include `all` or one of the story's, remembering the sections that matched, and each such ADR (step `briefed`, reason `topics: cli, go`), a document already loaded whole getting the reason in `also` instead; and `Step`, which from every named section, every topic-selected section of a briefed file, and every briefed ADR briefs the ADRs they link or name (`linked from design/system/x.md § Heading`), the ADRs a named or briefed ADR refines (`refined by ADR-nnnn`), and the ADRs that refine one (`refines ADR-nnnn`), not followed again. A superseded ADR, wherever chosen, is replaced by the ADRs in force along `superseded_by` (`supersedes ADR-nnnn`). A design brief is `Doc.FirstParagraph` (the first paragraph under the title, else under the first heading with prose) and the outline of every heading below the title, marked `selected` when the topics chose some headings and not others and `loaded` when a section is loaded; an ADR brief is `Doc.Decision`, the first sentence under `## Decision` with bold markers removed (and the next when the first is under five words, as with a bold lead-in), a sentence ending at `.`, `!`, or `?` outside backticks before a space or the end. `Pack.AddDesign` measures the pack (`Pack.size` counts `Header` and `Body` together, re-measuring until the header's own number is stable): when the conventions and open issues alone exceed the budget it sets `exceeded: conventions` and the pack is them and a catalog of every document; when adding what is named exceeds it, `exceeded: named`, and the briefs are added but nothing ranked; when the briefs take it over, `exceeded: briefs`, every brief is kept, and nothing is ranked (TH-0032); otherwise `Selection.Candidates` indexes each section nothing loaded as a `search.Doc` (briefed documents included; a superseded ADR, an ADR loaded in any part, the token file, or a section with nothing below its heading left out), searches with the story's title, `## Goal`, and `## Acceptance criteria` without stopwords (`context.Query`, `context.Terms`), keeps an ADR once at its best section, and returns them best first; each is tried in order, an ADR whole and then as its best section, a design section alone, and kept (`rank n`, `Selection.Rank`) when the pack still fits, undone when it does not. `Selection.Items` lists what is named, then design briefs, then ADR briefs, then what is ranked; loaded items print each reason's consecutive sections of one document as one item, the whole file with its front matter when the reason owns all of it; a briefed document that ends up loaded whole prints once, loaded, with the brief's reasons in `also`. The digest step ([ADR-0194](../adrs/0194-a-story-s-pack-loads-the-digest-entries-its-topics-or-claim-select-ranked.md), S-0430, `context.digestFor` and `Pack.addDigest` in `digest.go`) runs after the briefs and before the ranked step: the `active` and `compacted` entries of `design/digest/` that share a topic other than `all` with the story, or whose paths meet its claim, never an `absorbed` one, ranked by BM25 against the ranked step's query, claim matches first, each an item with `step: digest`, its `D-nnnn`, and the reason `path: <claimed path>; rank n` or `topics: <topics>; rank n` (`; compacted` after a compacted one, which loads as a one-line brief), added while the digest's items and heading stay within `Budget/DigestShare`, an eighth, and, on a pack within its budget before the step, the pack within the budget, and printed under a `digest` heading; on a pack over its budget before the step, whatever `exceeded` says, it still loads within the eighth (TH-0624). A sub-agent's pack takes the same entries as one-line briefs, and the orchestrator's (`orchestratorDigest` and `answersFor` in `strategic.go`) the `answer` and `recommendation` entries ranked against each thread awaiting the operator by its title and first entry, round by round, with the reason `thread TH-nnnn; rank n`. `Selection.Catalog` lists the documents neither loaded nor briefed (`superseded_by` noted) and those loaded in part with their heading outline. `Pack.Header` names the story, each topic with `StoryTopic.Summary`, the size in bytes and lines against the budget, a line saying which part alone is over when one is, and `contents, in bytes`: one row per convention, the open issues, each named, briefed, and ranked item (the ADR briefs as one row), the catalog, and the left-out list. `Pack.Body` is the conventions, the open issues, each loaded item headed with path, `§` heading path, and `reason:` (with `also`), `briefs` (one `path: title (whole bytes; first reason, and n more)` line each, a line telling the agent to decide from the brief and read the file before relying on it or changing it when the reason is `named in`, or that the design system reaches the story and to read it whole before building or changing UI when the reason is `design system: topics`, then the brief), `decisions` (one `- ADR-nnnn title (path; first reason, and n more): decision` line each), the ranked items, `catalog`, and `left out`. `--json` is the `Pack`: `story`, `title`, `topics` with every source, `budget`, `exceeded`, `size`, `readme`, `conventions` (each with `kept` and `left_out` sections, their heading paths, lines, and topics, the filtered `text`, and its `size`), `open_issues`, `left_out`, `items` (`path`, `id`, `title`, `heading`, `label`, `step`, `reason`, `also`, `size`, `whole`, `text`), and `catalog` (`not_loaded`, `in_part` with `outline`). An archived story gets today's pack, so a pack can be replayed; an unknown ID or an item that is not a story fails naming it. `flai check` warns with `adr.decision` about an ADR whose `## Decision` is missing or opens with a list, table, quote, code block, or heading, or whose first paragraph does not end a sentence. `--role explore` or `--role verify` (S-0175, [ADR-0059](../adrs/0059-a-story-s-agent-hands-search-test-runs-and-verification-to-an-explorer-and-a.md)) prints the pack for a sub-agent of the story's agent, built by `ctxpack.ForRole`: the conventions whose front matter `roles` are empty or list the role (`conventions.File.ReadBy`; `flai check` accepts `conventions.Roles`, which are `story`, `explore`, `verify`, `plan`, `orchestrate`, `analyze`, and since S-0431 `curate`, and warns with `conventions.roles` about any other), filtered by the story's topics as above, less `design-system.md` unless the design system reaches the story, with no README and no open issues; the story's `## Goal` and `## Acceptance criteria` (`context.Goal`, printed under `story` and in the JSON as `goal`); and briefs, never bodies, from a `Selection` with `BriefNamed` set, so that what the story, its epic, and its tasks link or name is briefed with its reason (a design file's brief counting every section named as selected, so the one step reaches from it), then the design system's guidance and token file when it reaches the story, then what their topics select, then the ADRs one step reaches, in the order `Items` gives them, each while the budget has room (`Pack.AddBriefs`); `briefs_left_out` counts the rest, and the header says so. Nothing is ranked and there is no catalog. The header names the role and tells the sub-agent to report to the agent that started it and to change no item or thread. The budget is half the story's agent's (`prime.budget`, else 80 KB) unless `--budget` is given; `exceeded` is `conventions` when the role's conventions and the goal alone exceed it. `--role explore` or `verify` without `--story` fails. `--role plan`, `orchestrate`, `analyze`, or since S-0431 `curate` (S-0207, [ADR-0075](../adrs/0075-the-planner-the-orchestrator-and-the-analyzer-prime-by-role-plan-orchestrate-or.md)) prints the pack for a strategic agent, one that works above a story, built by `ctxpack.ForStrategic` (`conventions.StrategicRoles`): the planner with `--epic E-nnnn` or `--story S-nnnn`, and the orchestrator, the analyzer, and the curator for the whole project, with neither. Its topics are the role's, `planning`, `orchestration`, `analysis`, or the curator's `digest` and `planning`, with source kind `role` (`context.FromRole`), first, and for the planner the item's after them: a story's as `topics.ForStory` gives them, an epic's as `topics.OfStory` derives them from its own topics, tags, and touches. It holds the conventions whose `roles` are empty or list the role, filtered by those topics, with no README; the open issues; for the planner what the item names, whole, through the same `Selection` as a story's pack (a story with its epic and tasks, an epic alone, whose stories the planner reads as items), and the sections ranked highest against the item's title and body; briefs of the design, tech, and ADRs its topics select and the ADRs one step reaches; and a catalog; no design system step runs. The budget is the story's agent's, not halved. The header names the role and, for the planner, the item. `--json` has `role` and, for the planner, `item`, and no `story`. The orchestrator and the analyzer refuse `--epic` or `--story`; the planner refuses neither given, both, a task, or an ID of the other type than its flag; `--epic` without `--role plan` fails, and so does an unknown role. `--part N` (S-0261, [ADR-0104](../adrs/0104-the-mcp-prime-tool-returns-a-context-pack-in-parts-of-at-most-40-000-bytes-of.md)) prints part N of the pack exactly as the MCP `prime` tool returns it, and implies `--json`: the pack's JSON cut into parts of at most `context.PartLimit` (40,000 bytes) each, because Claude Code saves a tool result over 50,000 characters to a file. The pack, its budget, and its order are unchanged. Part 1 carries the header (`story`, `title`, `role`, `goal`, `topics`, `budget`, `exceeded`, `size`, `readme`, `open_issues`, `left_out`), then conventions and items in the pack's order while they fit; each later part carries the story, its number, and the next conventions and items; the catalog is in the last part. Every part carries `part` and `parts`, and a pack that fits one part is `parts: 1`. A part ends before the convention or item that would take it over the limit; one too large for any part is split at line boundaries into chunks carrying `chunk` and `chunks`, each starting a part. A part past `parts` is refused with the number of parts. Parts are cut afresh on each run, so a pack that changes between two runs can repeat or skip an element at a boundary. Without `--part`, the whole pack prints as before. |
| `flai guard` | Reads a Claude Code `PreToolUse` hook's input on standard input and refuses the call with exit 2 and the reason on standard error when the input's `agent_id` says a sub-agent makes it (`agent_type` names it in the message; a session started with `--agent` may carry a type but not an ID) and it would change a work item, a thread, a conversation between stories (S-0331), a narrative, or history (S-0175, [ADR-0060](../adrs/0060-a-claude-code-pretooluse-hook-flai-guard-refuses-any-sub-agent-s-call-that.md)): a flai MCP tool other than those in `guard.MCPReads`: `board`, `doc_get`, `doc_search`, `item_get`, `order_by_policy`, `prime`, `promote_candidates`, `release_evaluate` (S-0217), `test` (S-0273), `thread_get`, `verify` (S-0270), and `who_touches`, and those in `guard.MCPStoryReads`, `message_get` (S-0331), so `message_send`, `message_reply`, `message_escalate` (S-0332), and `message_share` (S-0334) are refused; a flai command other than `board` without a subcommand, `check`, `cod`, `components list` and `components` without a subcommand (S-0477), `doc search` and `doc show`, `forecast`, `help`, `issue list`, `message list` and `message show` (S-0331), `prime`, `show`, `stats`, `stream diff`, `test` (S-0273), `thread list` and `thread show`, `touches suggest` (S-0210), and `version`, or any flai command given `--help`, and, since S-0217, the commands that read only in one form (`guard.flagReads`): `order` with `--by` and without `--apply`, a story, or a flag that places one (`ordersByPolicy`, S-0219), `plan` with `--candidates` (S-0219), `promote` with `--candidates` or, since S-0219, `--drafts`, `release` with `--evaluate`, and, since S-0270, `verify` without `--record-issues`, which writes issues, `--last` included, the flag counted as given when its last occurrence is bare or has a value that is neither empty nor false, and `--apply` refusing whatever its value; and a git command other than `blame`, `cat-file`, `describe`, `diff`, `grep`, `log`, `ls-files`, `ls-tree`, `merge-base`, `rev-list`, `rev-parse`, `shortlog`, `show`, and `status`. `internal/guard` splits a shell line into simple commands at `;`, `&`, `\|`, newlines, subshells, and command substitutions, and looks at every word of each, not only the first, so a command run through an assignment, `env`, `sudo`, `timeout`, `xargs`, or `find -exec` is found: a word is flai run with a command when one of flai's command names (`cmd.Root().Commands()`) follows it after `--config` and its value, and git run with one when a plain lower-case word follows it after `-C`, `-c`, `--git-dir`, `--work-tree`, and `--namespace` and their values; a shell (`sh`, `bash`, `zsh`, `dash`, `ksh`) given a flag group holding `c` (`-c`, `-lc`, `-ec`) has its script checked, and so does what follows `eval`. Since S-0246 (I-0058) a heredoc's body is input, not commands: `guard.commands` reads `<<` and `<<-` with a plain, quoted, or backslashed delimiter, leaves each body out up to its delimiter's line (tabs stripped for `<<-`; with none, to the end), and splits it as a script only when a word on the line that opens it names a shell (`runsShell`), as in `bash <<EOF` or `cat <<EOF \| sh`. A here-string (`<<<`), and `<<` inside quotes or `$(( ))`, opens none. Before, the body's lines were read as commands, so a sub-agent's `python3 - <<'EOF'` or `cat > f <<'EOF'` whose text named a flai write was refused, and an apostrophe in it left a quote open over the lines after. It is not a shell: a name with a backslash in it, or a command held in a variable, gets past it, and `echo git commit` is refused. A call with no `agent_id`, the session's own agent's, passes; so does input it cannot parse, with a `warn` event: it fails open. The template's `.claude/settings.json` runs it before `Bash` and every `mcp__flai__` tool, in a line of shell that passes on only exit 2 and its message (`out=$(flai guard 2>&1); [ $? -eq 2 ] \|\| exit 0; …`), so that a project whose `flai` on `PATH` has no `guard`, or that has none, sees no hook error and gets no guard; the template's `min_flai` stays as it is. This repository's runs `scripts/flai.sh guard` the same way. Since S-0208 ([ADR-0082](../adrs/0082-flai-serve-starts-the-planner-for-an-epic-or-a-story-behind-the-plan-host.md)) it also holds a planner session to planning: with `FLAI_ROLE=plan` in its environment (`guard.Guard.Role`), a call with no `agent_id`, the planner's own, is checked by `Guard.plan`. It passes the reads above; the MCP tools in `guard.MCPPlans` (`activity_log`, `inbox`, `item_edit`, `item_new`, `thread_open`, `thread_reply`, `wait_for_events`), `item_new` of a story only with `draft: true` (S-0209), and `item_move` whose `to` is `backlog`; the commands `edit` (refused with `--no-draft`: finalizing is the operator's), `epic new`, `issue new` and `bump`, `story new` only with `--draft`, the last one deciding (S-0209: the planner's stories are drafts for the operator to finalize), `task new` (S-0255), `thread new` and `reply`, and `touches`, and `move <id> backlog` (its positional words read past `--by`, `--config`, and `--reason` and their values). It refuses every other flai tool and command, git's writes, and `Edit`, `Write`, and `NotebookEdit`, each with the rule and `ask the operator with thread_open on the item, or say it in your final summary`; other shell commands pass, as a sub-agent's do. The planner's sub-agents are checked as any sub-agent's. Claude Code runs a hook only for the tools its matcher names, so both settings files carry a second `PreToolUse` entry, on `Edit\|MultiEdit\|Write\|NotebookEdit` since S-0299 and on `Edit\|Write\|NotebookEdit` before it, whose command exits 0 at once unless `FLAI_ROLE` is `plan`, since S-0218 `orchestrate`, since S-0223 `analyze`, or since S-0431 `curate`, or, since S-0299, `FLAI_STORY` is set. Until S-0299 a story's session ran no guard per edit (the operator's choice on TH-0096); it now runs one, about 18 ms a call, so that a story's sub-agent's write to a path Claude Code protects meets the refusal described at the end of this row, and the operator's own session, with none of those set, still runs none. The template's `min_flai` stays as it is: an older `flai guard` passes the planner's calls. See [strategic-agents.md](strategic-agents.md#the-guard). Since S-0218 ([ADR-0087](../adrs/0087-flai-serve-runs-one-orchestrator-per-project-behind-the-orchestrate-host-action.md)) it also holds an orchestrator session to the operator's `orchestration.permissions`: with `FLAI_ROLE=orchestrate`, the command opens the project from the working directory (`app.guardProject`), sets `Guard.Permissions` from its manifest, all off with a `warn` event when it cannot, and `guard.Decide` checks a call with no `agent_id` with `Guard.orchestrate`, which returns a `guard.Verdict` (`Reason`, `Call`, `Needs`; `guard.Refusal`, with `Why`, in Claude Code's form). It passes the reads above, the MCP tools in `guard.MCPOrchestrates` (`activity_log`, `inbox`, `thread_open`, `wait_for_events`), and the commands in `cliOrchestrates` (`issue new` and `bump`, `thread new`). Each permission (`manifest.Permissions.Allows`) allows more: `plan_backlog_epics` the MCP tool `plan` and `flai plan` on an epic's ID (`isEpic`); `plan_backlog_stories` (S-0328) the MCP tool `plan` and `flai plan` on a story's ID, `item_edit` on a story whose input gives `cost_of_delay` with only `revenue_per_week`, `penalty_per_week`, or `time_lost_per_cycle` and no field but `id`, `hash`, and `project`, `flai edit` with only those three input flags beside the same flags `finalize_drafts` allows, and on a thread a story's planner opened (`planner-S-nnnn`) `thread_reply` and `thread reply` as an answer, with or without a source, or as a recommendation, and `thread_resolve` and `thread resolve`, whatever `answer_threads` says; `finalize_drafts` `item_edit` whose input gives `draft: false` and no field but `id`, `hash`, and `project` (`finalizesDraft`, S-0219), and `flai edit` with `--no-draft` and nothing else but `--by`, `--config`, `--hash`, `--json`, `--verbose`, and `--yes` (`finalizesOnly`); `promote_to_ready` `item_move` whose `to` is `ready` and `move <id> ready`; `order_ready` `flai order --by <policy> --apply`, and never, since S-0219, `flai order` placing a story by hand (`byHand`); `answer_threads` `thread_reply` and `thread reply` on a thread another opened, as a recommendation (`recommendation`, `--recommend`) while it is `recommend`, and also as an answer citing a source (`source`, `--source`) while it is `autonomous`, an unsourced answer refused with the advice to post it as a recommendation (S-0220); on a thread the orchestrator opened, read through `Guard.Opener` (`guard.ThreadOpener`, `threads.Get` in the main checkout), it follows up and resolves but never recommends or answers, and it never resolves another's thread, never runs `thread confirm`, and never names another author with `--by`, whatever its permissions; a thread whose opener cannot be read is held as another's; `accept_reviews` `accept` and `move <story> done`, each only with `--by orchestrator`, any other `--by` refused as `acceptsAsSelf` whatever the permission (S-0221, [ADR-0093](../adrs/0093-with-accept-reviews-on-the-orchestrator-accepts-a-story-in-review-through-flai.md)), while `item_move` to done is never allowed and its refusal names `flai accept --by orchestrator` (`acceptsWithFlai`), and since S-0403 the enqueue as itself, the MCP tool `queue_add` (`guard.QueueAdd`) and `flai queue add` with `--by orchestrator` (`enqueuesAsSelf`), while `flai queue` with no subcommand is a read whatever the permissions, and `flai accept --batch` and `flai queue remove`, `hold`, and `resume` are refused whatever they are; `publish` the MCP tool `release_publish` (`guard.ReleasePublish`), and since S-0222 ([ADR-0094](../adrs/0094-with-publish-on-the-orchestrator-publishes-only-through-release-publish-by-its.md)) nothing else: `flai release` other than `--evaluate`, `flai push`, `git push`, and `git tag` are refused as what the orchestrator never does, whatever its permissions (`publishesThrough`), and `release_publish` is refused to every session whose `FLAI_ROLE` is none of `orchestrate`, `plan`, and `analyze`, the planner's and the analyzer's own rules refusing it there. A call a permission would allow is refused while it is off, with `Needs` set to `orchestration.permissions.<name>` and the message `the orchestrator cannot <what>: it needs orchestration.permissions.<name>, which is off`; any other write, `Edit`, `Write`, and `NotebookEdit`, every other flai tool and command that writes, `item_edit` that changes anything but the draft flag or, under `plan_backlog_stories`, a story's cost of delay inputs among them, and git's writes, is refused as what `the orchestrator never does, whatever its permissions`, with the reason. Both end by saying to ask the operator with `thread_open` on the item. Each refusal of the orchestrator's own call is appended with `workitem.Repo.AppendRefusal` to `wip/agents/orchestrator.md` under `## Refusals` (time, `- Call:`, `- Needs:` or `none`), under the activity document's lock (`lockActivity`, `.flai-cache/activity-<kind>.lock`, taken over after 10 s); one that cannot be logged is a `warn` event, and the call is refused all the same. Since S-0219 flai holds the orchestrator to its work permissions itself as well, `plan_backlog_stories` among them since S-0328, so a call the guard does not see is held (see `flai plan`, `flai edit`, `flai move`, and `flai order --by`). See [strategic-agents.md](strategic-agents.md#its-permissions-and-the-guard). Since S-0223 ([ADR-0099](../adrs/0099-the-analyzer-runs-behind-the-analyze-host-action-and-writes-one-report-under.md)) it also holds an analyzer session to its report: with `FLAI_ROLE=analyze` (`guard.RoleAnalyze`), the command opens the project from the working directory (`app.guardProject`) and sets `Guard.Root` and `Guard.Reports`, the reports folder (`analysis.Dir`), leaving both empty with a `warn` event when it cannot, and `Guard.Decide` checks a call with no `agent_id` with `Guard.analyze`. It passes the reads above, `stats` among them, and git's (`analyzing`); the MCP tools in `guard.MCPAnalyzes` (`activity_log`, `inbox`, `thread_open`, `thread_reply`, `wait_for_events`, and since S-0224 `issue_new` and `issue_bump`); since S-0224 the commands in `cliAnalyzes`, `issue new` and `bump`, with which it files and bumps the issues its findings call for; and `Edit`, `Write`, and `NotebookEdit` only on a `file_path` or `notebook_path` inside the reports folder (`Guard.inReports`: a relative path joined to `Root`, cleaned, and it and the folder each resolved with its symbolic links along the longest part that exists, so that neither `..` nor a link leads out), every edit refused while `Root` or `Reports` is empty. It refuses `item_new`, `item_edit`, `item_move`, and `issue_story` (`itemWrites`), and the commands `story`, `epic`, `task`, and `issue story` (`cliAuthors`), as `the analyzer authors no stories`; every other flai tool, `plan`, `analyze`, and `release_publish` among them; every other flai command, `issue close` among them; and git's writes, each with the rule and the advice to put what it found, and the stories it would suggest, in its report, or ask the operator with `thread_open`; a refused edit names the folder. Other shell commands pass, as a sub-agent's do. The analyzer's sub-agents are checked as any sub-agent's. See [strategic-agents.md](strategic-agents.md#the-analyzers-guard). Since S-0431 ([ADR-0196](../adrs/0196-the-curator-runs-behind-the-curate-host-action-and-takes-in-what-the-archive.md)) it also holds a curator session to the digest: with `FLAI_ROLE=curate` (`guard.RoleCurate`), `Guard.Decide` checks a call with no `agent_id` with `Guard.curate`. It passes the reads above and git's (`curating`), the MCP tools in `guard.MCPCurates` (`activity_log`, `inbox`, `thread_open`, `thread_reply`, `wait_for_events`), and the commands in `cliCurates`, `digest harvest`, `new`, and `edit`, and since S-0432 ([ADR-0199](../adrs/0199-the-curator-runs-after-acceptances-coalesced-and-on-digest-schedule-marks.md)) `absorb`, `cited`, and `compact`; `--by` on `digest absorb` names a document, not an author (`bySubNamesNoAuthor`), so the writes-as-itself check passes it. It refuses `Edit`, `MultiEdit`, `Write`, and `NotebookEdit` on every file; `digest seed` (`seedsUnjudged`); every other flai tool, `item_new`, `item_edit`, `item_move`, `issue_new`, `issue_bump`, `curate`, `analyze`, `plan`, and `release_publish` among them; every other flai command that writes; and git's writes, each saying that the curator writes digest entries only with `flai digest new`, `edit`, `absorb`, `cited`, and `compact`, edits no file, and authors no work item. Other shell commands pass. The curator's sub-agents are checked as any sub-agent's. See [strategic-agents.md](strategic-agents.md#the-curators-guard). Since S-0285 ([ADR-0092](../adrs/0092-a-story-s-agent-waits-for-a-sub-agent-by-launching-it-in-the-foreground-and.md)) it also keeps a story's agent from waiting on its sub-agents with `wait_for_events`. Given a `SubagentStart` or `SubagentStop` hook's input, as `hook_event_name` says (`guard.HookSubagentStart`, `guard.HookSubagentStop`; any other input, named `PreToolUse` or not, is a tool call), the command opens the project from the working directory and `guard.Record` notes the sub-agent (`agent_id`, `agent_type`, and when it started) as running in the hook's session, or no longer, in `.flai-cache/guard/<session_id>.json` in the main checkout (`guard.RecordDir`), read and replaced under a lock (`<session_id>.json.lock`, waited on for 10 s and taken over after 5 s), since a layer's sub-agents start in the same second; a record with none running is removed, and a session ID that is not a safe file name is ignored. These hooks never refuse and print nothing; a record that cannot be written is a `warn` event. In a story's agent's session, `FLAI_STORY` set and no `FLAI_ROLE` (`Guard.Story`), `Guard.wait` refuses the agent's own `mcp__flai__wait_for_events` while `guard.Running` lists a sub-agent of the session and no unresolved thread is on the story or one of its tasks (`guard.StoryThreadOpen`), naming each running sub-agent by its type and ID and saying to wait for one by launching it with the Agent tool's `run_in_background` set to false, a layer's in one message, and to use `wait_for_events` only for a thread awaiting the designer; with such a thread open the wait passes and returns on the designer's answer. A project, record, or threads it cannot read let the call through, with a `warn` event. Both settings files run it on `SubagentStart` and `SubagentStop` behind `[ -n "$FLAI_STORY" ] \|\| exit 0`, discarding its output and exiting 0, so that only a story's agent's session records its sub-agents and a project whose `flai` has no record sees no hook error; an older `flai guard` reads the new events as tool calls it does not refuse. A session that ended with a sub-agent out keeps it in its record, since no `SubagentStop` fires. Since S-0295 ([ADR-0096](../adrs/0096-a-story-in-review-holds-nothing-an-overlap-inside-the-manifest-s-shared-paths.md)) it refuses the MCP tool `shared_paths_edit` and `flai shared add` and `remove` to every session flai serve starts (`FLAI_STARTED_BY=flai-serve`: a story's agent and its sub-agents, the planner, the orchestrator, and the analyzer), whatever the orchestrator's permissions, because the shared paths decide what holds; the refusal says to ask the operator on a thread. `shared_paths`, `flai shared list`, and `flai shared check` are reads. Since S-0299 ([ADR-0102](../adrs/0102-while-auto-approve-is-off-flai-guard-refuses-a-story-s-sub-agent-a-write-to-a.md)) it refuses a sub-agent's `Edit`, `MultiEdit`, `Write`, or `NotebookEdit` of a path Claude Code protects (`guard.ProtectedWrite`, on `file_path` or `notebook_path`: until S-0286 a file with a folder named `.claude` along its path, and since then any path `protected.Path` names but one in `.git`, which `permission_prompt` refuses at once, [ADR-0106](../adrs/0106-a-story-whose-branch-changes-a-path-claude-code-protects-is-accepted-by-the.md)) at once, before its other rules, while the project's `auto-approve` host action is off (`Guard.AutoApprove`, which the command sets for such a call alone with `app.guardAutoApprove`: the configuration's `host_actions` for the main checkout the hook runs in, as `flai mcp` reads it for `permission_prompt`). Otherwise `permission_prompt` would open a thread and hold the call, and the sub-agent's layer with it, until the owner answered, as I-0093 records, or since S-0309 four minutes at most ([ADR-0124](../adrs/0124-permission-prompt-holds-a-write-at-most-four-minutes-then-refuses-it-and-leaves.md)). The refusal names the sub-agent and the file and tells it to put the file's whole new content in its final message, for the story's agent to write; the story's agent's start prompt says the same (see `flai serve agent`). With `auto-approve` on the write passes, and `permission_prompt` allows it at once. A configuration or project the command cannot read counts `auto-approve` as off, with a `warn` event. The story's agent's own write carries no `agent_id` and passes to `permission_prompt`, which decides it as ADR-0086 says. Since S-0353 ([ADR-0130](../adrs/0130-every-harness-meets-flai-through-neutral-contracts-an-adapter-s-capabilities-a.md)) the rules above name no harness. They decide on a neutral `guard.Call` (`call.go`), which holds the harness session's ID (`Session`), whether a sub-agent makes the call (`Subagent`) and its definition's name (`Agent`), `FLAI_ROLE` and `FLAI_STORY` (`Role`, `Story`), the `Kind` (`Shell`, `FileWrite`, `FlaiTool`, or `Other`, on which no rule holds), the shell line (`Command`), the file written (`Path`), the flai tool's name without any harness prefix (`Tool`) with its arguments (`Args`), and the harness's own name for the tool (`Name`), used only in a refusal's text. One decision, `guard.Decide(g, c)`, takes the `Guard`, the project's policy (orchestration permissions, the thread opener, the reports folder, auto-approve, the read-only definitions, the session's own names, and `Running`, the running sub-agents, read only when the wait rule needs them), and returns a `guard.Verdict`: `Allow`, `Deny` with a `Reason`, or `Ask` with a `Reason`, which since S-0354 a story's agent's write to a protected path gets (below); for the orchestrator's own refusals it carries `Call` and `Needs` too. One reader per harness (`guard.Reader`, by name in `guard.ReaderFor`) turns the hook's input into a `guard.Hook`, a `Call` or a sub-agent's start or stop (`guard.SubAgentChange`, which `guard.RecordChange` notes), and answers a verdict with the exit the harness expects. Claude Code's (`claudecode.go`, `guard.ClaudeCode`) is the one built: it maps `Bash` onto `Shell`, `Edit`, `MultiEdit`, `Write`, and `NotebookEdit` onto `FileWrite` with `file_path` or `notebook_path`, `mcp__flai__<tool>` onto `FlaiTool`, `agent_id` onto `Subagent`, `agent_type` onto `Agent`, and `SubagentStart` and `SubagentStop` onto the record, and answers `Deny` with exit 2 and the reason on standard error, and, since S-0354, `Ask` with exit 0, as it answers `Allow`, so that Claude Code's own permission handling calls `permission_prompt`. `guard.Event`, `Guard.Decide(Event)`, `Guard.Check`, and `guard.Record` stay as its forms of the call. `flai guard --harness <name>`, default `claude-code`, names the reader; a harness with none passes the call with a `warn` event naming the readers there are, as the guard fails open (ADR-0060). The refusal texts did not change. One rule widened: the planner, the orchestrator, and the analyzer meet `MultiEdit` as they meet `Edit`, where before it passed them. Since S-0354 ([ADR-0130](../adrs/0130-every-harness-meets-flai-through-neutral-contracts-an-adapter-s-capabilities-a.md)) the guard asks. `guard.Decide` returns `Ask` for a `FileWrite` by a story's own agent, with `Story` set and no `Role`, to a path flai protects (`protected.Path`) inside the story's worktree, below `.flai-cache/worktrees/<story>/` (`inWorktree`), other than one in `.git` (`protected.Git`), while `Guard.AutoApprove` is off; with it on, it allows the write. `flai guard` reads auto-approve for that write as it does for a sub-agent's (`app.guardAutoApprove`). A sub-agent's write to a protected path is still refused at once while auto-approve is off ([ADR-0102](../adrs/0102-while-auto-approve-is-off-flai-guard-refuses-a-story-s-sub-agent-a-write-to-a.md)). `guard.Hold(ctx, g, a, c, bound)` decides as `Decide` does and holds an `Ask` through an `ask.Asker` within `bound`, the harness's: it reads the story, denies the write when the story is not in progress, asks the story's owner or the project's owner with the tool, the path relative to the worktree, and why, and allows the write or denies it with `ask`'s reason; any other verdict comes back as `Decide` gives it. `Hold` is for a reader whose harness calls no permission tool of flai's. Claude Code's reader does not use it, and no other reader is built. What a story's agent sees is unchanged. Since S-0428 ([ADR-0191](../adrs/0191-the-digest-is-one-markdown-file-per-entry-under-the-design-folder-s-digest.md)) `digest list` and `digest show` are reads, in `cliReads`, so every sub-agent and the planner's, the orchestrator's, and the analyzer's own calls read the digest; `digest new` and `digest edit` write an entry and are refused to every one of them, read-only or not. Since S-0477 ([ADR-0186](../adrs/0186-flai-guard-lets-every-agent-run-flai-components-list-and-a-story-s-task-sub.md), I-0165, I-0156) `components list` and `components` alone are reads, so every sub-agent and the planner's, the orchestrator's, and the analyzer's own calls list the component library; a sub-agent that is not read-only, a story's task sub-agent among them, runs `components serve`, with or without `--story`, so that it can run a pass of the harness it wrote. The sub-agent rules are `subAgent(readOnly)`, which `asSubAgent` builds per call from `Guard.ReadOnly`. A read-only sub-agent is refused `serve`, since it writes the harness's Vite config under `.flai-cache` and with `--story` may install the worktree's dependencies, which [ADR-0141](../adrs/0141-flai-guard-refuses-a-read-only-sub-agent-a-shell-command-that-writes-a-file.md) forbids it (`servesComponents`, which says to ask the story's agent, or a task sub-agent, to run it). `components config`, which rewrites `system-flow.yaml`, is refused to every sub-agent (`configsManifest`). The strategic agents' own rules refuse `serve` and `config`, as they refuse any command they do not list. |
| `flai release <id> [--dry-run] [--deliver] [--apply]` | Compute the semver release for one item per the git convention (delivered component gets the delivery-type bump, touched components a patch; the delivered component is `--deliver`, else the one the item's tags and then its epic's tags name among the components its commits touched, the most touched files winning and the earlier tag breaking a tie, else the only touched one; a tag naming an untouched component never delivers, S-0047) and with `--apply` create the tags and version bumps. A `research` or `experiment` story plans no release ([ADR-0025](../adrs/0025-research-is-accepted-without-a-release.md), [ADR-0066](../adrs/0066-an-experiment-story-is-accepted-like-any-other-and-records-its-results-in-a.md)): `skipped` gives the reason, naming the nature, and `unreleased` lists `{ component, files }` for each component its commits touched. |
| `flai release --pending [--dry-run]` (S-0087, [ADR-0032](../adrs/0032-accepting-a-story-merges-it-publishing-is-a-deliberate-batched-step-over.md)) | Publish everything accumulated. Since S-0195 ([ADR-0067](../adrs/0067-accepted-work-reaches-the-remote-only-when-it-is-published-and-agents-publish.md)) publishing, `git fetch` then this or the board's Publish (`publish.run`), is the one way accepted work reaches the remote, and agents publish only when the operator asks. `release.Pending` finds, per component, every accepted item since that component's last tag (or ever, with none) and takes the highest delivery level among them, one bump for the batch, not one per item; a component with nothing since its last tag is left out. Applies (version files, one changelog entry naming every item the batch bundles), commits, tags — skipping a tag that already exists, from a prior run that tagged but failed to push — then pushes the branch and every tag together, three tags to a push (`pending.Batches`, I-0026). Resumable: rerun after a partial failure finishes what is left, without redoing what already tagged or pushed. `--dry-run` prints every pending plan and changes nothing. Before anything is applied it asks the remote the branch tracks, else `origin`, for its release tags (S-0174, `release.CheckRemote`: one `git ls-remote --tags --refs`, waited on for 15 seconds, its answer kept a minute per root so the board's preview does not ask the network on every change; flai never fetches). A code component whose highest `<name>/vX.Y.Z` there is newer than the highest here refuses the publish with exit 3 (`conflict: refusing to publish`) and `git fetch --tags <remote>` as the fix, since a plan built on the stale tag would tag versions already published; so does a remote that cannot be asked, since the push needs it. The same `ls-remote` also asks for the head of the branch this clone tracks (S-0195, ADR-0067): when that commit is not in this clone's history, the publish is refused the same way, before anything is planned, committed, or tagged, since its push would be refused after tagging; the fix is `git fetch <remote> && git merge <remote>/<branch>`, or `git merge <remote>/<branch>` when the commit is already fetched, and the message adds that rebasing onto it does instead of merging. flai still never fetches. Each batch is pushed `--atomic` (S-0242), and when one is refused and `release.CheckRemoteAfresh` finds the branch moved, `remoteMovedUnderPush` deletes the release tags of that batch and every later one, keeps and names those already sent, and exits 3 with `remote_moved`, `deleted_tags`, and `kept_tags`; recovery is in [pushing-from-the-board.md](pushing-from-the-board.md). A clone with no remote is not checked, and a template is not compared (its version is in `template.yaml`). `--dry-run` (and `publish.preview`) then offers no plan and carries `remote` (`behind` with each component's tag here and there, `branch` with `upstream`, `head`, and `fetched` when the remote branch has commits this clone lacks, `fix`, `message`); with the remote unreachable it keeps the plan and carries `remote.unchecked` as a warning. Accepted items no plan covers (`release.PendingBatch`, I-0024: an item no longer there, one with no release rule, or one touching two components with no tag saying which it delivers to) are no longer skipped silently: `--dry-run` lists them (`left out:`, `unplanned` in JSON), publishing warns of each, and `release.PendingIDs` counts them unpublished so the board keeps them in view. |
| `flai release --evaluate [--json]` (S-0217) | Says whether `system-flow.yaml`'s `orchestration.release` policy is met, why in one sentence, and the figures it rests on (`release.Evaluate`), as the orchestrator's arithmetic (see `flai order --by`). It weighs the stories accepted and not yet released: those `release.PendingBatch` finds since each component's last tag, unplanned ones included, as `flai release --pending --dry-run` would publish them; their count, and their cost of delay `value` per week summed in `planning.currency`. `threshold` is met when that sum is at or over the manifest's `value`, or that count at or over its `count`; a story with no value counts toward the count, adds nothing to the sum, and is named in `unvalued`. With nothing pending, or with neither figure set, it is not met. `theme` is met when every story of its `epic`, or else its `tag`, archived or not and cancelled ones aside, is accepted and at least one is not yet released; the ones not yet accepted are named in `not_accepted`, and a theme with no stories, or every one already released, is not met. `judgement`, the default, is never met by itself: whether to release is the orchestrator's call or the operator's. A value that is not a policy is never met. It tags, bumps, commits, and pushes nothing, so it refuses an item ID and `--apply`, `--pending`, `--dry-run`, and `--deliver`; it needs `git`, since what is released is read from tags. With `orchestration.release.whole_epics` set (S-0222), no policy is met while a pending story belongs to an epic in neither `review` nor `done`; each such story is in `held_by_epic` with its epic and the epic's status, empty when the epic is not found, and the text output lists them under `held by epic:`. A story with no epic is never held. `--json` prints `release.Evaluation`: `{ policy, met, reason, currency, value, count, value_threshold?, count_threshold?, epic?, tag?, pending: [{ id, title, status, value?, accepted, released }], unvalued?, theme?, not_accepted?, held_by_epic?: [{ id, title, epic, epic_status }] }`. The hostapi read `release.evaluate` (`{}`) and the MCP tool `release_evaluate` (`{}`) answer with the same JSON; the tool says to run the command on the host when its server reads no git history. `flai guard` passes `release --evaluate` to a sub-agent and the planner as a read. |
| `flai accept <id> [--by] [--trailer] [--verified <commit>] [--evidence <file>] [--batch <id>] [--keep-removal <path> --reason "<why>"]...` | The operator's acceptance in one step, and nothing more since S-0087: preflight, before anything changes (committer identity; the workflow's rules for done, open tasks and unticked criteria, checked on a copy since S-0041; the story worktree readable by git, and nothing uncommitted in it, listed as `worktree_uncommitted` since S-0140; an experiment story without its results document `design/experiments/<S-nnnn>-*.md` on its branch, or in the main checkout when it has none, refused with the document it expects named, [ADR-0066](../adrs/0066-an-experiment-story-is-accepted-like-any-other-and-records-its-results-in-a.md); `internal/experiment` holds the folder and naming rules), rebase the story branch through `flai stream sync`'s `syncStoryBranch`, which regenerates `design/issues/summary.md` when a rebase stops on it alone ([ADR-0098](../adrs/0098-flai-stream-sync-and-flai-accept-regenerate-design-issues-summary-md-when-a.md)), and merges an issue file both sides changed and folds an open issue the branch added into the main branch's open issue of the same title ([ADR-0126](../adrs/0126-a-rebase-of-a-story-branch-merges-an-issue-file-both-sides-changed-by-its.md)), refuse it, merging nothing, when any file it adds or changes against the main branch carries a merge conflict marker as `internal/conflictmark` reads one, the same lines `markdown.conflict-marker` reports, naming each `path:line` and saying to resolve the conflict in the worktree, commit, and accept again (every file, not only markdown, with binary files skipped; S-0253, I-0066). One check finds them for the acceptance and its preview, so the two cannot disagree (S-0276): `conflictmark.Branch` reads the files the branch adds or changes against the main branch, and `preview.ConflictMarkers` words the refusal. The preflight reports it as a blocker, so `--dry-run` and the dashboard's Accept dialog name each marker before Accept is pressed, and `refuseConflictMarkers` in `cmd/branch.go` runs it again after the sync and before the fast-forward, on the branch as it will be merged. Then fast-forward it into main and remove its worktree and its branch, the branch with `git branch -D`, since a branch flai serve pushed tracks an upstream the rebase left behind, which `git branch -d` would refuse after main already holds the work; an acceptance stopped after its fast-forward finishes when run again, which fast-forwards nothing (S-0493, I-0204). Then move to done, archive, resolve the threads left on what it archived, commit, and then tell every open story whose claim covers a path the merge changed which paths those are (S-0132): `overlapsOf` in `cmd/accept_overlap.go` judges them with `workitem.Holds.Claim` and `PathsOverlap` (an empty claim covers every path), `itemedit.RecordOverlap` appends them to `.flai-cache/overlaps.jsonl`, the command prints `told <id> it overlaps: <paths>`, and `--json` returns them as `overlaps`. The MCP server reports them as `overlapped` changes. Since S-0277 ([ADR-0109](../adrs/0109-archiving-an-item-resolves-the-threads-still-open-on-it.md), I-0073) the archive step resolves every thread still `open` or `answered` anchored on what it archived, the story and its tasks, and an epic that followed it to done with its cancelled stories and their tasks (`preview.ThreadItems`), through `threads.ResolveOnItems` (`internal/threads/archived.go`): each gets the entry `Resolved: <id> was accepted` by `--by`, else the author, and a narrative still live is mirrored again. The thread files go into the acceptance commit, the command prints `resolved TH-nnnn, open or answered on what it archived`, and `--json` lists them in `resolved_threads`. The preview names them (`threadsToResolve`): `--dry-run` prints `would resolve TH-nnnn, open or answered on what it archives`, and a thread it cannot read is a blocker. Since S-0330 ([ADR-0120](../adrs/0120-agents-of-two-open-stories-message-each-other-in-conversations-kept-under-wip.md)) the same step closes every conversation under `wip/messages` still stored `open` that names a story it archived (`closeAccepted`, `messages.CloseOn`): the accepted story's with the entry `Closed: <id> was accepted`, and those of an epic's cancelled stories archived with it with `Closed: <id> was archived`, by `--by`, else the author. The files go into the acceptance commit, the command prints `closed MS-nnnn, open with S-nnnn`, naming the other story, and `--json` lists them in `closed_messages`. The preview names them (`messagesToClose`): `--dry-run` prints `would close MS-nnnn, open with S-nnnn`, and a conversation it cannot read is a blocker; one that cannot be closed after the archive stops the acceptance before the commit, as a thread does. A thread that cannot be resolved after the archive stops the acceptance before the commit (`resolveAccepted`), adding `<branch> is merged and <id> is done and archived, but not committed; once the thread above is fixed, run flai accept <id> to resolve the threads left and commit`. No tag, no version bump, no push: publishing what has accumulated is `flai release --pending`, a deliberate step of its own. Completes a story that is done but unarchived. The commit step's `git add -A` and `git commit` run through `storygit.RunPastIndexLock` (`internal/storygit/indexlock.go`; S-0307, I-0100): while git says another process holds the index lock (`index.lock': File exists`), it runs the command again after 100ms, 200ms, 400ms, 800ms, 1.6s, and 2s three times, about nine seconds in all. It never removes the lock file, which may belong to a live process. When the lock is held throughout, it gives up with git's whole output and a line saying another git process held the index lock for as long as flai waited. A commit that fails even so (`commitAcceptance`, `notCommitted`) keeps git's whole output and adds `<branch> is merged and <id> is done and archived, and its changes are staged in the main checkout but not committed; once git's error above is resolved, run flai accept <id> to commit them` (`not yet staged` when `git add` failed; no branch clause when nothing was merged), and the command exits non-zero, so the dashboard and the host see the acceptance fail. A done, archived item whose own archived file is not committed is such an acceptance: `preview.AcceptWith` marks it `resumed` and `commit_only`, and `finishCommit` resumes it at the commit, with no merge, no transition, and no archive. It resolves the threads still open or answered on what the acceptance archived, those an older flai left among them, closes the conversations still open of the stories it archived, and commits what the main checkout holds under the usual subject, naming the epic when the epic's archived file is uncommitted too (`archivedEpic`), and prints `completed the acceptance of <id>: done and archived already, committed`. Its overlap notices take their paths from the story's commits (`storygit.Committed`), since the merge is behind it. `--dry-run` on it prints `would commit the acceptance of <id>, done and archived but not committed: <subject>` and changes nothing. A done, archived item whose file is committed is still refused as already done. `--dry-run --json` is the dashboard's preview (branch, blockers, a branch's conflict markers among them since S-0276, uncommitted). Since S-0286 ([ADR-0106](../adrs/0106-a-story-whose-branch-changes-a-path-claude-code-protects-is-accepted-by-the.md)) the preview reads the files the story branch changes against the main branch (`storygit.StoryDiff`, a renamed file's old path beside its new one) and lists those on a path Claude Code protects (`protected.Changed`) as `protected`, with `operator_only` the sentence `only the operator (<names>) accepts S-nnnn: its branch changes paths Claude Code protects`; `--dry-run` prints the sentence, a colon, and the files. The operator is the story's `owner` or the manifest's `owner`, each named once and joined with `or`, or, when neither is named, anyone but the orchestrator (`preview.isOperator`). An acceptance by anyone else carries the blocker `only the operator (<names>) accepts S-nnnn, not <by>: its branch changes paths Claude Code protects, <files>; ...`, so it is refused before anything is merged; a diff it cannot read is a blocker too. The dashboard previews and accepts as the manifest's `owner`, or `designer` when it names none (`hostapi.owner`), so its Accept of such a story goes through when the manifest names its owner, or when neither the manifest nor the story names one. When the story is its epic's last open one (S-0200, [workflow.md](workflow.md#an-epic-follows-its-stories)), acceptance walks the epic to done after the story and archives it with its cancelled stories in the same commit, subject `chore: [S-nnnn] accept and archive, with E-nnnn`; the dry run prints `would also move E-nnnn <title> from <state> to done, following S-nnnn, and archive it`, a walk or archive that would fail is a blocker before the merge (`internal/preview`), `--json` has `epic` (a `workitem.Followed`), and the progress log has an `epic` step between done and archived. Uncommitted changes under `wip/` are expected; anything else is refused unless `--yes` includes it in the acceptance commit. A dry run never refuses for them: it lists them in `uncommitted`, with or without `--yes`, so the choice is made before confirming (S-0051). `--by orchestrator` is the orchestrator's acceptance (S-0221, [ADR-0093](../adrs/0093-with-accept-reviews-on-the-orchestrator-accepts-a-story-in-review-through-flai.md)), with `--verified <commit>`, the commit its verifier passed, and `--evidence <file>`, `-` for standard input. `acceptance` in `cmd/accept.go` refuses, as `rule:` and before the preview: `--by orchestrator` while `orchestration.permissions.accept_reviews` is off, naming it (`Repo.OrchestratorPermits`); any other `--by`, or none, under `FLAI_ROLE=orchestrate`; `--verified` or `--evidence` with any other `--by`; a real run without `--evidence` (a dry run may leave it out); and evidence with a heading line (`readEvidence`) or that `preview.ParseEvidence` cannot read: it wants one `Verdict:` line and one `- <n>: <files>` item per criterion, the files being the item's backtick spans, else its comma-separated text. `preview.AcceptWith` with `AcceptOptions.Orchestrator` adds `orchestratorBlockers` (`internal/preview/orchestrator.go`), each a `preview.Blocker` `{code, message}` in `--json`'s `orchestrator_blockers` and its message in `blockers`: `not_story`; `unverified`, a `--verified` that is missing, names no commit, or is not the story branch's head; `criterion_unticked`; `outside_touches`, a path the branch changes under none of the story's claim, the `wip` folder, `design/issues`, and an experiment's results never counting; `thread_open`, on the story or one of its tasks, which stands beside the resolving, so that the orchestrator's acceptance resolves only the threads on an epic that follows the story and on its cancelled stories ([ADR-0109](../adrs/0109-archiving-an-item-resolves-the-threads-still-open-on-it.md)); since S-0467, `own_thread_open` in its place for a thread the orchestrator opened and alone wrote on (`threads.Thread.OrchestratorsOwn`), whose message says the orchestrator resolves it with a reason ([ADR-0189](../adrs/0189-the-orchestrator-s-acceptance-lands-on-a-dirty-main-as-the-merge-queue-s-does.md)); `dirty_main` (`dirtyMainBlocker`), since S-0467, when the story's change, `main...story/S-nnnn`, would overwrite a file the main checkout has not committed outside `wip/`, or an untracked one, naming the files and the command that sets them aside (`mergequeue.FreeCommand`); `criterion_unevidenced`, a criterion the evidence has no item for or whose item names no changed file; and, since S-0286, `protected_paths`, a branch that changes a path Claude Code protects, naming the files and the operator, since only the operator accepts it whatever the orchestrator judges ([ADR-0106](../adrs/0106-a-story-whose-branch-changes-a-path-claude-code-protects-is-accepted-by-the.md)). A story done but never archived, or done and archived with its acceptance commit missing, it finishes as the operator's resume does, with no evidence, none of the orchestrator blockers above, which held when it moved to done, and no refusal while the merge queue batches, only when its last move to done was the orchestrator's (`Acceptance.DoneBy`); one anyone else made is refused, naming who, as the operator's to complete (S-0467, [ADR-0189](../adrs/0189-the-orchestrator-s-acceptance-lands-on-a-dirty-main-as-the-merge-queue-s-does.md)). On success the done transition is `by: orchestrator`, and `orchestratorNotes` appends `### Accepted by the orchestrator`, with `- Verified: <commit>`, `- At: <time>`, and the evidence as given, to the end of the story's `## Notes` before it is saved and archived, so the acceptance commit holds it. The text adds `accepted by the orchestrator at <commit>; its evidence is under ### Accepted by the orchestrator in the Notes of <id>`. `--json` carries `by` on every acceptance, and for the orchestrator's `verified`, the full commit, and `evidence` (`verdict`, `criteria` as `{n, files}`, `text`). The orchestrator's steps are in [strategic-agents.md](strategic-agents.md#accepting-a-story-s-0221). Since S-0337 a rebase of the acceptance that stops on conflicts appends an `accept` line to the conflict log before the refusal (`logAcceptConflict`), which `flai stats` counts under `coordination` ([ADR-0138](../adrs/0138-flai-stats-reports-per-week-the-conversations-between-agents-and-how-they-ended.md)). Since S-0467 ([ADR-0189](../adrs/0189-the-orchestrator-s-acceptance-lands-on-a-dirty-main-as-the-merge-queue-s-does.md)), when that stop is the orchestrator's acceptance's own, without `--batch`, `sendBackUnsettled` aborts it (`storygit.AbortRebase`, which checks the branch is back at its own head with nothing uncommitted), moves the story back to in-progress as `flai` with the paths in the reason, and opens one thread on it by `flai`, `storygit.SentBackTitle`'s `Acceptance sent back: <branch> stopped rebasing onto <base> on conflicts in <paths>`, or adds to the one still open. It exits saying the story was sent back; with `--json` it exits 4 and prints `{"refused": {"id", "reason", "sent_back", "branch", "base", "conflicts", "thread"}}`. `flai serve`'s `startAcceptSentBack` starts the story's agent on that thread, in its last run's session where it has one, and `resolveAcceptSentBack` resolves the thread once the story is back in review. A rebase found in progress, the operator's acceptance, and `--batch` leave the stop in the worktree. Since S-0367 ([ADR-0139](../adrs/0139-a-test-tier-s-fix-command-rewrites-the-files-a-story-changes-before-flai-task.md)) acceptance runs each test tier's `fix_command` twice. Its rebase of the story's branch, `storygit.Rebase`, fixes and commits the files the branch changes as `flai stream sync` does. In the main checkout, just before the commit step's `git add -A`, it runs `verify.CheckoutFix` over the paths it has not committed, the work items, narratives, threads, and conversations it wrote, so the acceptance commit holds them fixed. A fix command that fails is logged at warn and stops nothing. What the fix leaves refuses the acceptance, as the operator decided on TH-0413 ([ADR-0140](../adrs/0140-flai-accept-refuses-an-acceptance-whose-files-a-tier-with-a-fix-command-still.md)): before anything changes, accept fixes the main checkout's uncommitted files and runs `verify.CheckoutUnfixed`, the `command` of each tier that carries a `fix_command`, over them; after the branch's rebase and fix, before the merge, it runs it over the files the branch changes.
