A failure is a `rule:` refusal naming each finding as `path:line: message`, or the tier's last lines of output for a `plain` tier, and the story stays in review. With `--json` that refusal prints `{"refused": {"id", "reason", "findings"}}` and exits 4 (`acceptRefused` in `cmd/accept_markdown.go`; S-0378, [ADR-0145](../adrs/0145-a-dashboard-save-may-name-a-story-and-is-then-committed-on-that-story-s-branch.md)): each finding has `tier`, `checkout` (`main`, or `story` with `story`), `path`, `message`, and, when markdownlint gives them, `line`, `column`, `level`, `rule`, and `alias`. Without `--json` the text refusal and its exit 1 are unchanged. The host method `accept.run` maps exit 4 to Refused with `{id, reason, findings}` as the error's data, which the dashboard's review page opens file by file. `--dry-run` neither fixes nor checks. Since S-0371 ([ADR-0155](../adrs/0155-a-rebased-story-branch-that-undoes-main-s-change-to-a-path-is-recorded-at-sync.md), refining ADR-0093 and ADR-0098) a path the story's rebased branch undoes of main's change blocks the acceptance until it is restored, kept, or the story is sent back. The preview (`--dry-run`, and the host read `accept.preview`) reads the story's record with `storygit.RecordedUndone`, as `flai stream sync` writes it, and gives each path still undone as `undoes_main`, `{path, lines, old_base, kept_reason?}`, with main's lines the branch removes; each path not kept is a blocker (`preview.UndoneBlocker`) naming `flai stream restore S-nnnn --path <path>`, `--keep-removal`, and sending the story back to in-progress. `flai accept` refuses on it before anything is merged; with `--json` it exits 4 and prints `{"refused": {"id", "reason", "undoes_main"}}` (`undoneRefusal`). Its own rebase runs the check too (`mergeStoryBranch` in `cmd/branch.go`, from `syncStoryBranch`), and records what it finds, so a path only that rebase finds is refused the same way, merging nothing. `--keep-removal <path>` (repeatable) with `--reason "<why>"`, one per keep in order or one for all, keeps a path (`keptRemovals`): a keep of a path the record does not hold (`keptHeld`), one with no reason, an empty reason, a reason on more than one line, a path named twice, and any keep with `--by orchestrator` are refused as `rule:` before anything changes, since an undone path waits for the operator. Each kept path is written, with who kept it, when, and why, under `### Removals kept` at the end of the story's `## Notes` (`keptNotes`), in the acceptance commit; `--dry-run` prints `would keep the removal of the main branch's lines at <path>: <reason>`. `flai move S-nnnn done` shares `--keep-removal`, its own `--reason` being the one reason for every keep. The host write `accept.run` takes `keep_removals` and passes a refusal through as Refused with `undoes_main` as data. Since S-0395 ([ADR-0169](../adrs/0169-under-pull-request-acceptance-every-acceptance-the-operator-s-and-the.md)), under `acceptance.mode: pull-request`, the preview adds the forge blockers, `forge_blockers` and `pull_request` in `--json`, whoever accepts, and the real run refuses on them before anything is merged ([The acceptance gate](#the-acceptance-gate)). Since S-0397 ([ADR-0170](../adrs/0170-under-pull-request-acceptance-flai-accept-merges-the-story-s-pull-request-on.md)), under `pull-request` the rebase and fast-forward give way to the merge of the story's pull request on the forge, and the steps after it are as under `local` ([The merge through the remote](#the-merge-through-the-remote)). Since S-0399 ([ADR-0173](../adrs/0173-a-merge-queue-in-flai-serve-with-no-model-runs-behind-the-merge-queue-host.md)), accepting a story takes it out of the merge queue and drops the batch that holds it ([the `flai queue` writers](#commands)). Since S-0400 ([ADR-0176](../adrs/0176-the-merge-queue-lands-each-story-of-a-passing-batch-through-flai-accept-batch.md)), `--batch <id>` is the merge queue's landing, which `flai serve` runs for each story of a batch it lands ([The merge queue pass](#the-merge-queue-pass)). `acceptance` refuses it as `rule:` under `acceptance.mode: pull-request` and beside `--verified`; with `--by orchestrator` it needs no `--evidence`. `preview.AcceptWith` with `AcceptOptions.Batch` asks `mergequeue.Landable` (`internal/mergequeue/land.go`) whether the story may land now, and sets `verified` to the story's rebased head in the batch. Each reason it gives is a blocker, for the orchestrator an `unverified` one in place of `--verified`'s check: the queue holds no batch, or another; the batch is dropped; it is not verified, failed and not bisected, or its landing has not begun; the story is not among those it lands, has landed, is held back (S-0402), or is not the next; the story's branch is not at the head the batch rebased a copy of; or the main branch is not at the landing's `head`. On success `leaveQueue` calls `mergequeue.Landed`, which takes the story out of the queue with the outcome `landed` and adds it to the landing's `landed`, keeping the batch. `orchestratorNotes` adds `- Batch: <id>` after `- Verified:`. The text adds `landed from merge queue batch <id>, verified at <commit>`, and `--json` carries `batch`. Since S-0403, while the merge queue batches for the project (the `merge-queue` host action on, `accept_reviews` on, local acceptance), `acceptance` refuses `--by orchestrator` as `rule:`, but with `--batch` or `--dry-run`, naming `flai queue add <id> --by orchestrator --verified <commit> --evidence <file>` and `queue_add`: the orchestrator enqueues the story instead. Under `FLAI_ROLE=orchestrate` it refuses `--batch` outright, since the orchestrator never lands a story by hand. The landing of a story the orchestrator enqueued, given no `--evidence`, takes the evidence recorded with the enqueue (`enqueuedEvidence`), and `orchestratorNotes` adds `- Enqueued verified: <commit>` after `- Batch:`; a story enqueued without evidence lands on the batch's verification alone. Since S-0402 ([ADR-0178](../adrs/0178-the-merge-queue-lands-each-story-in-the-main-checkout-with-git-s-own-check.md)) `--batch` does not refuse the main checkout's uncommitted files outside `wip/`: it reads them before anything changes and leaves them as they are, out of the lint fix over the checkout's uncommitted files and out of the acceptance commit, which holds only the acceptance; its `--dry-run` says `the merge queue's landing leaves them as they are, out of the acceptance commit (ADR-0178)`. `git merge --ff-only` moves main and the checkout's files together, so the checkout is never left behind main. Since S-0467 ([ADR-0189](../adrs/0189-the-orchestrator-s-acceptance-lands-on-a-dirty-main-as-the-merge-queue-s-does.md)) `--by orchestrator` leaves them the same way (`leftUncommitted`), held only by `dirty_main`, and its `--dry-run` says `the orchestrator's acceptance leaves them as they are, out of the acceptance commit, and is refused only while its change would overwrite one (ADR-0189)`. By hand the refusal and `--yes` stand. Since S-0483 ([ADR-0185](../adrs/0185-flai-accept-runs-a-sub-project-s-install-command-in-the-main-checkout-when-the.md)), after the commit step, `flai accept` runs the `install.command` of each sub-project whose `install.paths` match a path the acceptance brought into the main branch, the paths its overlap notices read, in the sub-project's folder in the main checkout (`installAccepted` in `cmd/accept_install.go`). A resumed acceptance uses the story's committed paths. Each command runs once, in sub-project order, with its output on standard error, from the manifest the main checkout holds after the commit, so an `install` the accepted change adds counts. One that succeeds is printed as `installed <sub-project>: <command>`. One that fails or cannot start is logged at warn and printed as `install for <sub-project> failed: <reason>; run <command> in <folder>`, and the acceptance still succeeds. `--json` lists each run in `installs` (`project`, `command`, `ok`, `error`). `--dry-run` runs none. |
| `flai issue new "<title>" --class <c> [--cost] [--note] [--story] [--revenue-per-week] [--penalty-per-week] [--time-lost-per-cycle] [--evidence] [--report] [--commit \| --autocommit] [--trailer]...`, `flai issue bump <id> [--cost] [--note] [--story] [--revenue-per-week] [--penalty-per-week] [--time-lost-per-cycle] [--evidence] [--report] [--commit \| --autocommit] [--trailer]...`, `flai issue close <id> --reason [--commit \| --autocommit] [--trailer]...`, `flai issue list [--all] [--story]`, `flai issue story <id> [--epic] [--story] [--owner] [--autocommit] [--trailer]`, `flai issue summary` | Record, increment, and close recurring friction in `design/issues`; every command that writes an issue regenerates `summary.md` (average and total cost, most expensive first). Each instance names its story: `--story`, else `FLAI_STORY`, else `FLAI_AGENT` of the form `agent-S-nnnn`, else the checked-out `story/S-nnnn` branch, else none. `list --story` filters by it; `list --json` adds `stories` and `story`, the open story that links the issue. Since S-0224 `new` and `bump` take a finding's impact and the report that found it, for the analyzer ([strategic-agents.md](strategic-agents.md#how-the-analyzer-files-its-findings-s-0224)): `--revenue-per-week` and `--penalty-per-week`, amounts of zero or more, `--time-lost-per-cycle`, a duration longer than zero, and `--evidence` write the issue's `## Impact` section, the evidence then one `- key: value` line per figure (`issues.Impact`, `setImpact`), a bump replacing the figures it gives and keeping the others; `--report`, a markdown file under `design/analysis/` other than its README, from the project root (`reportPath`), puts `Report: <path>.` in the instance and `Found by the analysis in [<file>](../analysis/<file>).` in `## Remediation`, once (`linkReport`). A figure or report path that does not parse is refused before anything is written. With `--report`, `new` runs `issues.NewOrBump`: it bumps the open issue of exactly that title rather than open a second, or leaves it as it is when an instance already names the report, and says which; `--json` adds `outcome`, `opened`, `bumped`, or `already recorded`. `issue story` makes a backlog remediation or improvement story from an open issue through the same checked creation as `story new --body-stdin`; the story's body links the issue. It refuses a closed issue or one an open story links. The story is a draft carrying the cost of delay inputs `issues.ForStory` takes from the issue's cost, count, and `## Impact`; since S-0224 its goal links each analysis report the issue names (`issues.Reports`: its instances' `Report:` lines, then its Remediation's report links), its Notes say the analyzer found it, and the report links are left out of the solution it carries. The `Then` it gives `itemnew.Options` names the story in the issue's `## Remediation` with `issues.LinkStory` and returns the issue's path to commit with it, or none when `--story` read the issue from another worktree (S-0203, [continuous-improvement.md](continuous-improvement.md#cadence)). `--owner`, `--autocommit`, and `--trailer` are `story new`'s, passed to `itemnew.Options` the same way, so the dashboard's `issue.story` makes it as `item.new` makes any item (S-0198); `--json` adds `draft`, `cost_of_delay`, `committed`, `commit`, and `commit_error` from `itemnew.Result`. Issues live under `Root` and are committed on the story's branch, so `--story` on `list` and `story` reads them from that story's worktree when it has one; work items are read from the main checkout either way. `issue new` numbers one past the highest issue in the checkout it runs in, the main checkout, every linked worktree (uncommitted files included), the main branch, and every local `story/*` branch, which `storygit.FolderNames` lists for the issues folder, so stories worked in parallel do not take the same number (S-0252, I-0065); a worktree or branch git cannot read is skipped. Since S-0275 `new`, `bump`, and `close` take `--commit`, which makes recording from a story's worktree one call (`cmd/storycommit.go`). The command writes as without it, then commits exactly what it wrote, the issue's file and `summary.md`, on `story/S-nnnn` as `docs: [S-nnnn] record\|bump\|close I-nnnn <title>` with each `--trailer` line (`storygit.CommitPaths`), leaving everything else in the worktree as it was. It then widens the story's touches in the main checkout to the files committed (`itemedit.WidenStory`), as `flai touches --add` would. The story resolves as an instance's does; `close` has no `--story`. `beginStoryCommit` refuses before anything is written when no story resolves, when the checkout is not on the story's branch, when a rebase is unfinished, and when `--autocommit` is given too. The text output adds `commit: <hash> <subject>` and `touches: added to S-nnnn: ...`; `--json` adds `commit` (`{hash, subject, paths}`, null when nothing changed) and `touches_added`. A `new --report` that bumps commits as `bump`, and one already recorded commits nothing. `--autocommit` is the main checkout's: it commits the issue's file and `summary.md` on their own where the command ran, as `docs: record\|bump\|close I-nnnn <title>` with each `--trailer`, unless `dashboard.autocommit` is false, and widens no story's touches. A failed commit is reported, not failed, and `--json` adds `committed`, `commit`, and `commit_error`, as `flai adr new --autocommit` does. The dashboard's `issue.new`, `issue.bump`, and `issue.close` run it (see `flai hostapi`). |
| `flai template show`, `flai template update`, `flai template use <repo> [--ref]` | Inspect, clone again, and switch the template source. A clone at a branch is fetched again on every use, so `update` is needed only for a broken clone, which the error that meets one names (see the cache above). |
| `flai template push [dir] [--remote] [--ref] [--tag] [--dry-run] [--force]` | Publish a locally developed template to its git remote: clone the branch, replace contents, commit with the version, push, and with `--tag` push `v<version>` (refused if it exists). Defaults from `publish` in `template.yaml`. Git errors surface verbatim; no retries; no force push unless `--force`. `flai release --pending` and `flai push --pending --publish` call it for template components (S-0087). |
| `flai config get [key]`, `flai config set <key> <value>`, `flai config path` | Read and edit `~/.flai/config.json` by dotted key; `path` prints the resolved file without creating it. Keys: `template.*`, `dashboard.*`, `cache_dir`, `author`, and `worktrees.relative_paths` (boolean, default false, ADR-0022). |
| `flai reference [--write <file> [--settings <page>]]` (hidden, S-0017, S-0028) | Render every command `help` lists (not hidden or deprecated) as one markdown page: usage, description, aliases, examples, local flags, and flags inherited from a parent other than the root, the root's global flags once at the top. Written with the standard library, not `cobra/doc`. `--write` replaces the file only when the body below the front matter changed, so `updated` moves only then. `make flai-reference` (`scripts/flai-reference.sh`, `go run`, leaving `bin/flai` alone) writes [docs/users/flai-reference.md](../../docs/users/flai-reference.md), and `TestReferenceIsCurrent` (integration tier) fails when the committed page differs from the help. `--settings` also replaces, in [docs/operators/settings.md](../../docs/operators/settings.md), what is between its `flags:begin` and `flags:end` markers with a table of every flag and the commands that take it, linked to their sections of the reference, and moves that page's `updated` only when the table changed; the hand-written rest of the page is left alone, and `TestFlagIndexIsCurrent` fails when the committed table is stale. Help text is the source: fix a command's `Short`, `Long`, `Example`, or flag usage, then regenerate. |
| `flai version` | Version, commit, build date, Go version, and the release stamp (S-0235): a last line `release: signed`, `release: development build, no stamp`, `release: missing stamp`, or `release: invalid stamp: <reason>`. `--json` adds `release: {statement, signature, state, reason, development}`, `state` one of `signed`, `missing`, `invalid`, and `reason` only when invalid. `--json` also carries `task_done_rules`, the task-done rules number (S-0445, [Versions](#versions-the-hosts-flai-and-the-tree)). `buildinfo.OwnStamp` verifies `buildinfo.ReleaseStatement` and `ReleaseSignature`, which `flai/.goreleaser.yaml`'s `ldflags` set from `FLAI_RELEASE_STATEMENT` and `FLAI_RELEASE_SIGNATURE`, with the embedded release key, and checks that the statement is `flai <version> <commit>` for this build's `Version` and `Commit`. A development build (`buildinfo.Development`: `dev`, empty, or starting `0.0.0`) is `missing` with `development: true`. `flai serve` logs one `release stamp not valid` (warn, `component=serve`, `state`, `reason`) at start for a build that is neither development nor signed ([release-signing.md](release-signing.md#the-stamp-as-built-s-0235)). |
| `flai license` (S-0231) | Print the Bytepunx Shield License, the terms flai, flaiover, and the repository are distributed under; `--json` gives `{name, text}`. The text is embedded from `internal/license/LICENSE.md`, a copy of `LICENSE.md` at the repository root that `TestEmbeddedLicenseMatchesRepository` keeps byte for byte the same, because Go embeds nothing above its module; the release archives carry the same file. The dashboard's Host › License page shows the copy its image carries. |
| `flai host`, `host start [serve\|mcp\|all]`, `host stop [serve\|mcp\|all]`, `host restart <serve\|mcp\|all>`, `host status`, `host check`, `host versions`, `host upgrade [--version X.Y.Z]` (S-0106, [ADR-0040](../adrs/0040-one-flai-host-per-machine-runs-flai-serve-and-each-project-s-mcp-server-as-its.md); `versions` and `--version` S-0298, [ADR-0117](../adrs/0117-the-host-api-installs-a-release-the-dashboard-names-only-when-flai-lists-it-as.md)) | One process per machine that runs `flai serve` and each served project's `flai mcp http` as its children (`internal/host`, with `cmd/host.go`'s `hostLauncher` saying how to start each). It binds `127.0.0.1:4241` (`FLAI_HOST_ADDR`, `host.AddrEnv`) before anything else, which is what makes it one per machine; a second host is refused with the first one's `/_health` (pid, version, config, started: the one route without a token) as `AlreadyRunningError`. The control API on that address needs `Authorization: Bearer` with the token the host makes at each start, hands its children in `FLAI_HOST_URL` and `FLAI_HOST_TOKEN`, and writes to `host/token` (0600) beside the config; a request with an `Origin` is refused. Routes: `GET /status`, `POST /process {process, action}` (`serve`, `mcp`, `all`; `start`, `stop`, `restart`; a restart of a stopped one starts it; a stop keeps it stopped until a start), `PUT /mcp {roots}` (the projects whose MCP server to keep: missing ones started, others stopped), `GET /check` (`flai self-upgrade --check --json`), `GET /versions` (`flai self-upgrade --list --json`), `POST /upgrade` (`flai self-upgrade --json`, or with `{version}` `flai self-upgrade --version <version> --json`, which refuses a version that is not published; when it installed, the host answers, stops its children, and `Run` returns `ErrRestart`, on which `cmd` replaces the process with the new binary, `host.Reexec`: `syscall.Exec` on Unix, a new process on Windows). A child is started from the binary on disk, with `--config` and `--exit-with <host pid>`, its output appended to its log (`serve/serve.log`, the project's `.flai-cache/mcp-http.log`); one that ends unasked is started again after a backoff from 1 s doubling to 30 s, reset after a minute of running; when `Run` returns every child gets SIGTERM and after `hostGrace` (15 s) SIGKILL. A `flai serve` or MCP server already running that the host did not start is `external`: used, never stopped, looked at again every 15 s. MCP addresses are `mcpAddrFor`'s, with those given to other roots left out. State `host/state.json` (pid, version, started, updated, addr, config, children with name, root, state `running`\|`waiting`\|`external`\|`stopped`, pid, since, version, restarts, last exit, last error), rewritten every second and stale after ten; log `host/host.log`. `flai host` runs in the foreground; `start` detaches it in the working directory (which `flai serve` inherits, so a folder is served whole, S-0102) and waits for its state; `stop` signals it and waits; `status` asks the API, else reads the state, and names a host that runs for another config. Under the `serve` child, while `flai serve` runs, `status` prints `serveProjectLines`: by root, `<root>:` and the refusal of a project not connected (S-0237), then `<root>:` and its image line in `flai serve status`'s words (S-0238); `--json` adds `images`, the verdicts by root. |
| `flai serve`, `flai serve start`, `stop`, `status` (S-0072, ADR-0029) | flai's process on the host that dashboards are reached through: one per user, serving every project registered with it. For each project it opens a WebSocket to that project's dashboard (`/agent`), the two prove to each other that they hold the agent credential without sending it (HMAC over two nonces), and then speak JSON-RPC 2.0: the dashboard asks, flai answers from a table of named methods (`internal/channel`), checked against the project key on every request. The methods are `flai hostapi`'s, below. It also watches each project's design, docs, and wip folders and its manifest (`internal/watch`: a poll every 300 ms, a file reported once it has looked the same for one tick) and sends the dashboard a `change` notification with the repository-relative path; nothing is queued for a dashboard that is not connected. Pings every 4 seconds, reconnection with backoff and jitter from 250 ms to 4 s, or a minute after the dashboard refuses it with 4409 because another flai that answers holds the project ([ADR-0063](../adrs/0063-the-dashboard-refuses-a-second-flai-for-a-project-with-close-code-4409-while.md)), a 16 MiB cap on a message, cancellation by notification. Since S-0237 `hello` is protocol 2 and carries flai's release stamp, and flai checks the dashboard's before it sends `hello.prove` ([release-signing.md](release-signing.md#the-exchange-as-built-s-0237)). A close with 4403, from either side, waits the same minute as 4409 and logs one `error`, `connection refused as unsigned`, with `refused_by`, `reason`, and `peer_version`. The connection state keeps `refused_by` (`dashboard` or `flai`), `refused_reason`, `peer_version`, and `peer_signed`, and `cmd`'s `refusal` words them for `flai serve status` (every project list), `flai host status` (a line per refused root under the `serve` child), and `flai dashboard status` (the `host flai` line, and `refused` under `host_flai`): `the dashboard refused this flai: unsigned` or `flai refused the dashboard: unsigned`, then `(<reason>, dashboard <version>)` after a space, each part left out when not known. Since S-0238 every client `flai serve` dials, a served project's or a candidate connection's, asks a gate before every dial and every reconnection (`channel.Client.BeforeDial`): `serve.CheckImage` asks Docker which image the `flaiover` container runs and judges its `RepoDigests` against the cached verified digest lists, `listed`, `unlisted`, or `not-measured` ([release-signing.md](release-signing.md#flai-measures-the-container-before-it-dials)). An unlisted image is not dialled: the gate's error, `the dashboard runs an unsigned image <image ID> (<RepoDigests>): <reason>`, is the connection's `refused_reason` with `refused_by` `flai`, the client logs one `error`, `flai refused to dial the dashboard`, per distinct refusal, and waits the same minute before it looks again. A dashboard not measured is dialled, and the stamp alone admits or refuses it. Since S-0239, under `dashboard.allow_unsigned` an unlisted image is dialled and a dashboard with no stamp accepted, and the connection keeps its unsigned sides, which `flai serve status` and `flai host status` print ([Allowing unsigned builds](#allowing-unsigned-builds)). The state keeps each root's last verdict in `images` (`kind`, `container`, `image`, `version`, `repo_digests`, `digest`, `release`, `reason`); `cmd`'s `imageVerdictLine` words it under each project in `flai serve status`: `image: signed, flaiover <release>, <digest>`, `image: the dashboard runs an unsigned image <image ID> (<RepoDigests> or no RepoDigests); flai does not dial it`, or `image: not measured: <reason>; the release stamp alone is checked`; a refused project's line reads `not connected: flai refused the dashboard:` and the gate's error. Bare `flai serve` runs in the foreground; `start` runs it detached without root and waits for its first state; `stop` terminates it; `status` (and `--json`) says whether it runs, since when, and for each registered project the dashboard address and whether it is connected or why not. Registry (`projects.json`), state (`state.json`, rewritten every second, stale after ten), and log live in a `serve` folder beside flai's config file, so a repository with its own `FLAI_CONFIG`, and every test, has its own. A second `flai serve` refuses while the first is alive Since S-0098 it also offers the board, over candidate connections, the repositories under the folders named with `flai serve import` (see `flai serve import`, and the dashboard channel's candidates). Since S-0096 each served project's HTTP MCP server is kept running; since S-0106 `flai host` does it and `flai serve` starts no MCP process: `internal/serve/mcp.go`'s `mcpTeller` tells the host every served root (`MCP.Keep`, `cmd`'s `hostMCP` over the host's client) when the set changes and every 15 s, and nothing when `flai serve` stops, so a restart of serve leaves the servers running ([ADR-0040](../adrs/0040-one-flai-host-per-machine-runs-flai-serve-and-each-project-s-mcp-server-as-its.md), superseding ADR-0034). Outside a host (no `FLAI_HOST_URL`) it keeps none, and none under a host its config does not name (S-0183): when the `host/token` beside its config is not `FLAI_HOST_TOKEN`, as for a `flai serve` an agent runs with a `--config` of its own, it warns that the host in its environment is not the one its config names and keeps none, rather than stop the operator's servers. `flai serve start` starts the host, or asks the running one to start serve; `stop` asks the host to stop serve and terminates one the host did not start; a hidden `--exit-with` ends it when the host is gone. Since S-0102, started in a folder that is not a project (its working directory, which `flai serve start` passes on), it treats the folder as `flai dashboard` there does ([ADR-0036](../adrs/0036-a-folder-that-is-not-a-project-is-served-whole-by-flai-mcp-and-flai-dashboard.md), an added case). `Options.Folder` is looked in every `ScanEvery` for projects (`workitem.FindProjects`), which are served for the first dashboard, by address, that the registered projects or a recorded one reach, for as long as it runs and without being written to the registry. The folder is also an import root. `Status` carries `folder` and `folder_projects`, and `flai serve status` lists them. Since S-0120 ([ADR-0045](../adrs/0045-a-system-flow-repository-under-a-folder-named-for-import-is-served-whether-or.md), refining ADR-0035) the git repositories with a manifest below the import roots are served the same way (`serve.FindBelow` finds both kinds, `serve.Place` places them: a registered root is the registry's; a manifest that does not load, no key, a key a registered or earlier project has, or no dashboard known leaves one unserved with a reason). `Status` adds `import_projects` and `unserved` (root, key, reason); `flai serve status` prints them under `served from the folders named for import` and `not served`, and when `flai serve` does not run works them out itself, giving `flai serve is not running` for what it would serve (`unserved` in `--json`). |
| `flai serve enable\|disable <action> [--all-projects]`, `flai serve actions`, `flai serve journal [-n N]` (S-0078, ADR-0029), `flai serve orchestrate stop\|start` (S-0228), `flai serve components [show\|idle <duration>]` (S-0438) | Host actions: what `flai serve` does on the host as the operator because a dashboard asked. There are fifteen: `push`, `agent`, `dashboard` (S-0081), since S-0082 `checks`, since S-0105 `settings`, since S-0106 `host` ([ADR-0039](../adrs/0039-a-settings-host-action-turned-on-only-in-a-shell-lets-the-dashboard-change-the.md)), since S-0144 `auto-publish` ([ADR-0048](../adrs/0048-pushing-accepted-work-releases-nothing-unless-the-auto-publish-host-action-is-enabled.md)), and since S-0208 `plan` ([ADR-0082](../adrs/0082-flai-serve-starts-the-planner-for-an-epic-or-a-story-behind-the-plan-host.md)), which `flai plan`, the MCP tool `plan`, and the write `plan.run` read, since S-0257 `auto-approve` ([ADR-0086](../adrs/0086-flai-serve-gives-a-claude-code-agent-flai-s-permission-prompt-as-its-permission.md)), since S-0218 `orchestrate` ([ADR-0087](../adrs/0087-flai-serve-runs-one-orchestrator-per-project-behind-the-orchestrate-host-action.md)), since S-0387 `auto-upgrade` ([ADR-0158](../adrs/0158-flai-serve-checks-each-served-project-for-a-newer-flai-release-every-minute.md)), since S-0438 `components` ([ADR-0163](../adrs/0163-flai-runs-a-sub-project-s-own-vite-dev-server-on-127-0-0-1-behind-the.md)), since S-0393 `forge` ([ADR-0166](../adrs/0166-under-pull-request-acceptance-flai-serve-pushes-a-story-s-branch-and-opens-or.md)), since S-0399 `merge-queue` ([ADR-0173](../adrs/0173-a-merge-queue-in-flai-serve-with-no-model-runs-behind-the-merge-queue-host.md)), which `flai serve`'s merge queue pass reads ([The merge queue pass](#the-merge-queue-pass)) and which gates the writes `queue.add`, `queue.remove`, and `queue.hold`, the dashboard seeing it and `settings.action` turning it on and off, and since S-0223 `analyze` ([ADR-0099](../adrs/0099-the-analyzer-runs-behind-the-analyze-host-action-and-writes-one-report-under.md)), which `flai analyze`, the MCP tool `analyze`, the write `analyze.run`, and `flai serve`'s analysis scheduler read (`AgentConfig.Analyze`); the dashboard sees it and `settings.action` turns it on and off, and [strategic-agents.md](strategic-agents.md#the-analyzer) has what it starts. `curate` ([ADR-0196](../adrs/0196-the-curator-runs-behind-the-curate-host-action-and-takes-in-what-the-archive.md)), which `flai curate`, the MCP tool `curate`, the write `curate.run`, and since S-0432 `flai serve`'s curate scheduler read (`AgentConfig.Curate`); the dashboard sees it and `settings.action` turns it on and off, and [strategic-agents.md](strategic-agents.md#the-curator) has what it starts. `components` gates `components.start` and `components.stop`, whose specs run no flai command: their `do` answers in `flai serve`'s own process through `hostapi.Host.Components`, which `serveHost` wires to a `serve.Components` that supervises one dev server per project and sub-project and stops each one idle, and every one as `flai serve` stops ([component-library.md § Under flai serve](component-library.md#under-flai-serve), [dashboard-host-channel.md](dashboard-host-channel.md#decision)). `flai serve components idle <duration>` writes `components.idle` (`config.ComponentsConfig.Idle`) to the host configuration, refusing what is not a positive Go duration; the supervisor reads it at every look, 30 minutes when it is unset or not positive. `flai serve components show`, and `flai serve components` alone, print it, `(default)` when unset, and whether the action is on for the project in the working directory; `--json` is `{idle, idle_seconds, idle_set, enabled_here}`. `orchestrate` gates two writes, since S-0228 (below); otherwise `flai serve` reads it (`AgentConfig.Orchestrate`) at every look of a project's launcher, every minute and on each change, and runs one orchestrator per project while it is on. The dashboard sees it and `settings.action` turns it on and off. Its `orchestrator` (`internal/serve/orchestrate.go`) starts the run when none is going, through the launcher's `orchestrate` with `Manifest.OrchestrationAgent` (`orchestration.agent` over `agent`, `Agent.With`) and a `harness.Request` with `Role` `orchestrate` (`conventions.RoleOrchestrate`) and neither `Item` nor `Story`, named `orchestrator`, in the main checkout, with `FLAI_ROLE=orchestrate` from `roleEnv`; again when a run ends, but no sooner than `orchestrateRetry` (a minute) after one that `failed`; and stops a live run when the action is off, as `Stop` stops a story's agent (marked `Stopped` first, `TerminateGroup`, `KillGroup` after the grace). A start refused before it spawns (no harness and no command; the harness's refusal, such as `claude-code` with no `.claude/agents/orchestrator.md`, naming `flai upgrade`) is recorded as a failed run and journalled once: the launcher keeps the last refusal and does not record the same one again. The run is `AgentState.Orchestrator` (`orchestrator` in `serve/agents.json`, the newest, with up to 20 earlier runs in `PastOrchestrators`, `past_orchestrators`, since S-0228, as the analyzer's are in `past_analyzers`; `AgentRun.orchestrates` when it has neither story nor item), never in `Running` or `Last`, counted by no limit; its log is `serve/agents/<key>-orchestrator-<start>.log`. `orchestrateEnded` settles it, from its wait or `settleOrphans`: `stopped` when it was stopped, `failed` on a non-zero exit, `worked` otherwise, and logs the activity since the last entry in `wip/agents/orchestrator.md` with `LogRunEnd`, whose summary is the run's final text, or `stopped: orchestrate turned off`, and which names no item, so that it charges no item and its cost is the orchestrator's project strategic total ([ADR-0095](../adrs/0095-an-orchestrator-activity-s-usage-is-charged-evenly-to-the-work-items-it-named.md)); an activity the orchestrator logs with `activity_log`, or its `thread_reply`'s own entry, is charged to the work items it names. Starts, ends, failures, and stops are journal entries with action `orchestrate` and method `serve.orchestrate`. `agent.status` carries the run as `orchestrator`; `agent.stream` takes `role: orchestrate` in place of `story` or `plan`, or `orchestrator: true` as before S-0228, and `role: analyze` for the analyzer's (`Host.RoleStream`, `serve.RoleStream`); `launcher.changed` notifies the dashboard with `agent` and `{project, role: orchestrate}`. Since S-0228 the operator may hold it stopped (`internal/serve/orchestrate_hold.go`): `flai serve orchestrate stop` (`OrchestratorStop`) sets `Held` on its run, marks a live run `Stopped`, ends it as `Stop` ends a story's agent, and waits up to two seconds for the serving flai to settle it before settling it itself; `orchestrateEnded` logs the end as `stopped: stopped from the dashboard` (`orchestrateHeld`), and `launcher.ended` keeps `Held` on the run that ended. While the run is held, a look starts nothing; turning the action off lifts the hold (`orchestrator.unhold`), so that turned on again it starts. `flai serve orchestrate start` (`OrchestratorStart`) starts it at once through the launcher's `orchestrate`, handed over to the serving flai, and recording the new run lifts the hold, as does a start that fails. Each is a `*Refused` while the action is off; the stop when flai serve has started no orchestrator or it is already held; the start when it is not held, or its run is still being stopped. Both are journalled under `orchestrate`, and are the writes `orchestrate.stop` and `orchestrate.start` (`orchestrateNow`, `describeOrchestrate`). The look tells the dashboard of a run a process of its own started or held (`orchestrator.notice`), since that process tells none. `claude-code` names the session `<key> orchestrate`, adds `.claude/agents/orchestrator.md` to `--agents` (`claudeCodeStrategic`), and selects it with `--agent orchestrator`; `command` gets `FLAI_ROLE` and an empty `{story}`. `harness.Prompt` sends `orchestratePrompt`. See [strategic-agents.md](strategic-agents.md#the-orchestrator). `forge` gates no method either: `flai serve`'s forge pass reads it at every look, once a minute, and under `acceptance.mode: pull-request` pushes each story in review and opens or updates its pull request ([Reaching the forge](#reaching-the-forge)); the dashboard sees it and `settings.action` turns it on and off, and its pushes, requests, and first failures are journal entries with action `forge` and method `serve.forge`. `auto-publish` gates no method: `flai push --pending` reads it, wherever it runs, to decide whether a push first tags a release. Since S-0195 ([ADR-0067](../adrs/0067-accepted-work-reaches-the-remote-only-when-it-is-published-and-agents-publish.md)) it is shell-only (`hostapi.DashboardSees` is false for it): `flai serve actions` lists it and `flai serve enable`/`disable` change it, with a description of what it has flai do rather than what it lets a dashboard do, but `project.info`'s `host_actions` and `settings.get` leave it out and `settings.action` refuses it, naming the shell command. The `push` action now means letting a dashboard publish. `auto-approve` gates no method either and is shell-only in the same way (`hostapi.shellOnly`): the stdio `flai mcp` reads it at every `permission_prompt` request (`app.mcpAutoApprove`, so a change applies to agents already running; a configuration it cannot read counts as off), and with it on the tool allows a story agent's write to a path Claude Code protects in its worktree, `.git` excepted, without a thread, logging each: since S-0286 ([ADR-0106](../adrs/0106-a-story-whose-branch-changes-a-path-claude-code-protects-is-accepted-by-the.md)) a `.claude/` file, `.mcp.json`, and the rest of `internal/protected`'s list, `.claude/` alone before. Such a write reaches no running agent until the story is merged, and the story is then accepted by the operator alone (see `flai accept`), who reads the change in its diff. Since S-0299 `flai guard` reads it too (`app.guardAutoApprove`): with it on, a story's sub-agents may make those writes, which the guard otherwise refuses them at once, so that none waits on a thread ([ADR-0102](../adrs/0102-while-auto-approve-is-off-flai-guard-refuses-a-story-s-sub-agent-a-write-to-a.md)). The dashboard token cannot give agents the right to rewrite their own hooks and permissions. Each is off until enabled by name, per project (the main checkout's path) or for all (`*`), in `host_actions` of the host's configuration, which is not among `config.Keys`, so `flai config set` cannot reach it. `internal/hostapi` has no method that reads the journal or runs `flai config`. Only the `settings.*` methods run `flai serve`, `flai agent`, or a token command, all gated by `settings`, and nothing turns `settings` itself on or off: a test holds that. `hostapi.Host{Enabled, Record}` is the operator's say, wired by `cmd` to read the configuration at every question (no restart) and to append to `serve/journal.jsonl` (0600) beside the state. A spec with `action` is refused while disabled with `Disabled` (-32012, HTTP 403) and `Data{action, enable}`, journalled as `disabled`; a spec with `uses` does without the action when it is off and is journalled when it is on. `accept.run` (`flai accept`) never pushes or releases since S-0087 and is not gated on `push` at all any more, closing I-0028 differently than before: there is nothing left for it to do unasked. `publish.run` (S-0087, [ADR-0032](../adrs/0032-accepting-a-story-merges-it-publishing-is-a-deliberate-batched-step-over.md)) is `flai release --pending`, gated on `push` since it acts with the operator's credentials, and maps exit 3 to `Conflict`; it is the one way the dashboard sends accepted work to the remote. `push.run` (`flai push --pending --publish`) and the read `push.pending` were removed in S-0195 (ADR-0067). `publish.preview` is a read (`flai release --pending --dry-run`), not gated. `dashboard.restart`, `dashboard.upgrade`, and `dashboard.stop` (S-0081) gate on `dashboard` and wrap `flai dashboard restart\|upgrade\|stop`, `dashboard.upgrade` with `--published --tag` when it is given a `tag` (S-0298); `dashboard.status`, `dashboard.check`, and since S-0298 `dashboard.versions` are reads, not gated, matching `publish.preview`'s precedent (`dashboard.check` still pulls an image to compare it, a read that does real work, as `publish.preview`'s `ls-remote` does). `checks.run` and `checks.cancel` (S-0082) gate on `checks` and wrap `flai checks run\|cancel`, and since S-0273 `test.run` (`{id, paths, all, max}`) gates on it too and runs `flai test --json` in the item's worktree or the main checkout, and since S-0270 `verify.run` (`{id, max}`), which runs `flai verify <id> --json` in the project, flai verify finding the story's worktree; `plan.run` (S-0208, `{id}`) gates on `plan` and runs `flai plan <id> --json`, refusing a task's ID before flai runs; `describePlan` journals the run it started. `checks.status`, `checks.tail`, and since S-0270 `verify.status` (`{id}`, `flai verify <id> --last --json`: the stored report, or null) are reads, not gated, `checks.tail` streaming its run's own log lines as `$/progress`. `dashboard.restart`\/`upgrade`\/`stop` and `checks.run` set `spec.detachTimeout`: a write whose own success (or, for checks, the operator simply closing the page) can end the request's own connection runs with a background context instead, so that connection dying does not cancel the command through it (found live, S-0081's T-0325). A detached write (those, `host.process`, `host.upgrade`, and `import.run`) is recorded under its request ID before it acts, through `hostapi.Host.Requests`, which `cmd` wires to `serve.Dir.Requests`, `serve/requests.json` (0600) beside the journal, and its outcome when it ends; a record is kept for the write's detach timeout plus ten minutes, then ten minutes after it ends. A repeat is answered from the record and never run again: with the recorded outcome, or as a success `{"repeat": {request_id, started, under_way}}` with a warning, `under_way` saying whether this serve's PID took it, false when the serve that took it ended before recording the outcome, which is what a restart of serve looks like (S-0109, TH-0006: found live in S-0107, a serve restart ran twice when the dashboard's retry reached the next serve). A write that cannot be recorded is refused (`-32603`) without acting. Other writes keep the in-memory journal of completed ones, since a lost connection cancels their command. `project.info` reports `host_actions` so the dashboard can say what will happen; it is asked fresh, not remembered, because no file of the project changes with it |
| `flai serve agent`, `agent set [--name] [--auto-restarts <n>] [-- <program> [args...]]`, `agent start <story>`, `agent restart <story>`, `agent commit <story>`, `agent stop <story>`, `agent stream <story> [--from <offset>] [--follow]`, `agent usage [<story>...] [--all] [--write]`, `agent harness [<name>] [--program] [--guard none\|""] [--deny-protected[=false]] [--reset] [-- args...]`, `agent provider [<name>] [--api] [--base-url] [--key-env] [--spend-key-env] [--model alias=name]... [--reset]`, `agent show`, `agent clear` (S-0079, S-0104, S-0142, S-0143, S-0170, S-0349, S-0355, S-0357, ADR-0029, [ADR-0058](../adrs/0058-the-agent-host-action-lets-the-dashboard-stop-a-story-s-agent-and-a-stop.md), [ADR-0038](../adrs/0038-flai-serve-starts-a-story-s-own-agent-through-an-adapter-with-what-the-operator.md), [ADR-0129](../adrs/0129-a-story-s-agent-names-a-provider-beside-its-harness-and-model-and-the-providers.md), [ADR-0131](../adrs/0131-a-harness-without-a-guard-hook-runs-a-story-s-agent-but-none-of-its-roles.md), [ADR-0132](../adrs/0132-a-story-s-cost-comes-from-its-provider-s-spend-log-when-it-has-a-provider-from.md)) | The second host action, `agent`, is not asked for by any method of the channel: `flai serve` performs it itself (`internal/serve/agents.go`). Per served project a launcher is told of every changed path under `kanban/` and `threads/` by the watcher, of the end of an agent it started, and every minute. It starts an agent for each ready story in pull order that is not blocked, has no agent running, and has had no agent started since it entered ready or since its agent was changed, whether it entered ready before or after `flai serve` started: the first look after a restart is like any other, and the story's run in `serve/agents.json` is what keeps a restart from starting it twice (S-0112, [ADR-0041](../adrs/0041-a-story-in-ready-gets-its-agent-whether-it-entered-ready-before-or-after-flai.md), superseding ADR-0038 in part). Each run records the story's agent as it was started (`story_agent`); a ready story whose agent says something else now (harness, model, or any config value, `manifest.Agent.Same`) and has no agent running is started again, so fixing a failed story's agent needs no move out of ready and back (S-0116). A run recorded before S-0116 carries none and counts as unchanged. It stops when the in-progress limit is reached, counting a running agent whose story is still in ready as a story in progress. It starts none while review is at or over its limit (S-0243, [ADR-0073](../adrs/0073-a-full-review-holds-the-pull-and-flai-check-strict-passes-over-review-over-its.md)): `readyStories` returns `BoardView.ReviewHold`, and each ready story waits with `review is full (5 of 5): accept or send back a story; it holds S-…` as its reason; runs already started go on. It skips a held story (S-0128, [ADR-0046](../adrs/0046-a-ready-story-whose-claim-overlaps-an-open-story-s-is-held-yellow-and-with-its.md)): `readyStories` returns the `workitem.Holds` of the reading, `look` opens the claim of each ready story with a live run and of each it starts (`agentStarted`, "its agent started"), and a todo story that `Holds.Of` holds is skipped with `<id> held (…)` as its reason and takes no room, so the next clear one is started and the held one is first once clear. Since S-0334 ([ADR-0134](../adrs/0134-flai-serve-asks-the-agent-of-a-story-in-progress-about-each-ready-story-it.md)) the look also asks about each ready story held on overlap alone (code `overlap`, not `after` or `no-touches`): for each story in progress that holds it, `messages.AskHold` writes a message by `flai` from the held story to the holding one, in their open conversation or a new one, `about` the overlapping paths, with the held story's title and goal and the three answers (narrow the touches, `flai message share`, or a reply saying why the hold stands). It asks once per pair while the held story stays in ready, and a message that cannot be written is logged and the look goes on. The holds the look reads include the shares in force, so a story a share cleared is started like any other; its prompt then names the conversation, the shared paths, and the split, and its first `inbox` lists the conversation under `messages`. Nothing else holds a story back: nobody attending does (S-0116, [ADR-0043](../adrs/0043-flai-serve-starts-a-ready-story-s-agent-whenever-the-in-progress-limit-has-room.md), superseding ADR-0042's hold). `attended_minutes` is retired: the config still loads with it, and `agent set --attended-minutes` is hidden, says it does nothing, and stores nothing. `agent restart <story>` (`serve.Restart`, in `internal/serve/restart.go`) runs in a process of its own. It starts a new agent, in a new session, for a story in ready or in-progress whose newest run is not running and did not end asking, through `serve.StartNow`, the launcher's own start without checks. The run is recorded in `serve/agents.json` and the serving launcher settles it with `settleOrphans`. `harness.Request.Restart` carries how the last run ended into the prompt. For a story in ready with the limit full, review full (S-0243), or held (`roomFor` returns the hold as well), it queues instead (S-0118, [ADR-0044](../adrs/0044-a-retry-of-a-failed-agent-on-a-ready-story-with-the-in-progress-limit-full-is.md)). `serve.queue` sets `queued` on the story's run, journals it, and returns it, and the command prints `queued another agent for S-…` (`--json`: `story`, `agent`, `queued`). The launcher's look does not hold back a ready story whose run is queued as already tried, so it starts that story within the limit, in pull order, with the restart's prompt. The new run replaces the queued one. Since S-0177 ([ADR-0064](../adrs/0064-a-story-in-ready-or-in-progress-with-no-agent-run-on-this-host-is-started-here.md)) a story with no run here (or only a queued stand-in, which never started) is started too: one in ready through `startNow` with `readyStory.Started`, or, with the limit full, review full, or a hold, queued as a stand-in run naming only the story (`restartWhy` of a run that never started is empty, so the look's start gets the launcher's own prompt); one in progress with `readyStory.Begun`, `serve.begun`'s reading of its files: who last moved it to in-progress and when (`By`, `At`), the narrative's `agent` and `host`, and each thread on the story or its tasks with an entry by anyone else since then. `harness.Request.Begun` reaches `Prompt`, which says the story was begun elsewhere (`Begun.Said`: by whom, when, and on which host, or another host when the narrative does not say), that its branch and worktree may not be here, to reconcile through `flai stream open` (which reopens it), the narrative, and the tasks and go on from what is committed, and to read those threads; the journal says `on the operator's word, begun …`. It refuses, as `serve.Refused` and `rule:` on the command line, when the action is off, the story is in another state, its run is live or asked, it is in ready and already queued, or nothing can start it. `agent start <story>` (`serve.Start`, in `internal/serve/start.go`, S-0115) starts a story in ready through `serve.StartNow` whatever the launcher's own rules say: whether it has had a run since it entered ready, and when it entered. It refuses, the same way, when the action is off, the story is not in ready, its run is live or asked, or nothing can start it. A full in-progress limit (`roomFor`, which restart shares) does not refuse it: it starts past the limit and logs the warning `agent started past the in-progress limit`, which the `agent.start` write passes on among its warnings. Nor does a full review (S-0243; `room` returns it apart from the limit): it starts and logs `agent started past a full review`. Nor does a hold: it starts and logs `agent started for a held story` with the reason. Since S-0182 (I-0050) it passes what it went past, the hold's reason, `the in-progress limit was full`, and `review was full (5 of 5)`, as `readyStory.Started` and `Past` to `harness.Request`: `Prompt` then says the operator started the story now, past those, and asks the agent to pull it as asked and keep its touches to what it changes rather than narrow them only to clear the hold, and the journal's `serve.agent` entry says `on the operator's word, past …`. A start past nothing is told only that the operator started it. Before, its agent got the launcher's prompt (`because it entered ready`) and took the start for flai serve's own. `agent commit <story>` (`serve.Commit`, in `internal/serve/commit.go`, S-0140) starts a story in review through `startNow` with `readyStory.Commit` set to its worktree, which reaches `harness.Request.Commit`: `Prompt` then asks for the worktree's uncommitted changes to be committed on the story branch and nothing else, and `command` gets `FLAI_COMMIT`. The journal says `to commit what its worktree holds`. `agent stop <story>` (`serve.Stop`, in `internal/serve/stop.go`, S-0170, ADR-0058) stops the story's newest run: it marks the run `stopped` in `serve/agents.json`, then, while the run is `running()` (`Owns(pid, start)`: alive, its own session's leader, and on Linux with the start in clock ticks since boot, `/proc/<pid>/stat` field 22, that the launcher recorded in the run's `start` when it started it), sends SIGTERM to the process group and SIGKILL after `Options.StopGrace` (10 s), and records the run ended with outcome `stopped` and why `stopped by the operator at <time>`. A run that ended asking is recorded the same and so is not resumed; a run whose PID is not the agent's is only recorded. The launcher's `ended`, on its own wait and in `settleOrphans`, keeps a stop it finds on the same run rather than judging the end. `settleOrphans`, and the refusals of `start`, `restart`, and `commit` for an agent that runs, test `running()` too, so a run whose PID another process has after a reboot is settled at the next look instead of reading as working for good. `Activity` reads a stopped run as `failed` with that why, so Retry offers a new agent. It refuses a story with no run and one whose agent has ended otherwise, and journals `stopped <story>'s agent <agent>: …` with what it did. It refuses when the action is off, the story is not in review, its run is live or asked, it has no worktree, `git status --porcelain` there lists nothing (through `Options.Git`, the system's git when nil), or nothing can start it; the limit and holds do not apply. `judge` reads a run that leaves the story in review as `worked`. `StartNow` hands the process over (`launcher.handOver`): the short-lived command does not wait for it, and the serving launcher settles the run with `settleOrphans`. Writes to `serve/agents.json` take turns across processes through `agents.json.lock` (created exclusively, taken over after 10 s). A story that names no harness while no command is set waits, which is not a failure. Every ready story it does not start and that has no agent running is named with its reason in the state's `waiting`, the reasons joined by semicolons: already tried since it entered ready (when, and the outcome), no harness and no command, the action off, held (the hold's reason), the limit, or a full review; and `agent not started` is logged with the story and reason when a story's reason changes in kind, not when a detail in it moves, and not at every look. Each run is started through `internal/harness`: `harness.For` picks the adapter from the story's `agent.harness`, or `command` when it names none. Beside `DefaultHost` and `Start`, every adapter states its `Capabilities` and gives a `Reader` (S-0352, [ADR-0130](../adrs/0130-every-harness-meets-flai-through-neutral-contracts-an-adapter-s-capabilities-a.md)). `Capabilities` are what flai serve may count on the harness to do: `Resume`, go on with an agent in the session it ran before; `Guard`, run `flai guard` as a hook on the agent's calls, so that a call it refuses is never made; `Ask`, hold a call to ask the owner on a thread, as `permission_prompt` does, and answer it allow or deny; `RoleModels`, give each sub-agent role its own model; `StrategicAgents`, run the planner, the orchestrator, or the analyzer from its definition; and `Cost`, report what its calls cost. `claude-code` states all six and `command` none. Since S-0355 ([ADR-0131](../adrs/0131-a-harness-without-a-guard-hook-runs-a-story-s-agent-but-none-of-its-roles.md), refining ADR-0060, ADR-0086, and ADR-0102) `launcher.start` reads `Guard` and `Ask` before a story's agent starts, after `harness.For` and before the command is built, through `startAllowed`, with the host's lifts for the harness (`AgentConfig.Lifts`, `serve.HarnessLifts{GuardNone, DenyProtected}` from `agent.harnesses.<name>`) and whether the project's `auto-approve` action is on (`AgentConfig.AutoApprove`). Without `Guard`, a story whose `agent.roles` are not empty is refused `harness <name> cannot run flai guard, so the story's roles (<roles, sorted>) cannot run; flai serve agent harness <name> --guard none runs them unguarded (ADR-0131)`, unless the host sets `guard: none` for the harness; then it starts, and the journal entry's detail and the `agent started` log line (`roles`) add `its roles (<roles>) run without flai guard (guard: none on harness <name>, ADR-0131)`. A story with no roles is never refused for it. Without `Ask`, every start is refused `harness <name> cannot ask before a protected write, so flai serve does not start a story's agent on it; turn on the project's auto-approve host action (flai serve enable auto-approve), or say your arguments for it deny the protected paths with flai serve agent harness <name> --deny-protected (ADR-0131)`, unless `auto-approve` is on for the project or the host sets `deny_protected` for the harness, which is the operator's word that their arguments deny the protected paths in the harness's own vocabulary, which flai cannot read. When both refuse, the two are joined by "; and". A refusal is the start's failure, as an unknown harness's is (`run.Error`, `could not be started: …` as the run's why and on the dashboard's story page, the journal's `could not be started`, the `agent could not be started` log line). Every start of a story's agent passes through it: the look's, a resume's, an automatic restart's, and `agent start`, `restart`, and `commit`; the planner, the orchestrator, and the analyzer do not. Nothing reads the other four capabilities yet. `Reader` is a fresh `usage.Reader` of the harness's log, for the logs of one measurement read in order: `claude-code`'s is `usage.ClaudeCode()`, which reads stream-json, and `command`'s reads nothing, since flai does not know what the operator's command logs. `claude-code` builds `claude -p <prompt> --output-format stream-json --verbose --mcp-config {flai: this flai's mcp --agent <name>} --strict-mcp-config --name "<key> <story>" --session-id <uuid> [--model] [--effort] [--max-budget-usd] [--fallback-model] [--agents]` then, since S-0257 ([ADR-0086](../adrs/0086-flai-serve-gives-a-claude-code-agent-flai-s-permission-prompt-as-its-permission.md), refining ADR-0038), `--permission-prompt-tool mcp__flai__permission_prompt` (`harness.PermissionPromptTool`, for the planner's session too), and then the operator's arguments (default `--permission-mode acceptEdits --allowedTools Bash,mcp__flai`). Run headless, Claude Code refuses a write to a path it protects, a `.claude/` folder, `.mcp.json`, and the others [ADR-0106](../adrs/0106-a-story-whose-branch-changes-a-path-claude-code-protects-is-accepted-by-the.md) lists, unless a person or that handler approves it; the handler adds no permission the operator did not give, and prompts other than those writes are still refused. Since S-0286 flai serve checks the handler against each Claude Code version it runs, once (`internal/serve/claudecheck.go`). At each look that may start an agent, the first after it starts among them, for a project whose `agent`, `plan`, `orchestrate`, or `analyze` action is on and whose manifest has an agent run `claude-code`, it looks at the program the adapter runs (`harness.ClaudeCodeHost`); when that file, its symbolic links followed, differs in path, size, or time from the one last looked at (`programIdentity`), or the project's default agent names a provider that claude was not yet looked at through, it reads `claude --version` in the background, one check at a time, never delaying an agent's start. Since S-0360 the check runs through the provider the project's default agent names (`agent.provider`, [ADR-0129](../adrs/0129-a-story-s-agent-names-a-provider-beside-its-harness-and-model-and-the-providers.md)), so a host that reaches Claude only through a gateway is not reported failing for want of Anthropic access: `providerEnv` resolves it with `manifest.ResolveProvider` from `AgentConfig.Providers` and the project's manifest, as a start does, takes the `claude-code` adapter's `Start` environment for it (`ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` empty, and `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_HAIKU_MODEL` where `models` names `haiku`, so `haiku` resolves on the gateway), and copies the key into `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` from its `key_env` as `providerKeys` does, laid over the check's environment. A project whose default agent names none is checked against Anthropic, recorded as `anthropic`. A version with an entry in `claude-checks.json` in the serve folder for that provider is not checked again, whatever its outcome; removing the entry has it checked again. A version passed through one provider is checked again through another the first time a project names it. A provider whose `key_env`, or a variable `EnvFrom` names, is unset or empty in flai serve's environment is not checked: flai serve logs `claude code check skipped` (warn) with the version, the provider, and a reason naming the variable, never its value, records nothing, and opens no thread; it is looked at again once flai serve restarts or the `claude` changes. A provider that cannot be resolved, or that the adapter refuses, logs `claude code not checked` and records nothing. Otherwise `newClaudeScratch` makes a project in a temporary folder, a git repository with one story in progress, its worktree's folder, and a flai configuration of its own with `auto-approve` on, and `harness.ClaudeCodeCheck` runs a headless `claude -p` in it on `haiku`, with this flai's MCP server on that configuration as its only server and a story agent's permission arguments (`--permission-prompt-tool mcp__flai__permission_prompt`, then the host's arguments), asking it to Write `.claude/flai-check.md` in the worktree with one line naming the version, for at most five minutes. The file holding that line is a pass. The entry, keyed by version and then by provider, has `checked`, `program`, `outcome` (`passed` or `failed`), and on a failure `error`: what went wrong and the last 20 lines of what Claude Code said (its tool calls, tool errors, how the session ended, and any line that is not stream-json); a file written before S-0360, one entry per version, is read as checked through `anthropic` and written again in the new shape at the next record. On a failure flai serve opens one thread, by `flai serve`, on `system-flow.yaml` of each served project whose agents run `claude-code` and whose default agent names the provider the check ran through, titled `Claude Code <version> through <provider> fails flai's permission_prompt check`, quoting the error and naming the entry to remove; the agents still run. Log lines of the check carry `provider`. A `claude` it cannot find, a version it cannot read, and a check stopped with flai serve record nothing. One check costs about 0.02 USD. Its prompt (`harness.Prompt`, sent by `claude-code` only) asks the agent to work its story to review and, since S-0175 ([ADR-0059](../adrs/0059-a-story-s-agent-hands-search-test-runs-and-verification-to-an-explorer-and-a.md)), to keep its context for decisions and edits and hand noisy work to sub-agents with the Agent tool (search to the explorer; test, lint, and check runs and long logs to the verifier until S-0270, below), giving each the worktree, the story and task IDs, the question, and the shape of the answer, taking back a summary, asking the designer itself what a sub-agent returns, and having a fresh verifier check the diff against the criteria and conventions before review. Since S-0189 it is told to run only the tests for what it changed and, until S-0270, was told to leave the whole suite, the lint, and `flai check` to one verifier before review, through the project's close-out script where it has one, and one more after fixing what that one found, fixing it itself and not through a sub-agent, and not to repeat a verifier's passing run; a resumed or commit run is not told again. From S-0266 to S-0270 it was told to have that verifier run the close-out once, in one command without a pipe or a file, and read its last line, which names the outcome and the step it stopped at, and to name any step it already knows will stop, and why, so that the verifier reports the stop and checks the steps after it rather than running the close-out again. Since S-0270 ([ADR-0110](../adrs/0110-a-story-s-agent-runs-the-close-out-which-runs-flai-verify-itself-before-review.md), refining ADR-0059) it is told to hand code, document, and log search to the explorer and only the review of a large diff to the verifier; to leave the whole suite, the whole lint, and `flai check` to the close-out before review and run it itself, once, in the worktree: the project's close-out script, which runs `flai verify <story> --record-issues` and then commits, or `flai verify <story>` (the MCP tool `verify`) where it has none, as one Bash call with a timeout of 600000 ms and its exit status echoed after it, never piped or redirected into a file; to read its last line and the findings of the step that failed, fix them itself, commit, and run it again, never handing the run to a sub-agent; that its passing run is the story's run before review, which `flai verify <story> --last` prints again; and to check the diff against the criteria and the conventions itself, naming the criteria it meets by number, handing that review to one fresh verifier only when the diff is too large to read in its own context, telling it the run passed, at which commit, and to read `flai verify <story> --last` rather than run the suite. The orchestrator's prompt, under `accept_reviews`, reads the story's last result with `flai verify <story> --last --json`, runs `flai verify` when it did not pass or its commit is not the head of `story/<story>`, counts a run that did not pass as a blocker, and hands the worktree and the result to its verifier, which reads it rather than running the suite and checks the diff against each criterion ([strategic-agents.md](strategic-agents.md)). Since S-0268 it is told to make independent edits and commands in one turn, as several tool calls in one message, and to move a task it has just written to ready and in-progress in one command (`flai move T-nnnn ready && flai move T-nnnn in-progress`), since `flai move` refuses a task straight from backlog to in-progress; and `git.md` tells it to show that a new test fails without the change from a scratch copy of the main branch's files, or the old binary, and never with `git stash`, whose stack every worktree of a clone shares. Since S-0176 it is told to plan the story's tasks as it writes them (each one's `touches` and `after`, the layers that can run together, and why, in the narrative's `## Decisions`) and to hand each task to a task sub-agent, running a layer's tasks at once only when they are long, and reviewing, committing, and moving each task itself. Since S-0299 ([ADR-0102](../adrs/0102-while-auto-approve-is-off-flai-guard-refuses-a-story-s-sub-agent-a-write-to-a.md)) it is told, before it launches any sub-agent, that a write to a file in a `.claude/` folder, and since S-0286 to any path Claude Code protects such as `.mcp.json`, is never a sub-agent's, since `flai guard` refuses it unless `auto-approve` is on: to say so in the prompt of each sub-agent whose task changes such a file and have it return the file's whole new content in its final message; to make the write itself once the layer's sub-agents are back, never while a layer runs, since `permission_prompt` opens a thread and holds the call. Since S-0309 ([ADR-0124](../adrs/0124-permission-prompt-holds-a-write-at-most-four-minutes-then-refuses-it-and-leaves.md)) it is told that `permission_prompt` refuses such a write after at most four minutes unanswered, leaving its thread open, so it goes on with work that does not need the write, makes the same write again once the thread is answered, and, when nothing else is left, writes the narrative's Current state and Next steps naming the thread and ends; before, it was told to stage each file in the worktree's ignored `.flai-cache/` folder and open a thread with the `cp` commands that put each in place. The session loads the project's `.claude/agents/` (the template's `explorer` and `verifier`) and `.claude/settings.json` (the `flai guard` hook) as any session started in the project does; the adapter adds no flag for them, and the Agent tool needs no `--allowedTools` entry. Since S-0189 a story whose `agent.roles` give a role a model is started with `--agents` (`harness.subAgents`, [ADR-0065](../adrs/0065-a-story-s-agent-carries-a-model-per-sub-agent-role-and-claude-code-runs-each.md)): for each such role, the project's `.claude/agents/<definition>.md` (`explore` is `explorer`, `verify` is `verifier`), its front matter but `name` with `tools` as a list and its body as `prompt`, and the role's model as `model`; a session's `--agents` outranks the project's files, so only the model differs. A role it has no definition for, whose definition cannot be read, that names a harness other than `claude-code`, or that names config, refuses the start. A story's config key it does not take, or a value outside its pattern, refuses the start. `command` is `config.Agent.Command` with `{story}`, `{root}`, `{model}`, `{harness}`, and `{provider}` replaced, and `FLAI_MODEL`, `FLAI_HARNESS`, `FLAI_AGENT_CONFIG` (JSON), `FLAI_AGENT_ROLES` (JSON, when the story's agent has roles), on a resume `FLAI_ANSWERED` (the thread answered, or the conversation written on, S-0335), and, when the agent names a provider, `FLAI_PROVIDER*` (below). The program and arguments of each harness are the operator's, in `config.Agent.Harnesses` (`flai serve agent harness`; `--` alone sets no arguments, `--reset` the adapter's defaults and clears the lifts), not among `config.Keys`. So are its lifts (S-0355, [ADR-0131](../adrs/0131-a-harness-without-a-guard-hook-runs-a-story-s-agent-but-none-of-its-roles.md)): `guard` (`--guard none`, `--guard ""` to clear; `config.Parse`, `Save`, and `AgentStart.WithHarness` refuse any other value) and `deny_protected` (`--deny-protected`, `--deny-protected=false`), each changing only itself. The `command` harness takes only `--guard`, `--deny-protected`, and `--reset` there, since its program is `agent set`'s. `agent harness` with no name, and `agent show`, print under each harness the lifts set on it with what each gives up, and `--json` carries `guard` and `deny_protected`. The dashboard's `settings.harness` takes `guard` and `deny_protected`, and the command's name for those and `reset` alone, and `settings.get` gives each harness, the command's included, its `guard`, `deny_protected`, `guard_capable`, and `ask_capable` ([flaiover-dashboard.md](flaiover-dashboard.md)). The providers a story's agent may name (S-0349, [ADR-0129](../adrs/0129-a-story-s-agent-names-a-provider-beside-its-harness-and-model-and-the-providers.md)) are the operator's too, in `config.Agent.Providers` (`flai serve agent provider <name>`), not among `config.Keys`: each entry an `api` (`anthropic-messages`, `openai-chat`, `openai-responses`), a `base_url` (an absolute `http` or `https` URL with no user or password), a `key_env`, the name of the host's environment variable that holds the key and never the key, optional `models`, the harness's aliases and their names on the provider, and optional `spend_key_env`, the name of the variable of a key that can read the gateway's spend log where the agent's own key cannot, such as LiteLLM's master key (S-0357, [Pricing a run from its provider's spend log](#pricing-a-run-from-its-providers-spend-log)) (`manifest.Provider`). A new entry needs `--api`, `--base-url`, and `--key-env`; on one that is set, the flags given change those fields, and `--model`, given at all, replaces the aliases; `--spend-key-env ""` clears `spend_key_env`. `--reset` removes the entry; with a name and no flag it is printed, with no name every entry, each with the variable its `spend_key_env` names where set, `--json` as `{providers}`; `agent clear` keeps them. `config.Parse` and `AgentStart.WithProvider` refuse an entry `manifest.HostProviderProblems` finds incomplete or invalid, and no message repeats a `key_env` that is not a variable's name, since it may be the key. `manifest.ResolveProvider(name, host, project)` is what a start resolves: no name is no provider, the harness's own default; a name the host has no entry for is refused, naming `flai serve agent provider <name>`; otherwise the host's entry with the manifest's `providers.<name>` `api` and `base_url` laid over it and its `models` merged alias by alias, the project's winning, refused when the result lacks `api`, `base_url`, or `key_env` or holds a value that is not valid. Since S-0350 every start, a story's agent's and the planner's, orchestrator's, and analyzer's, builds its command in `launcher.startOf`: `provide` resolves the provider the agent names from `AgentConfig.Providers` and the project's manifest, read at each start, into `harness.Request.ProviderName` and `Provider`, and records the name and the `key_env` on the run (`AgentRun.Provider`, `KeyEnv`, `provider` and `key_env` in `serve/agents.json`); an agent that names none is started as before and the manifest is not read. The adapter derives the environment from the resolved entry ([agent-adapters.md](agent-adapters.md#harness-provider-and-model), what each harness would derive). `claude-code` (`claudeCodeProvider`) requires `api: anthropic-messages` and sets `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` empty so a key flai serve inherited is not sent to the gateway, and `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_OPUS_MODEL`, `_SONNET_`, and `_HAIKU_` for each of those aliases `models` names; it sets no `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL` and does not check `fallback_model` against the provider. `command` sets `FLAI_PROVIDER`, `FLAI_PROVIDER_API`, `FLAI_PROVIDER_BASE_URL`, and `FLAI_PROVIDER_KEY_ENV`, the variable's name, and replaces `{provider}`; it takes any `api`, and the command reads the key itself from the environment it inherits. No adapter reads a key: `harness.Start.EnvFrom` maps a variable of the child's to the name of a variable of flai serve's environment (`ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` to `key_env` for `claude-code`), and `providerKeys` reads each with `os.LookupEnv` at the start and hands the pairs to `spawn` alone, which adds them to the child's environment. `Argv` and `Env` never carry a key, so the run, its log, the journal, a log line, and an error can hold the whole `Start` and still not hold one. Each refusal is the start's failure, recorded as an unknown harness's is (the journal's `could not be started`, `run.Error`), and names its fix: a provider the host has no entry for; a resolved entry incomplete or invalid; an `api` the harness cannot speak (`claude-code`: `flai serve agent provider <name> --api anthropic-messages`, or another provider); a `key_env`, or a variable `EnvFrom` names, unset or empty in flai serve's environment, checked whatever the harness (set it where `flai host` or `flai serve` runs, or `--key-env`); and a manifest that cannot be read, since a start without the project's overrides could send the calls elsewhere than the project says ([ADR-0129](../adrs/0129-a-story-s-agent-names-a-provider-beside-its-harness-and-model-and-the-providers.md)). flai serve reads its environment as `flai host` handed it over, so a variable set after the host started is seen only once the host is started again. It is `exec`ed in the project's directory, detached (`serve.Detach`), with flai serve's environment less `FLAI_HOST_URL`, `FLAI_HOST_TOKEN`, and `FLAI_CONFIG` (S-0183, `serve.agentEnv`: a flai the agent runs finds its own config and not the operator's host), less each variable a host provider's `spend_key_env` names that is no provider's `key_env` (`withoutSpendKeys`, S-0357: an agent never holds a key that reads a gateway's spend log), and `FLAI_AGENT` `<name>-<story>`, `FLAI_STORY`, `FLAI_SESSION`, `FLAI_STARTED_BY=flai-serve`, its output in `serve/agents/<key>-<story>-<time>.log`. When it ends, `judge` reads where it left the story: review or done is `worked`; an open thread on the story whose last entry is the agent's is `asked`, with the thread; anything else is `failed`, with where and whether blocked and the exit code. Since S-0335 (`judgeRun`) a run is `asked` too when a conversation of its story awaits the other story's reply, or when a message to its story came after the run started and has no reply, a reply that came before the end was judged, which starts it again at once; the run keeps those `conversations` and the stories it `waits_on`. `resume` starts such a run again in its session (`answered`) when the other story's agent has written last on a conversation it ended on, when that conversation reads as closed, or when a message to its story came after it ended (`messaged`), even while its question to the operator is unanswered; `FLAI_ANSWERED` then names the conversation, and the prompt says another story's agent wrote on it, to read it with `inbox` or `message_get`, and to answer with `message_reply`. An `asked` run is started again, as the same agent in the same session (`--resume`), as soon as its thread has an entry by someone else or is resolved, by `resume` while its story is open, in progress or in review, whatever the limit or anyone attending: an open story is counted in the limit already and holds others rather than being held. A story sent back to ready meanwhile is started again in its session by the look instead (`readyStory.Asked`), only when nothing holds it, the limit has room, and review is under its limit, and until the answer it waits with `<id>'s agent is waiting for an answer to <thread>`; one in backlog is not started (S-0182). Since S-0294 ([ADR-0108](../adrs/0108-flai-serve-restarts-a-story-s-agent-that-ended-with-its-story-in-progress-up-to.md), refining ADR-0043) a `failed` run that started and was not stopped by the operator, on a story in progress and not blocked, is started again by the look as `agent restart` starts it: a new session, the same agent name, the run in `serve/agents.json`, and a prompt that says how the last run ended and that flai serve restarted it on its own. The in-progress limit, review, and holds do not hold it back. It does so while the story's count of automatic restarts is under the host's `agent.auto_restarts` (`agent set --auto-restarts <n>`; 2 when unset, 0 turns it off). The count is per story, kept on its runs, and starts again from 0 with any run flai serve did not start on its own: the operator's restart or start, or the start when the story enters ready again. A run that could not be started is not restarted. When a run ends that would be restarted but for the limit, flai serve opens one thread on the story, by `flai serve`, saying how many times its agent ended, how the last run ended, and that `flai serve agent restart` or Retry starts it again. Since S-0394 ([ADR-0167](../adrs/0167-flai-serve-reads-each-pull-request-s-required-checks-and-reviews-into-kept.md)) `startSentBack`, before `restartEnded` at each look, starts the agent of a story in progress and not blocked that flai sent back from review on its pull request ([Reading the pull request back](#reading-the-pull-request-back)), while the latest send back's thread is open. It starts it once per send back: only when the story's newest run started before the send back, has ended, and was not stopped by the operator. With that run's session, the agent goes on in it (`--resume`), its prompt naming the thread and what failed, and saying to fix the branch, close out, and move the story to review again. With none, it starts in a new session, told to take the story up from its narrative and worktree and to read the thread. It spends no automatic restart and does not wait for room under the in-progress limit, since the story kept its place. A story flai serve never ran an agent for is left to the session that works it, whose inbox lists the thread, so that no headless agent starts beside an interactive one.
