The run records the thread as `sent_back`, and the journal says the agent was started again after flai sent the story back. A run whose process is gone and that no launcher waits for (it outlived an earlier `flai serve`) is judged at the next look. State per project in `serve/agents.json`: `stories` (each story's newest run, with harness, model, the story's agent, session, outcome, why, thread, since S-0335 `conversations` and `waits_on`, and since S-0394 `sent_back`), and `running`, `last`, and `waiting` as before. Every start and failure is a journal entry with action `agent`. `agent.status` (`hostapi`, read-only, asked fresh by the dashboard) reports `enabled`, that state with the command's name and never its arguments, `plans` (since S-0208: the newest planner run for each item, by its ID, as `serve/agents.json` keeps it), `orchestrator` (since S-0218, with `held` while the operator holds it stopped since S-0228) `analyzer` (since S-0223, with its `focus`, `trigger`, and, once it has ended, `report`), and `curator` (since S-0431, with its `role`, `since`, `trigger`, and, once it has ended, `entries`), each the project's newest such run or null, `past_orchestrators` and `past_analyzers` (since S-0228: up to 20 earlier runs of each, newest first), and `stories`: `serve.Activity`, each story's `working`, `waiting` (running with a question of its own open, a conversation of its story awaiting another story's agent's reply, or its story blocked, or `asked`; since S-0335 one that waits on another story's agent carries `waits_on` and `conversations`, `thread` staying the operator's question, and a why such as `waiting for S-0004's agent to reply on MS-0002: <title>`, and start, restart, and commit refuse it with `<story>'s agent is waiting for …; flai serve starts it again when that comes`), `failed`, or `worked`. A queued run whose story is in ready reads as `waiting`, why `queued: flai serve starts another agent when there is room`. A story in ready that a claim holds and whose agent is not running, and did not end asking or has its answer, reads as `waiting` with the hold's reason as why and `hold: {code, reason}` (`serve.held`), whether or not it has a run: one that never had an agent is given a run naming only the story and its harness and model, so a dashboard that reads `run` unguarded keeps working. Since S-0177 a story in progress with no run here, or only a queued stand-in, reads as `waiting` with `elsewhere: {by, at, agent, host, here}` (`serve.elsewhere`, `Elsewhere.Said` as why: `begun by <agent> on <host> at <time>; no agent here`, where the host is the narrative's, `another host` when it names none, and `this host, outside flai serve` when it is `workitem.ThisHost`), with the same stand-in run naming the narrative's agent. The configuration is read at every look, so enabling, disabling, and new commands need no restart. `serve.Stream` (`internal/serve/stream.go`, S-0142) reads the log of a story's newest run from a byte offset, or its last 256 KiB, whole lines only, and turns Claude Code's stream-json into entries of kind `session`, `text`, `thinking` (when not redacted), `tool` (the tool's name, and its input's description, command, file, pattern, query, URL, or prompt, else its fields), `result` (a tool's answer, `error` when it failed), `task` (a background task started or ended), and `end` (the session's outcome, turns, seconds, cost, and final text); any other line is `output` as it is, and token counts, heartbeats, and rate-limit events are left out. It reads stream-json itself, not through a `usage.Reader`: it is display, not measurement. One read consumes at most 1 MiB, returns the newest 200 entries (`skipped` counts the rest), and cuts a text to 2000 characters, a result to 400, and a tool's input to 300; a line over 1 MiB is shown by its size. It answers with the run's `agent`, `started` (a new run has a new log, so an offset from another means nothing), `ended`, `running`, `outcome`, and `from`, `next`, `size`, `more`. A run that could not be started has one `end` entry with the error; a story with no run is `hostapi.ErrNoAgent`. `agent stream <story>` prints it, one entry to a line (`kind!` when it failed), `--follow` reads again every second until the run has ended and the log is read, starting from 0 when another run replaces it, and `--json` prints each read as one line. `agent.stream` (`hostapi`, read-only and ungated like `agent.status`) answers the same read through `Host.AgentStream`, `{story, after?}`, -32004 when flai serve started no agent for the story; with `plan` or, since S-0228, `role` (`orchestrate` or `analyze`) in place of `story`, exactly one of the three, it reads the newest planner run for an item, or the project's newest run of that role, whose answer carries `role`. What a story's agents spent is measured from the same logs (`internal/usage`, `internal/serve/usage.go`, S-0143, [ADR-0051](../adrs/0051-work-items-record-the-tokens-and-cost-their-agents-spent-measured-from-the.md)). `usage.Read` takes a `usage.Reader` and the story's logs, `serve/agents/<key>-<story>-*.log` oldest first (`Dir.AgentLogs`). The reader turns each line into neutral events (`usage.Event`, S-0352, [ADR-0130](../adrs/0130-every-harness-meets-flai-through-neutral-contracts-an-adapter-s-capabilities-a.md)): `SessionStart`, with the session's model; `Call`, a line of a call to the model, with its message ID, model, and tokens; `ToolCall`, a call's use of a tool, with the tool's name as the harness writes it, its ID, and its input's description, prompt, command, and file; `ToolResult`, by the ID of the call it answers, with whether it failed, and its text only for `wait_for_events`; and `End`, the run's final text, cost, tokens, and totals and cost per model. Every event carries `ParentCall`, the ID of the tool call that started the sub-agent it is from, empty for the session's own agent, and `ParentDescription`, that call's description when the line repeats it. `internal/usage` measures from these events alone, never from a harness's format, and keeps two things: each session's newest `End`, whose totals per model are the session's, cumulative across the runs a resume adds; and each `Call`, gathered by message ID with the most any of its lines says of each kind of token, with its time and model. With Claude Code the input and cache tokens are exact and the output tokens the first chunk's. Claude Code's reader (`usage/claudecode.go`) maps `system` `init` to `SessionStart`; `assistant` to a `Call` with `message.id`, `model`, and `usage`, then a `ToolCall` per `tool_use`; each `tool_result` of a `user` line to a `ToolResult`; and `result` to an `End` with `total_cost_usd`, `usage`, and `modelUsage` with each model's `costUSD`; each line's `parent_tool_use_id` and `task_description` are on every event it holds. A story's logs are read with the reader of the harness its newest run recorded in `serve/agents.json`, else that of its agent as `harness.For` resolves it (`logReader`), so a `command` log yields no usage; `Dir.Rates` and a strategic agent's activity read with `claude-code`'s, the harness that reports cost and runs strategic agents. An empty wake is defined on these events in [metrics.md](metrics.md#waiting). Since S-0293 it also keeps each turn of the story's own agent, a `Call` with no `ParentCall` that makes a `ToolCall`, with its tool calls gathered by message ID, and classifies it by its calls as `ceremony`, `test_runs`, `empty_wakes`, `hand_edits`, or `work` (`usage/turns.go`); the counts per UTC day become the story's `usage.turns`, beside `usage.empty_wakes` ([ADR-0116](../adrs/0116-when-flai-measures-a-story-s-usage-from-its-logs-it-classifies-each-turn-of-the.md), [metrics.md](metrics.md#turns-s-0293)). `Record.Total` is the reported totals plus the calls after a session's newest `End`, priced at the model's rate (reported cost over reported tokens, across every log in `serve/agents`, `Dir.Rates`, read from `End` events alone) and marked `estimated`; the seconds are each run's first to last timestamped event. `Record.Tasks` gives a task each session's totals in the share of their calls' input and cache tokens that are its own, and its unreported calls the same way, marked `estimated` (S-0230, [ADR-0071](../adrs/0071-a-task-s-usage-is-the-calls-of-the-sub-agents-started-for-it-and-an-even-share.md), refining ADR-0051): the calls of the sub-agents started for it, wherever they fall, and an even share, among the tasks in progress at the time, of every other call made while it was in progress (a transition to `in-progress` until the next transition, the last open). A sub-agent's calls carry `ParentCall`, the ID of its `Agent` (or `Task`) call; it is a task's when that call's description names one of the story's task IDs, or, naming none, its prompt does. A task's seconds are the runs' seconds that overlap its intervals. `serve.Measure` writes the story's `usage` (`source: log`) and each task's that spent something, one that is done and was in progress with nothing measured as an empty measurement so it is not measured again, re-reading each item just before it saves, carrying its `usage.strategic` onto what it measured (`usage.WithStrategic`, ADR-0083), and writing only what differs; then `RollUp` sums the story's epic again. The launcher measures a story when an agent it started ends, whether it waited for it or `settleOrphans` found it gone, and at every look for each story whose agent runs and has a task in done with no `usage` (`measureDone`); a failure is logged as `usage not measured` (warn), a write as `usage measured` (info, with the tokens, the cost, `priced_by`, and how many items changed). Since S-0357 a run through a provider is priced from its gateway's spend log ([Pricing a run from its provider's spend log](#pricing-a-run-from-its-providers-spend-log)). `agent usage <story>...` measures on the operator's word and prints it (`--json`: `serve.Measured` per story: `story`, `logs`, `usage`, `tasks`, `changed`, and `unread`, the runs through a provider whose gateway did not say what it charged, each with `log`, `provider`, and `reason`); `--write` records it; `--all` measures every story of the project with a log (`Dir.LoggedStories`), so `--all --write` gives the stories measured before a release the values it added, such as `usage.turns`. A log the reader yields no events from measures as nothing |
| `flai serve forge [<forge>] [--token-env <NAME>] [--reset] [--json]` (S-0392, [ADR-0165](../adrs/0165-a-project-chooses-local-or-pull-request-acceptance-in-its-manifest-flai-reaches.md)) | The name of the environment variable a forge's token is read from on this host, kept in the host's configuration as `forges.<forge>.token_env` (`config.ForgeHost`), beside the providers' `key_env` ([ADR-0129](../adrs/0129-a-story-s-agent-names-a-provider-beside-its-harness-and-model-and-the-providers.md)) and not among `config.Keys` (`cmd/serve_actions.go`). The forges are `config.KnownForges`, `github` alone; unset, its variable is `GITHUB_TOKEN` (`config.DefaultGitHubTokenEnv`). `--token-env` names the variable, refused when it is not a variable's name, without repeating the value, since it may be the token pasted in its place; `--reset` removes the entry, so the default holds again. With no flag it prints the forge, or with no forge every forge, as `forge github: its token in the variable GITHUB_TOKEN (default), set in this environment`; `--json` is `{forges: {<forge>: {token_env, default, token_set}}}`. Whether the variable is set is read from this process's environment, never its value. No flag takes a token. `config.Parse` refuses a `forges` entry for a forge flai does not call or with a `token_env` that is not a variable's name. |
| `flai plan <E-nnnn\|S-nnnn> [--json]` (S-0208, [ADR-0082](../adrs/0082-flai-serve-starts-the-planner-for-an-epic-or-a-story-behind-the-plan-host.md)) | Starts the planner for an epic or a story now, on the operator's word; [strategic-agents.md](strategic-agents.md#the-planner) has the whole of it. Since S-0211 ([ADR-0084](../adrs/0084-flai-serve-plans-again-on-its-own-behind-the-plan-host-action-on-an-edit-when.md)) `flai serve` also plans again on its own while `plan` is on: on an edit, when work ahead completes or the order changes as `planning.replan` allows, and on `planning.schedule`. Its replanner (`internal/serve/replan.go`) queues runs and starts each with flai serve's own launcher after the same `planCheck` as `serve.Plan` and a check that the story's agent is not at work: `drain` drops a run for a story in progress or in review, or whose agent run is `running()`, and logs `queued planner dropped` (`worked`, I-0098); `editTrigger` gives no trigger for an edit by the story's own agent (`storyAgent`). It records its triggers as `AgentRun.Trigger`; a run `serve.Plan` starts records `asked`; see [Planning again](strategic-agents.md#planning-again). `serve.Plan` (`internal/serve/plan.go`) refuses, as `serve.Refused` and `rule:` on the command line, while the `plan` host action is off for the project (`AgentConfig.Plan`), for a task or an ID that is neither an epic's nor a story's, for an item archived, done, or cancelled, while the item's newest planner run is `running()`, and when the planner's agent names no harness and no command is set. The agent is `Manifest.PlanningAgent`, `planning.agent` merged over `agent` with `Agent.With`. The launcher's `plan` builds a `harness.Request` with `Role` `plan` (`conventions.RolePlan`), `Item`, and no `Story`, names the run `planner-<item>`, and starts it in the main checkout (`mainRootOf`) through `spawn`, as a story's run is started, with `FLAI_ROLE` and `FLAI_ITEM` from `harness.roleEnv` in place of `FLAI_STORY`; `agentEnv` drops `FLAI_STORY`, `FLAI_ROLE`, and `FLAI_ITEM` from flai serve's own environment first (`runMarks`). The log is `serve/agents/<key>-planner-<start>.log` (`openLog`: a strategic agent's run takes the first second from its start that no log has, within a minute, so `Dir.ActivityLogs` tells runs apart). The run is kept in `AgentState.Plans` by item (`plans` in `serve/agents.json`; `AgentRun.Item` set and `Story` empty), never in `Running` or `Last`, and counts against no limit. `handOver` is set: the command does not wait, and the serving launcher settles a gone planner run in `settleOrphans`, as it does a story's. `planEnded` records it with `judgePlan`'s outcome (`asked` with the thread when an open thread on the item ends with the planner's entry, `failed` on a non-zero exit or an unreadable project, `worked` otherwise, an unseen exit included) and calls `LogRunEnd` for `planner` (ADR-0079), which also charges the activity's apportioned usage to the planned item and every item above it with `Repo.ChargeStrategic` (ADR-0083; `activityMeasure.chargePlanned`, which finds the item as the one in `AgentState.Plans` whose run's log has the measured run's file name, and charges nothing when none has); `resume` does not start an `asked` planner again. Each start and failure is a journal entry with action `plan` and method `serve.plan`; `launcher.changed` gets the run, and `serve.Run` notifies the dashboard with `agent` and `{project, item}`. `claude-code` names the session `<key> plan <item>`, adds the project's `.claude/agents/planner.md` to `--agents` (`readDefinition`, with the planner's agent's model when it names one; a missing definition refuses the start, naming `flai upgrade`), and selects it with `--agent planner`; `command` gets `FLAI_ROLE` and `FLAI_ITEM` and an empty `{story}`. `harness.Prompt` sends `planPrompt`. With no `flai serve` running the command says on standard error that the end is recorded and the activity logged once one runs. `--json` prints `{item, agent, harness, command, pid, log, session, started}`. Since S-0219, under `FLAI_ROLE=orchestrate` the command and the MCP tool `plan` hold the orchestrator themselves (`app.orchestratorPlans`): only an epic `flai plan --candidates` lists, and only while `orchestration.permissions.plan_backlog_epics` is on, or, since S-0328 ([ADR-0119](../adrs/0119-with-plan-backlog-stories-on-the-orchestrator-asks-the-planner-for-each-backlog.md)), a story it lists, and only while `orchestration.permissions.plan_backlog_stories` is on; anything else is refused with why, as `rule:` on the command line. They start it with `serve.PlanForOrchestrator`, which is `serve.Plan` with the trigger `orchestrator` (`serve.TriggerOrchestrator`) in place of `asked`, so the run and its entry's `- Trigger:` line in `wip/agents/planner.md` say so, and the MCP tool journals `orchestrator` as who asked (`orchestratorBy`). |
| `flai plan --candidates [--json]` (S-0219, S-0328) | Lists, in ID order, the epics the planner should plan, then the stories, each with why (`Repo.PlanCandidates`, `workitem.PlanCandidatesOf`): an epic in the backlog with no stories, `in the backlog with no stories: the planner drafts them`; and an epic not done or cancelled whose stories, archived ones counted, are all done or cancelled with at least one done, `every story done or cancelled (N done, M cancelled) while the epic is <status>: the planner drafts what its outcome still lacks`. Since S-0328 ([ADR-0119](../adrs/0119-with-plan-backlog-stories-on-the-orchestrator-asks-the-planner-for-each-backlog.md)) the stories follow: a story in the backlog, draft or not, with no touches, no forecast duration, no cost of delay value, or no task that is not cancelled (`storyPlanLacks`), `in the backlog without a plan: no touches; no tasks`, naming what it lacks. Archived epics and stories are not judged. It lists apart, under `left out`, an epic or a story that would be one but whose planner runs for it now, or whose newest planner run ended `asked` on a thread still open with the planner's entry last, from flai serve's record of runs on this host (`planHeld` over `AgentState.Plans`). It leaves out a story too while a planner runs for its epic, and when its newest planner run ended and the story has not been updated since, `its planner ran until <time> and it has not changed since`, so that a planner that could not finish is not started again until something changes. It takes no item, starts nothing, and writes nothing. Its text begins `to plan:`, or says `nothing to plan`. `--json` prints `{ candidates: [{ id, type, title, status, reason }], left_out: [...] }`, `type` being `epic` or `story`. No MCP tool or hostapi read gives it; the orchestrator runs it in its shell, and `flai guard` passes it to a sub-agent, the planner, and the orchestrator as a read (`guard.flagReads`). |
| `flai analyze [--focus bottlenecks\|intent\|risk] [--json]` (S-0223, [ADR-0099](../adrs/0099-the-analyzer-runs-behind-the-analyze-host-action-and-writes-one-report-under.md)) | Starts the analyzer for the project now, on the operator's word; [strategic-agents.md](strategic-agents.md#the-analyzer) has the whole of it. `serve.Analyze` (`internal/serve/analyze.go`) makes the checks in `analyzeCheck`, which refuses, as `serve.Refused` and `rule:` on the command line, while the `analyze` host action is off for the project (`AgentConfig.Analyze`), for a focus that is not empty and none of `harness.Focuses` (`bottlenecks`, `intent`, `risk`), while the project's newest analyzer run is `running()`, naming its focus, trigger, PID, start, and log, and when the analyzer's agent names no harness and no command is set. The agent is `Manifest.AnalysisAgent`, `analysis.agent` merged over `agent` with `Agent.With`. The launcher's `analyze` builds a `harness.Request` with `Role` `analyze` (`conventions.RoleAnalyze`), `Focus`, and neither `Item` nor `Story`, names the run `analyzer` (`workitem.ActivityAnalyzer`), records its `Focus`, the focus or `harness.AllFocus` (`all`), and its `Trigger`, `asked` (`serve.TriggerAsked`), and starts it in the main checkout through `spawn` with `FLAI_ROLE=analyze` from `roleEnv`; `agentEnv` drops `FLAI_FOCUS` with the other `runMarks`. `roleEnv` refuses an analyzer given a story, an item, or a focus none of `harness.Focuses`, and a focus on any other request. The log is `serve/agents/<key>-analyzer-<start>.log`. The run is `AgentState.Analyzer` (`analyzer` in `serve/agents.json`, the newest alone; `AgentRun.analyzes` when it has neither story nor item and has a focus, and `AgentRun.orchestrates` now needs the focus empty), never in `Running` or `Last`, counted by no limit. `handOver` is set: the command does not wait, and the serving launcher settles a gone run in `settleOrphans`. `analyzeEnded` records its report (`AgentRun.Report`, relative to the root) as `newestReport` finds it: of the Markdown files directly in `analysis.Dir`, `README.md` aside, the one changed last, if no earlier than the second the run started. The outcome is `failed` on a non-zero exit, `failed` with `why` naming the folder when there is no report, and `worked` otherwise, an unseen exit included. It calls `logRunEndSaying` for `analyzer` with the run's trigger and `reportSaid`, which keeps the first line of the run's final text when it names the report, adds `(report <path>)` when it does not, and `(no report written under <folder>)` when there is none. Its items are the issues that name the report (`issues.NamingReport`, from `issues.Reports`, in ID order, open or closed), which `activityMeasure.charge` splits the cost between as it does an `activity_log` call's (S-0227); with no report, or when the issues cannot be read, which is warned of as `issues naming the analyzer's report not looked for`, the entry names none and charges none. Each start and failure to start is a journal entry with action `analyze` and method `serve.analyze`; `launcher.changed` gets the run, and `serve.Run` notifies the dashboard with `agent` and `{project, role: analyze}`; `agent.status` carries the run as `analyzer`. `claude-code` names the session `<key> analyze` and the focus, adds the project's `.claude/agents/analyzer.md` to `--agents` (`claudeCodeStrategic`, with the analyzer's agent's model when it names one; a missing definition refuses the start, naming `flai upgrade`), and selects it with `--agent analyzer`; `command` gets `FLAI_ROLE`, `FLAI_FOCUS` when there is a focus, and an empty `{story}`. `harness.Prompt` sends `analyzePrompt`. With no `flai serve` running the command says on standard error that the end is recorded and the report and cost logged once one runs. `--json` prints `{focus, agent, harness, command, pid, log, session, started, trigger}`. `serve.analysisScheduler` (`internal/serve/analysis_schedule.go`) starts the same run on `analysis.schedule` (`Analysis.AnalysisSchedule`, parsed by `internal/cron` as `planning.schedule` is): `serve.Run` makes one per served project beside the replanner and the orchestrator and calls its `look` with theirs. While the action is on and the schedule is due (`cadence`, which the replanner now shares), it makes the same checks with no focus and starts the analyzer with the trigger `schedule <spec>` (`scheduleTrigger`), logging a refusal at info as `scheduled analyzer not started`; while the action is off it moves its mark to now, and a schedule that is not valid is warned of once. |
| `flai curate [--since <day or time>] [--json]` (S-0431, [ADR-0196](../adrs/0196-the-curator-runs-behind-the-curate-host-action-and-takes-in-what-the-archive.md)) | Starts the curator for the project now, on the operator's word; [strategic-agents.md](strategic-agents.md#the-curator) has the whole of it. `serve.Curate` (`internal/serve/curate.go`) makes the checks in `curateCheck`, which refuses, as `serve.Refused` and `rule:` on the command line, while the `curate` host action is off for the project (`AgentConfig.Curate`), for a since that is neither a `YYYY-MM-DD` day nor an RFC 3339 time (`sinceReads`), while the project's newest curator run is `running()`, naming its since, trigger, PID, start, and log, and when the curator's agent names no harness and no command is set. The agent is `Manifest.DigestAgent`, `digest.agent` merged over `agent` with `Agent.With`. With no `--since`, the since is the time of the newest entry in `wip/agents/curator.md`, in UTC (`lastCurated`), or none on the first run. The launcher's `curate` builds a `harness.Request` with `Role` `curate` (`conventions.RoleCurate`), `Since`, and neither `Item` nor `Story`, names the run `curator` (`workitem.ActivityCurator`), records its `Role`, `Since`, and `Trigger`, `asked` (`serve.TriggerAsked`), and starts it in the main checkout through `spawn` with `FLAI_ROLE=curate` from `roleEnv`. `roleEnv` refuses a curator given a story, an item, or a since in neither form, and a since on any other request. The log is `serve/agents/<key>-curator-<start>.log`. The run is `AgentState.Curator` (`curator` in `serve/agents.json`, the newest alone, with no past runs; `AgentRun.curates` when its `role` is `curate`, and `AgentRun.orchestrates` and `analyzes` now need the role empty), never in `Running` or `Last`, counted by no limit. `handOver` is set: the command does not wait, and the serving launcher settles a gone run in `settleOrphans`. `curateEnded` records its entries (`AgentRun.Entries`) as `changedEntries` finds them: the IDs of the `D-nnnn-<slug>.md` files directly in the digest folder, the README aside, changed no earlier than the second the run started, in number order. The outcome is `failed` on a non-zero exit and `worked` otherwise, an unseen exit included. It calls `logRunEnd` for `curator` with the run's trigger and the entries as the items, which `activityMeasure.charge` charges nothing; entries that cannot be looked for are warned of as `curator's entries not looked for`, and the entry names none. Each start and failure to start is a journal entry with action `curate` and method `serve.curate`; `serve.Run` notifies the dashboard with `agent` and `{project, role: curate}`; `agent.status` carries the run as `curator`. `claude-code` names the session `<key> curate`, adds the project's `.claude/agents/curator.md` to `--agents` (`claudeCodeStrategic`; a missing definition refuses the start, naming `flai upgrade`), and selects it with `--agent curator`; `command` gets `FLAI_ROLE`, `FLAI_SINCE` when there is a since, and an empty `{story}`. `harness.Prompt` sends `curatePrompt`. With no `flai serve` running the command says on standard error that the end is recorded and the entries and cost logged once one runs. `--json` prints `{since, agent, harness, command, pid, log, session, started, trigger}`. Since S-0432 ([ADR-0199](../adrs/0199-the-curator-runs-after-acceptances-coalesced-and-on-digest-schedule-marks.md)) `serve.curateScheduler` (`internal/serve/curate_schedule.go`) starts the same run on its own: `serve.Run` makes one per served project beside the analysis scheduler and calls its `look` with theirs. While the action is on, it starts the curator, with no since, after the checks `curateCheck` makes, when a look's `news` show a story moved to `done`, with the trigger `accepted S-nnnn`; when stories were accepted while a curator ran, at the look its end brings, with every one in ID order, `accepted S-nnnn, S-nnnn` (`accepted`); and when `digest.schedule` (`Digest.DigestSchedule`, parsed as `analysis.schedule` is) is due (`cadence`), with `schedule <spec>` (`scheduleTrigger`). Acceptances and the schedule at one look start one run, with the acceptances' trigger. A start the checks refuse is logged at info as `curator not started on its own` with the trigger and the reason, and not tried again. While the action is off it keeps up with the acceptances and the schedule and starts nothing. What it has seen is kept in memory only. The journal entry's detail names the run's trigger (`startedBy`). |
| `flai agent`, `agent set [--harness] [--model] [--provider] [--config key=value]... [--unset key]... [--replace] [--autocommit] [--trailer]`, `agent clear [--autocommit] [--trailer]` (S-0103, [ADR-0037](../adrs/0037-a-story-carries-its-agent-copied-from-the-project-s-default-when-it-is-made.md)) | The project's default agent in `system-flow.yaml` (`internal/manifest/agent.go`): shown, changed where given (config keys added or replaced, `--unset` removes one), or removed. `manifest.WriteAgent` rewrites the top-level `agent:` block alone and leaves the rest of the file as it was. Not `flai serve agent`, the host's command for starting an agent (S-0079). Stories made while a default is set get a copy (`Agent.With`: the story's own values win); `flai show` prints a story's agent. `--replace` makes the default exactly what is given; `--autocommit` commits `system-flow.yaml` alone (`chore: set the project's default agent`), unless `dashboard.autocommit` is false, and the JSON answer names the commit (S-0105, the dashboard's settings page). The planner's agent is `planning.agent` merged over this default (`Manifest.PlanningAgent`, S-0208); `flai agent` does not change it, `flai manifest set` does, and `flai check` reports one that is not valid as `manifest.planning`. `--provider` (S-0349, [ADR-0129](../adrs/0129-a-story-s-agent-names-a-provider-beside-its-harness-and-model-and-the-providers.md)) names the host's provider the model's calls go to, checked for its shape only, a name such as `litellm`: whether the host has the entry is the start's to refuse. |
| `flai manifest set <key>=<value>... [--unset <key>]... [--autocommit] [--trailer]... [--json]` (S-0229, [ADR-0101](../adrs/0101-with-the-settings-host-action-on-the-dashboard-edits-the-strategic-agents.md)) | Writes the strategic agents' settings in `system-flow.yaml` (`cmd/manifest.go`, `manifest.WriteSettings` in `internal/manifest/settings.go`): `orchestration.permissions.*`, `orchestration.policy`, `orchestration.release.*`, `planning.agent`, `replan`, `schedule`, `hour_rate`, `cycle`, and `default_duration`, `analysis.agent` and `schedule`, since S-0431 `digest.agent`, `tests`, and, since S-0392, `acceptance.mode`, `forge`, and `merge_method`. Its help lists each with its kind, values, and default, from the catalog `manifest.Settings`, which `settings.get` and the dashboard's panels read too. A value is given as written: a boolean, digits, a Go duration, a cron expression or `daily`, a choice, a line of text, or an agent as JSON (`{}` unsets it); `--unset` removes a key so that its default applies. Each key's line or block is replaced or added in place, keeping the rest of the file, the order, and the comments. The manifest is checked whole, as it would be, with `flai check`'s validation, and anything wrong, a key outside the catalog, `planning.currency` (a hand edit, since changing it re-denominates every amount), a value not of its kind, or a problem the result would have, is refused with each field and its reason, writing nothing, exit 1, the message after `rule:`; with `--json` the refusal is `{"refused": [{field, reason}]}` on standard output and a change `{set, unset, commit?}`. `--autocommit` commits `system-flow.yaml` alone, `chore: change the manifest's settings: <keys>`, unless `dashboard.autocommit` is false. The host API's `settings.manifest` runs it for the dashboard. The keys and the rules are in [project-manifest.md](project-manifest.md). |
| `flai forge check [--json]` (S-0392, [ADR-0165](../adrs/0165-a-project-chooses-local-or-pull-request-acceptance-in-its-manifest-flai-reaches.md)) | Says whether the forge token this host names can read and push to the project's repository (`cmd/forge.go`), so that the token can be checked before a project switches to `acceptance.mode: pull-request`. It reads the forge from `acceptance.forge` under either mode, the repository from the `origin` remote (`git remote get-url origin`, `forge.ParseGitHubRemote`: https, `git@github.com:owner/name`, or `ssh://`, on github.com only), and the token through `config.ForgeTokenFunc`, then asks the adapter's `Access`. It prints `forge`, `repository` (from origin), the variable the token is read from, and, once the forge answered, `read` and `push`, `yes` or `no`; the token is never printed. It exits 1 with the reason, after what it learned, when there is no origin, origin is not a repository on the forge, the variable is unset or empty, the forge does not accept the token (401), the repository is not found or not visible to the token (404), the forge refuses the token or has rate-limited it (403), or the token can read but not push, each naming the variable and the fix. `--json` is `{forge, repository, token_env, read, push, ok, reason}`. See [Reaching the forge](#reaching-the-forge). |
| `flai forge push <story> [--json]` (S-0393, [ADR-0166](../adrs/0166-under-pull-request-acceptance-flai-serve-pushes-a-story-s-branch-and-opens-or.md)) | Does for one story what `flai serve`'s forge pass does at each look, on the operator's word, whether the `forge` host action is on or not (`serve.PushStory`, `cmd/forge.go`): pushes `story/S-nnnn` and opens or updates its pull request, reading the token through `config.ForgeTokenFunc` from this shell's environment, and records what it did in `.flai-cache/forge/S-nnnn.json`, where `flai forge status` reads it. It needs `acceptance.mode: pull-request` and the story in review. It prints `story`, `outcome` (`pushed`, or `up-to-date` when origin had the local head), the branch it pushed and whether with the lease, and `pull request: #<n> <opened\|updated\|unchanged>, <url>`. It exits 1 with the reason and what clears it when it did nothing (the mode is `local`, the story is not in review) or the push or the request failed, after printing what it did before the failure. `--json` is the recorded state, `{story, outcome, reason, at, local_head, remote_head, last_pushed, pushed_at, forced, pull_request, url, request, thread, thread_reason}`, empty fields left out. `flai guard` refuses it to every agent (`guard.forgePushes`); `flai forge check` and `status` read and stay open to them. |
| `flai forge status <story> [--json]` (S-0393, [ADR-0166](../adrs/0166-under-pull-request-acceptance-flai-serve-pushes-a-story-s-branch-and-opens-or.md)) | Says what flai last did for a story's pull request and why nothing was pushed (`cmd/forge.go`), without calling the forge, so it works offline and costs no rate limit. It reads `.flai-cache/forge/S-nnnn.json` and asks origin for the branch with `git ls-remote origin`, and prints the story and its status, the mode and forge, whether the `forge` host action is on, the token's variable and whether it is set in this shell (never the token), the pull request's number and URL or `none yet`, whether origin's branch matches the local head, the last look's outcome and time, and, when nothing was pushed or the branch no longer matches, `why`: the first of the mode is `local`, the host action is off, the variable is unset, the last push failed (its reason, time, and thread), the story is not in review, or nothing stops `flai serve`, which pushes it at its next look. It exits 0 whenever it can report. `--json` is `{story, status, mode, forge, host_action, token_env, token_set, pull_request, url, branch, local_head, remote_head, remote_error, matches, outcome, reason, at, thread, why}`, empty fields left out but for the booleans. Since S-0394 ([ADR-0167](../adrs/0167-flai-serve-reads-each-pull-request-s-required-checks-and-reviews-into-kept.md)), once flai serve has read the pull request back, it prints under the pull request line `read at <head>, <time>` and the check and review lines `flai show` prints; `--json` adds `pull_request_read`, what `flai show --json` gives as `pull_request`, since `pull_request` is the number here. |
| `flai hostapi [method] [params-json]` (S-0073) | The table of methods `flai serve` offers a dashboard (`internal/hostapi`), answered on a terminal for the project in the working directory: no arguments lists them; a method, with its params as a JSON object, prints the result as JSON, or `{"error": {code, message}}` with exit 2. So far `project.info` (the manifest's name, key, owner, layout, template version, sub-projects, and the dashboard settings the server acts on), `board.get` (`flai board --json`, `all` adds epics and tasks; since S-0388 a story's card carries `undoes_main`, the paths its record at `.flai-cache/undone/S-nnnn.json` says its branch still undoes of main's change (ADR-0155), sorted, read by `storygit.RecordedUndone` only for a story ready, in progress, or in review with a record, so a board with none runs no git; left out when there are none, and a record that cannot be read or checked is logged and left out, the board standing; since S-0398 `queue`, the merge queue, as on `flai board --json`), `items.list` (by `type` and `status`, `archived` and `bodies` on request), since S-0162 `items.count` (`{active, archived}`, how many of each, for a page that shows the archive's size and not the archive), `item.get` (any padding, with children, archive included; since S-0388 an open story's answer carries `undoes_main` as `board.get`'s card does, left out when there are none), `threads.list` (`on` a path or an item in any padding, `all` adds the resolved), since S-0336 `messages.list` (`story` in any padding, `all` adds the closed: the conversations between stories' agents as `flai message list --json` prints them, shaped by `messages.View`; a `story` that is not a story's ID is invalid params) and `messages.get` (`id` in any padding: one conversation with its entries, as `flai message show --json` prints it; an ID that is not a conversation's is invalid params, and one that does not exist is -32004), both read-only, and since S-0074 `docs.tree` (the manifest's three folders, each Markdown file with its title; since S-0162 without its front matter, which `doc.get` reads, and with each title kept by file while the file has the same identity, modification time, and size, so a warm answer reads no file), `doc.get` (one Markdown file under those folders, by its real location: front matter, body, raw), `adrs.list`, `activity.get` (each narrative's agent, session, the story's state, blocked, the task in progress, the last log entry), `inbox.designer` (what needs a human: threads awaiting the designer, open questions, stories in review, blocked items, the check's overlap findings, from the `wip.overlap` rule alone and not the whole check since S-0158; keys as the dashboard always made them, the item or path in place of a link; not the agent's MCP `inbox`), `search.query` (`q`, `docs`, `limit` to 100; see `design/tech/search.md`), since S-0381 `design.get` (no params; what `flai design show --json` prints, `designsystem.Show` on the manifest's `design.tokens` and `design/system/design-system.md`; with no token file, `exists` is false and `no_token_file` says so and names `flai design init`; since S-0384 it answers `stylesheet`, the manifest's `design.stylesheet`, left out when unset, and takes `{story}`: what `flai design show --story` prints, the design system in that open story's worktree, an ID no item has -32004, a refusal invalid params, and a worktree that cannot be read an internal error; flaiover's `REQUIRED_METHODS` lists it; see [Design system](#design-system)), since S-0384 `design.proposals` (no params; the list `flai design proposals --json` prints; flaiover's `REQUIRED_METHODS` lists it), and since S-0075 the dashboard's writes and the commands it only reads from: `item.move`, `item.move.preview`, `item.order`, since S-0167 `board.limit`, `item.block`, `item.unblock`, `item.new`, `item.template`, `accept.preview`, `accept.run`, `stream.diff`, `stream.log`, since S-0371 `stream.restore`, `thread.new`, `thread.reply` (since S-0220 with `recommend`, a bool, and `source`, `<path>` or `<path>#<heading>`, becoming `--recommend` and `--source=`), since S-0220 `thread.confirm` (`{id}`: `flai thread confirm <id> --by=<owner>`), `thread.resolve`, `doc.show`, `doc.save`, since S-0436 `file.get` (`{path, story?}`: `flai file show`) and `file.save` (`{path, content, hash, story?, message?}`: `flai file save`, ungated as `doc.save` is, exit 3 a conflict and exit 4 a refusal), each refusing as invalid params, before flai runs, a path `flai file` does not reach (`fileRel`), and both in flaiover's `REQUIRED_METHODS`, `adr.new`, `adr.template`, `adr.accept`, `stats.get`, since S-0087 `publish.preview`, `publish.run` (`push.pending` and `push.run`, from S-0078, were removed in S-0195, ADR-0067), since S-0217 `order.by`, `promote.candidates`, and `release.evaluate` (the reads of `flai order --by`, `flai promote --candidates`, and `flai release --evaluate`, which flaiover's `REQUIRED_METHODS` lists), and since S-0081 `dashboard.status`, `dashboard.check`, `dashboard.restart`, `dashboard.upgrade`, `dashboard.stop`, and since S-0082 `checks.status`, `checks.tail`, `checks.run`, `checks.cancel`, and since S-0116 `agent.restart` (`{id}`: `flai serve agent restart <id>`, gated by `agent`), and since S-0115 `agent.start` (`{id}`: `flai serve agent start <id>`, gated the same), and since S-0140 `agent.commit` (`{id}`: `flai serve agent commit <id>`, gated the same), and since S-0170 `agent.stop` (`{id}`: `flai serve agent stop <id>`, gated the same), and since S-0208 `plan.run` (`{id}`: `flai plan <id> --json`, gated on `plan`), and since S-0223 `analyze.run` (`{focus?}`: `flai analyze --json`, with `--focus=<focus>` when one is given, gated on `analyze`; a focus none of `bottlenecks`, `intent`, and `risk` is refused before anything runs, and `describeAnalyze` journals the run started), and since S-0228 `orchestrate.stop` and `orchestrate.start` (`{}`: `flai serve orchestrate stop` or `start`, gated on `orchestrate`, and `describeOrchestrate` journals what it did), and since S-0142 `agent.stream` (`{story, after?}`: a read of the story's agent's stream, as `flai serve agent stream --json` prints it, read-only; `{plan}` or `{role}` in place of `story` since S-0259 and S-0228), and since S-0090 `stream.answer`, and since S-0271 `stream.state` (`{id, current?, next?}`: `flai stream state <id> --current=<text> --next=<text> --json`, each text trimmed and one left out when blank; neither text, a text of only `-`, or an ID that is not a story is invalid params (-32602), and flai's refusal (exit 4) is Refused (-32010) with the refused object as data; flaiover's `REQUIRED_METHODS` lists it, but the dashboard does not call it, and ticks criteria through `item.criteria`), and since S-0106 `host.status` and `host.check` (reads of `flai host status` and `check`) and `host.process` (`{process, action}`: `flai host <action> <process>`) and `host.upgrade`, gated on `host` and detached from the request (`detachTimeout`), since a restart of serve ends the connection they came on, and since S-0298 ([ADR-0117](../adrs/0117-the-host-api-installs-a-release-the-dashboard-names-only-when-flai-lists-it-as.md)) the reads `host.versions` (`flai host versions --json`) and `dashboard.versions` (`flai dashboard versions --json`), not gated, and an optional `version` on `host.upgrade` (`flai host upgrade --version=<version>`) and `tag` on `dashboard.upgrade` (`flai dashboard upgrade --published --tag=<tag>`), each a bare `X.Y.Z`: anything else is invalid params (-32602) before a command runs, a release that is not published is the command's refusal, naming the published ones, and the image name is never taken from the request, and since S-0105 `settings.get` (read: every host action and where it is on, the default agent, the agent's name, minutes, and command, each harness, the checks and time limit, the manifest's checks, the import folders, the MCP server's address; whether `settings` is on here and everywhere; never a token) and the writes `settings.action`, `settings.default_agent`, `settings.agent`, `settings.harness`, `settings.check`, `settings.checks_timeout`, `settings.import`, `settings.mcp_token`, and `settings.dashboard_token`, and since S-0122 `settings.serve` and `settings.unserve` (`{root, key?}`: `flai serve project add -- <root>`, or `remove`, gated by the settings action of the project served or removed, not of the one the request arrives through, and journalled under it), while `settings.get`'s `host.projects` carries `flai serve project list` without the dashboard's address or the credential's path: `running`, each `served` project with `from`, `below` (the import folder or start folder it is served from), `state`, `since`, `last_error`, `reason`, and `settings` (whether the action is on for it), and each `unserved` one below an import folder with why. The last five named for every project are `hostwide`: refused unless `settings` is on for every project, with `Data{enable: "flai serve enable settings --all-projects", hostwide: true}`. Since S-0489 the write `settings.dashboard_hosts` (`{hosts}`) is host-wide too: it runs `flai config set -- dashboard.allowed_hosts <hosts>` ([Hosts the dashboard accepts](#hosts-the-dashboard-accepts)). Each is journalled as the flai command it ran (`say`), trailers left out. Each of these validates its arguments, builds a command line in which every value is `--flag=value` or follows `--`, and runs this flai in the project's folder with `--json`, reading the result, the warnings, and the exit code as the dashboard used to (`internal/hostapi/writes.go`): a rule is -32011, a conflict -32009 and a refusal -32010 with their data. Nine of them (six from S-0195, when it removed `push.pending`, until S-0217) are reads a command also prints and are answered in `flai serve`'s own process since S-0159, from the code the command prints with, starting no flai (`internal/hostapi/reads.go`): `item.show` (`itemedit.Show`), `item.move.preview` and `accept.preview` (`internal/preview`, whose dry runs `flai move` and `flai accept` start their real runs from), `stream.diff` (`internal/storygit`), `stats.get` (`metrics`; `since`, `type`, `by`, and since S-0163 `bucket`), `publish.preview` (`internal/preview`, over the items and git history the process keeps), and since S-0217 `order.by` (`workitem.ReadyOrderByPolicy`; `policy`, the manifest's when empty; it never applies), `promote.candidates` (`workitem.PromotionCandidates`; `limit`), and `release.evaluate` (`release.EvaluateRepo`). Each answers as its command did: the command's JSON as data, the warnings it logged, and a failure read as the process's was, so a missing item is -32004 and a remote that moved is -32009; a contract test in `cmd` runs each command with `--json` and the read on one fixture and compares them. Who acted is the manifest's owner. A write needs a `request_id` (8 to 64 characters) and a repeat of one within ten minutes is answered from a journal in memory. `accept.run` sends each log event as `$/progress`. Since S-0221 it also takes `by`, only `orchestrator`, `verified`, a commit in hex, and `evidence`, the text passed on standard input as `--evidence=-` ([ADR-0093](../adrs/0093-with-accept-reviews-on-the-orchestrator-accepts-a-story-in-review-through-flai.md)): `by: orchestrator` is refused as a rule while `orchestration.permissions.accept_reviews` is off (`orchestratorAccepts`), and `verified` or `evidence` without it as invalid params. Since S-0371 ([ADR-0155](../adrs/0155-a-rebased-story-branch-that-undoes-main-s-change-to-a-path-is-recorded-at-sync.md)) `accept.run` takes `keep_removals`, each `{path, reason}`, passed as `--keep-removal` and `--reason` pairs in order; a path that is not inside the checkout (`undonePath`: absolute, with `..`, starting with `-`, or holding a line break), a blank reason, and any keep with `by: orchestrator` are invalid params, and flai's refusal of a path still undone (exit 4) is Refused with `{id, reason, undoes_main}` as data. `accept.preview` gives `undoes_main` as `flai accept --dry-run --json` does. The write `stream.restore` (`{id, paths}`) runs `flai stream restore <id> --path=<path>... --trailer=<the dashboard's>`, each path checked as `keep_removals`' are; an ID that is not a story's or no paths is invalid params, and flai's refusal (exit 4) is Refused with the refused object as data. The review page's cards and the board's badge that call them are a later story (S-0388). A test fails when a method has no valid call and no refusals listed. Paths in answers are relative to the repository; an ID, a type, or a state that is not one is refused as invalid params (-32602), a missing item is -32004. `project.info` carries the default `agent`; `item.new` takes a story's `agent` and `item.edit` an `agent` that replaces the story's, or `null` to remove it, each value checked as `manifest.Agent.Validate` does before it becomes `--harness=`, `--model=`, and `--agent-config=` (S-0103). The planning data (S-0199): `item.new` takes a story's `draft` (`--draft`); `item.edit` takes `draft` (`--draft` or `--no-draft`), `cost_of_delay` (`revenue_per_week`, `penalty_per_week`, `time_lost_per_cycle`, `value`) and `forecast` (`duration`, `delivery`, `basis`) as strings, each given key becoming its flag and an empty one removing the key, and `clear_cost_of_delay` and `clear_forecast`, refused beside the block they clear; `item.move` takes `finalize`, which on a move to ready adds `--yes`, the designer's say that a draft is finished. `item.finalize` (S-0201, [ADR-0077](../adrs/0077-a-story-that-was-a-draft-records-who-finalized-it-and-when-and-the-dashboard.md)) takes `{id}` and runs `flai edit <id> --no-draft --by=<owner> --autocommit`, committed as `item.edit` is: an ID that is not a story is invalid params, and a story the edit leaves unchanged, which was not a draft, is refused as a rule (`Rule`), so a second finalize does not overwrite the first's record. `item.criteria` (S-0282, [ADR-0089](../adrs/0089-acceptance-criteria-are-ticked-through-flai-by-number-by-the-story-s-agent-once.md)) takes `{id, hash, tick \| untick}` and runs `flai criteria tick` or `untick <id> <n>,... --hash --by=<owner> --autocommit`, one or the other per call; a stale hash is a conflict (exit 3) and an archived or closed item is refused (exit 4). `task.done` (S-0269, [ADR-0107](../adrs/0107-flai-task-done-closes-a-task-in-one-call-commit-sync-move-log-widen-touches.md)) takes `{id, message?, log?}` and runs `flai task done <id> [-m <message>] [--log <log>] --json`, passing the message only when one is given, since a close with nothing to commit needs none (S-0322, [ADR-0128](../adrs/0128-flai-task-done-commits-the-paths-the-closing-task-covers-and-those-no-other.md)); a stopped sync (exit 3) is a conflict (-32009) and a failing check (exit 4) is refused (-32010), each with the result as data. `story.start` (S-0274) takes `{story, budget?}` and runs `flai story start <story> [--budget=<budget>] --json`, as `flaiover`, the agent every write runs flai as, answering its result as it is; an ID that is not a story or a budget that is not a size is invalid params, flai's refusal (exit 4: not ready, not a story, in progress already, or held) is Refused (-32010) with the refused object as data and nothing changed, and a step that failed after the move (exit 3) is an internal error (-32603) in flai's words, naming the step and the command that finishes it, with the result as data. `issue.new` (`{title, class, story?, cost?, note?}`), `issue.bump` (`{id, story?, cost?, note?}`), and `issue.close` (`{id, reason}`) (S-0275) run `flai issue new`, `bump`, or `close` in the main checkout with `--autocommit`, the dashboard's `--trailer=`, and `--json`, so each commits there on its own and widens no story's touches; `--commit` is the story's agent's, in its worktree. An empty or over-long title, a class not in `issues.Classes`, an ID that is not an issue's or a story's, a cost that is not a duration, or an empty reason is invalid params (-32602); flai's refusal (exit 4) is Refused (-32010). The answer is the issue as `--json` gives it, with `committed`, `commit`, and `commit_error`. flaiover's `REQUIRED_METHODS` lists the three. The shared paths (S-0295, [ADR-0096](../adrs/0096-a-story-in-review-holds-nothing-an-overlap-inside-the-manifest-s-shared-paths.md)): `settings.get` carries the manifest's `claims.shared` patterns; `settings.shared_check` (`{paths}` or `{story}`), a read gated like `settings.get`, answers what `flai shared check --json` answers; and `settings.shared` (`{action: add \| remove, pattern}`), a write gated by the `settings` host action, runs `flai shared add` or `remove`, answering with what changed, or with flai's refusal of a duplicate, a missing, or an invalid pattern. The strategic agents' settings (S-0229, [ADR-0101](../adrs/0101-with-the-settings-host-action-on-the-dashboard-edits-the-strategic-agents.md)): `settings.get` carries `host.strategic`, `{editable, enable, settings}`, whether the `settings` action is on for the project, the command that turns it on, and each key `flai manifest set` writes with its value, `set`, kind, values, default, meaning, and for a permission its risk (`strategicSettings`, `Manifest.SettingValues`); and `settings.manifest` (`{set: {key: value}, unset: [key]}`), a write gated by the `settings` host action beside `settings.default_agent`, runs `flai manifest set --autocommit --trailer=<the dashboard's> --json`, each `--unset=<key>`, then `--` and each `key=value` in key order. Each key must be one of the catalog's, and each value a boolean, a number, written out in digits, or a line of text, or for an agent an object of `harness`, `model`, `config`, and `roles` that `manifest.Agent.Validate` passes, sent as compact JSON; anything else, `planning.currency` among them, is invalid params with `Data{refused: [{field, reason}]}` before anything runs. flai's refusal, exit 1, is answered as Refused (-32010) with the same `refused` data, and with the action off it is Disabled with what enables it. It is how the table is looked at and debugged, and how flaiover's tests read their fixtures |
| `flai dashboard token [--rotate] [--no-restart]` (E-0006, S-0105) | Print the dashboard token and login link; `--rotate` replaces it and restarts the container. With `--no-restart` the container keeps running on the old token until it is told the new one: `settings.dashboard_token` answers with it and the dashboard takes it (S-0105). |
| `flai stream sync <story-id>` | Rebase `story/<id>` onto the main checkout's branch inside its worktree (S-0037). Agents run it after committing each task on the story branch and again before review, and never start a `git rebase` or `git merge` by hand ([ADR-0069](../adrs/0069-agents-commit-each-task-on-the-story-branch-after-flai-stream-sync-and-never.md), S-0197). It never stashes: `storygit.Sync` (`internal/storygit/sync.go`, out of `cmd` since S-0269; `flai accept` runs its rebase alone, `storygit.Rebase`) refuses, touching nothing, a worktree with uncommitted changes (`storygit.Uncommitted`), naming each path, to be committed (or stashed) before syncing again, or with a rebase already in progress (`storygit.RebaseInProgress`). A rebase that stops on conflicts is left in progress; `syncStopped` prints each conflicting path (`storygit.Conflicts`) on a line of its own, how to continue (in the worktree, resolve each path, `git add` it, `git rebase --continue`, then sync again) and how to abort (`git rebase --abort`, which puts the branch back as it was before the sync), and the command exits non-zero; `--json` gives `ok: false`, `worktree`, `uncommitted`, `conflicts`, `rebase_in_progress`, `continue`, and `abort`. A stop whose every conflicting path is a generated file is not left (S-0278, [ADR-0098](../adrs/0098-flai-stream-sync-and-flai-accept-regenerate-design-issues-summary-md-when-a.md), refining ADR-0069): `generatedFiles` in `cmd/branch.go` lists them, `design/issues/summary.md` alone today, passed to the sync as `SyncOptions.Generated`, and storygit's `continueOverGenerated` writes each again from the issue files in the worktree as they are at that stop (`issues.WriteSummary`, so its `updated:` line is the time of the sync), `git add`s it, and continues with no editor (`storygit.ContinueRebase`), at each stop, until the rebase finishes or stops on another path. A stop where any other path also conflicts is left as above, listing every path, `summary.md` included, since its rows depend on issue files that may be among them. A file that cannot be regenerated or staged leaves the stop for the agent with a warning. A rebase found already in progress is never continued. Since S-0326 ([ADR-0126](../adrs/0126-a-rebase-of-a-story-branch-merges-an-issue-file-both-sides-changed-by-its.md), refining ADR-0085 and ADR-0098) the rebase also merges an issue file under `design/issues` that both sides changed, when it stops on one: the base's instances, then each instance either side added, in timestamp order; `count` adds both sides' increments, `cost` is the average of every occurrence, `last_reported` and `updated` take the later, and every other field and the rest of the body come from the side that changed them. When both sides changed one of those differently, it merges nothing and the stop is left for the agent. A stop whose every path is a merged issue file or a generated file is continued, `summary.md` written again after the merge. Once the rebase is clean, each open issue the branch added whose title an open issue on the main branch also has is folded into that issue: its instances, count, and cost move in as the merge adds them, the branch's file is deleted, `summary.md` is written again, and the fold is committed on the branch as `docs: [S-nnnn] fold I-x into I-y`. A closed issue is never folded into. The output prints a line for each issue merged and each folded, with the issue it went into, and `--json` gives them as `merged`, the paths of the issue files merged, and `folded`, a list of `{from, into}` issue IDs. Since S-0306 ([ADR-0157](../adrs/0157-flai-stream-sync-and-flai-accept-merge-design-adrs-readme-md-s-index-row-by-row.md), refining ADR-0098 and ADR-0126) the rebase also merges `design/adrs/README.md` when it stops on it, from the stop's three versions (`internal/adrindex`, one more of the merged files in `issues.SyncFiles`): the text above the table, the header, and the text below each take the side that changed them; each row, keyed by the ADR file it links, takes the side that changed it, so rows both sides added are both kept and a note one side added to a row is kept; the rows come out in ADR number order. When both sides changed the same part or row differently, it merges nothing and the stop is left for the agent. A stop whose every path is the index, a merged issue file, or a generated file is continued. Since S-0401 ([ADR-0175](../adrs/0175-a-rebase-that-stops-on-a-markdown-file-inside-the-manifest-s-shared-paths.md), refining ADR-0098, ADR-0126, and ADR-0157) the rebase also merges, from the stop's three versions, a markdown file inside the manifest's `claims.shared` that none of those covers (`storygit.Files.Shared`, matched by `issues.FilesIn`'s `sharedMarkdown`), by sections (`storygit.MergeMarkdown`, `internal/storygit/mdmerge.go`): it cuts each version at its ATX headings outside fenced code into the front matter, keyed by its top-level keys, and sections keyed by their heading paths; a section one side changed takes that side, one both added appears once per side, ours first, and one both changed differently is merged line by line against its base, keeping changes to different base lines and, at one insertion point, ours' lines then theirs'; `updated` takes the later date. Ours is the branch rebased onto, so main's or the batch's lines come first. Both sides changing the same base lines, a section one side removed and the other changed, or another front matter key both changed differently answers `ErrNotSettled`, naming each section and key, and the stop is left for the agent with a warning. A stop whose every path is one of these or one of the files above is continued (`storygit.ContinueOverResolved`, which `flai stream sync`, `flai task done`, `flai accept`, and the merge queue's batch share), and `merged` lists the shared files too. After a clean rebase it runs the checks of [ADR-0046](../adrs/0046-a-ready-story-whose-claim-overlaps-an-open-story-s-is-held-yellow-and-with-its.md)'s safety net (S-0131, `internal/storygit/sync.go`): `checkSync` trial-merges the branch with the branch of every other story in progress or in review, in ID order, with `git merge-tree --write-tree --name-only --no-messages` in the main checkout (exit 1 is a conflict, and the lines after the tree are the paths; `withoutGenerated` drops the generated files from them, so a pair whose only conflict is `summary.md` is clean and gets no message, ADR-0098, and the issue files both branches changed are dropped too, since the rebase merges them, ADR-0126, as is `design/adrs/README.md`, so a pair whose only conflict is the ADR index is clean, ADR-0157, but the shared markdown files merged by sections stay among a pair's conflicts, since a section merge can fail, ADR-0175, and `changedByBoth` keeps only the paths both branches changed since they left main, each branch's `branchChanges`, `git diff --name-only <main>...story/<id>`, S-0251: the merge base of the two is the older branch's base, so a freshly synced branch carries what main gained since, and the trial merge also stops where main changed what the stale branch did, a conflict with main that the stale story's rebase settles, not one between the pair, [I-0064](../issues/I-0064-flai-stream-sync-s-trial-merge-blames-a-story-for-conflicts-between-main-and-another-story-s-stale-branch.md)), skipped with a warning below git 2.38 (`gitver.MergeTree`); and `outsideClaim` lists the paths of the syncing branch's `branchChanges` that no entry of `workitem.Holds.Claim` covers (equal, or below it), the wip folder left out. Since S-0332 ([ADR-0121](../adrs/0121-flai-tells-two-stories-of-a-trial-merge-conflict-or-a-grown-overlap-with-a.md)) `tellConflicts` tells each conflicting pair in its conversation, not a thread: a message from the syncing story to the other, by `flai`, `about` the conflicting paths, whose text (`storygit.ConflictText`) names both branches and the paths and not who synced, asks the two to agree who changes what, and says how to escalate. It goes to the pair's open conversation, or starts one (`messages.Notify`, shared with the notice at a task's close), unless flai's newest conflict message there has the same text. A clean merge, or the other story no longer in progress or in review, closes the story's open conversations with that story whose newest message by flai tells of a conflict, with the reason. `resolveConflictThreads` only resolves an open thread titled `S-nnnn and S-mmmm conflict when merged` that a sync before S-0332 opened, the same way, and mirrors the narratives; no sync opens one or adds to one. The output ends a conflicting pair's line with `; see MS-nnnn` and a clean pair's with `; resolved TH-nnnn` when it resolved an old thread. Neither check fails the sync; an error in them, or a message that cannot be written, is a warning. `--json` adds `branches` (`story`, `status`, `branch`, `clean`, `conflicts`, `conversation` on a conflicting pair, and `thread` only for an old conflict thread resolved), `outside_touches`, and `trial_merge_skipped`. Since S-0337 a rebase stopped on conflicts in `storygit.Rebase`, at sync or at `flai task done`, and each conflict message `tellConflicts` sends or changes append a `sync` or a `trial` line to the conflict log, `.flai-cache/conflicts.jsonl`, that `flai stats` counts under `coordination` ([ADR-0138](../adrs/0138-flai-stats-reports-per-week-the-conversations-between-agents-and-how-they-ended.md)). Since S-0367 ([ADR-0139](../adrs/0139-a-test-tier-s-fix-command-rewrites-the-files-a-story-changes-before-flai-task.md)) a clean rebase, after the fold, runs each test tier's `fix_command` over every file the branch changes against the main branch, deleted files left out (`storygit` `fix`, with `SyncOptions.Fix` calling `verify.CheckoutFix`). What it rewrote is committed on the branch as `chore: [S-nnnn] fix the lint of the files the branch changes, after the sync`, the output names the files, and `--json` gives them as `fixed`. Every clean sync does it, the close-out's included, so what the branch changes, and what the main branch brought into those files, is fixed before review. A fix command that fails, or files that cannot be listed, is a warning and the sync stands; a fix written but not committed is an error naming what to commit. Since S-0371 ([ADR-0155](../adrs/0155-a-rebased-story-branch-that-undoes-main-s-change-to-a-path-is-recorded-at-sync.md), [I-0124](../issues/I-0124-flai-stream-sync-replays-a-story-s-revert-of-its-own-change-over-an-identical-change-on-main-undoing-main-s.md)) `storygit.Rebase` reads the branch's merge base with main before `git rebase`, and after a clean rebase `checkUndone` runs `FindUndone` (`internal/storygit/undone.go`). For each path main changed since that old base and the rebased branch still changes against main, it compares the lines main added since the old base with the lines the branch's net change removes, as a multiset of line text. A path where the branch removes any of them undoes main's change, as a revert of the story's own change replayed cleanly over main's identical change does. The generated files and the files the rebase merged are left out, since flai writes them from both sides. An old base that is main's head, or that cannot be read, checks nothing. Each finding, its path, main's lines the branch removes, and the old base, is recorded with when it was found in `.flai-cache/undone/S-nnnn.json` in the main checkout, beside `flai verify`'s last result; the record is host-local. `Sync` then adds every path the record holds that the branch still undoes (`withRecordedUndone`), so a later sync, whose rebase starts past main's change and cannot find the path itself, still warns. `RecordedUndone` checks each recorded path against main now and drops one whose recorded lines the branch no longer removes, writing the record again without it. For each path, inside the story's claim or outside it, the output prints `story/S-nnnn removes <n> line(s) main added to <path> since <short base>; restore them with flai stream restore S-nnnn --path <path>, or keep the removal at acceptance with flai accept S-nnnn --keep-removal <path> --reason "<why>"`, and `--json` gives them as `undoes_main`, each `{path, lines, old_base}`. The sync still exits 0; a check or a record that fails is a warning and the sync stands. |
| `flai stream restore <story-id> --path <path>... [--trailer] [--json]` (S-0371, [ADR-0155](../adrs/0155-a-rebased-story-branch-that-undoes-main-s-change-to-a-path-is-recorded-at-sync.md)) | Commits the main branch's version of each `--path` onto `story/<id>` in the story's worktree, as one commit, `fix: [S-nnnn] restore main's <paths>, which the rebased branch undid (ADR-0155)`, with each `--trailer` (`restoreMain` in `cmd/stream_restore.go`: `git checkout <main> -- <path>` for each, then `storygit.CommitPaths`). It is for a path `flai stream sync` warns the branch undoes of main's change. Once committed, the branch no longer removes main's lines, so `RecordedUndone`, read again at once, drops the path from the story's record and it no longer blocks the acceptance. It is flai's git work, so ADR-0069's rule that agents never rebase or merge by hand stands. It refuses, changing nothing, with exit 4 (`restoreRefusal`): an item that is not a story, a story not in progress or in review, one with no worktree, a worktree with a rebase unfinished, with another branch checked out, or with uncommitted changes (each named), and a path the record does not hold (naming those it holds). A git step that fails after the worktree is written puts the paths back as the branch has them; a restore that changes nothing is an error. With `--json` it prints `story`, `branch`, `commit`, and `paths`, and a refusal as `{"refused": {"story", "reason", ...}}` with `status`, `uncommitted`, `not_held`, or `held` where they apply. The dashboard runs it through the host write `stream.restore`. |
| `flai story start <story> [--budget SIZE] [--json]` (S-0274) | Starts a ready story in one call, in place of the four a story's agent made: `flai move S-nnnn in-progress`, `flai stream open S-nnnn`, `flai prime --story S-nnnn`, and the MCP tool `inbox`. One package, `internal/storystart`, does it for this command, the MCP tool `story_start`, and the host channel's `story.start`. It first refuses, changing nothing (`storystart.Refused`): an ID that is not a story; a story in progress already, naming `flai stream open` and `flai prime --story` to take it up; a story in any other state but ready; and a ready story the board holds, its claim overlapping a story in progress or in review or its `after` naming a story not done (`Repo.Holds`, as `inbox` and `wait_for_work` judge it, S-0128). An unknown ID is an error. Then, in order: move (the story to in-progress under `flai move`'s rules, `Repo.TransitionAll`, as `FLAI_AGENT`, its epic following; a warning, such as a WIP limit passed, is printed and the move goes on); stream (the narrative opened, or taken up with `Repo.ReopenStream` when it was begun before, the stream index written again, and in a git repository `story/<id>` checked out in its worktree by `storygit.Open`, as `flai stream open` does); prime (the story's pack, `ctxpack.ForStory`, fitted to `--budget`, else `prime.budget`, else 80KB); inbox (the agent's inbox, `internal/inbox`, advancing the cursor the MCP tool `inbox` advances). A step after the move that fails is a `storystart.StepError`: the story stays in progress, what the steps before it did is printed, and the error names the step and the command that finishes it (`flai stream open S-nnnn, then flai prime --story S-nnnn`, `flai prime --story S-nnnn`, or the MCP tool `inbox`). The text is the move with any epic that followed and the warnings, the narrative, the branch, where it came from, and its worktree (or that the project is not a git work tree), the pack as `flai prime --story` prints it, and the inbox. `--json` prints `story` (`id`, `title`, `status`, `warnings`), `followed` (as `flai move --json` has it), `worktree` (absolute), `branch`, `from` (`local`, the remote's name, or `main`, as `flai stream open --json` has it), `pack` (the whole pack, as `flai prime --story --json` prints it), and `inbox` (as the MCP tool `inbox` answers it); the three git keys are empty outside git. Since S-0450 ([ADR-0179](../adrs/0179-task-done-and-story-start-carry-an-inbox-that-lists-the-story-s-threads-and.md)) the inbox is read with its story filter set to the story: it lists in full only the open threads on the story and its tasks, `awaiting_you` counting those, and counts the threads on other items in `threads_omitted`; `--json` is fitted to 40,000 bytes of JSON as the MCP tool's answer is (see `flai mcp`). A refusal with `--json` is `{"refused": {story, status, hold, reason}}`, `hold` the board's hold (`code`, `reason`) or null. Exit 0 when the story started, 4 when it was refused, 3 when it moved and a later step failed (`--json` prints what the steps before it did), and 1 when the start could not run. The MCP tool `story_start` answers the same keys, its pack fitted to one tool result (see `flai mcp`), and the host method `story.start` runs `flai story start <story> --json` (see `flai hostapi`). The prompt `flai serve` gives a story's agent, the MCP server's instructions, and the conventions start a story with it. |
| `flai task done <task> [-m "<message>"] [--log "<entry>"] [--json]` (S-0269, [ADR-0107](../adrs/0107-flai-task-done-closes-a-task-in-one-call-commit-sync-move-log-widen-touches.md), refining ADR-0069; S-0322, [ADR-0128](../adrs/0128-flai-task-done-commits-the-paths-the-closing-task-covers-and-those-no-other.md)) | Closes a task in one call, run from its story's worktree. One package, `internal/taskdone`, builds the result for this command, the MCP tool `task_done`, and the host channel's `task.done`. Before any step it refuses, changing nothing, while the running flai is older than the newest flai release in the project's history and that release raised task done's rules number or its major version (S-0363, S-0445, [Versions](#versions-the-hosts-flai-and-the-tree)). A change to what these steps commit, message, sync, move, log, add to the touches, or check raises that number, in `flai/internal/buildinfo/task-done-rules.txt`, by one in the same change; otherwise an older flai closes tasks by rules the project has outgrown ([ADR-0180](../adrs/0180-flai-task-done-refuses-an-older-flai-only-when-the-newest-flai-release-raised.md)). The steps, in order: commit (S-0322, [ADR-0128](../adrs/0128-flai-task-done-commits-the-paths-the-closing-task-covers-and-those-no-other.md): of the paths changed in the worktree, those the closing task's `touches` cover and those no other open task of the story covers, an open task being one neither `done` nor `cancelled` and a touch covering a path when it names the file or a folder above it; a path the closing task and another open task both cover goes with the closing task, and a file no task declares goes with it too; staged and committed as `storygit.CommitPaths` does, with `git add -A -- <paths>` and `git commit -- <paths>`, leaving the rest of the worktree as it was; a path only another open task covers is left uncommitted and listed as a path left, which stops nothing; `-m` is needed only when there is something to commit; nothing to commit records no commit and goes on; refused while a rebase is unfinished, as `flai stream sync` refuses); tell (S-0333: a message to each other story in progress or in review whose claim covers a path the commit changed, found with `itemedit.Covering` as the notice at acceptance finds them, the shared paths included and a story with an empty claim told of every path; sent with `messages.Notify` from the task's story, by the closing agent, on the conversation open between the two stories, either way round, its `about` widened by the paths, or a new one `about` them; it names the task and its title, the commit's short hash, the story branch, the commit's subject, and the paths, and says the change reaches the main branch when the task's story is accepted and `git show <hash>` shows it until then; nothing without a commit or with no story covering a path; the paths sync regenerates (`issues.Generated`, [ADR-0098](../adrs/0098-flai-stream-sync-and-flai-accept-regenerate-design-issues-summary-md-when-a.md)) are left out first, so a commit that changes only those tells no one (S-0372, [ADR-0154](../adrs/0154-a-reply-may-settle-a-conversation-which-then-awaits-neither-story-and-flai.md)); a message that cannot be written is logged as a warning and stops nothing; it runs before the sync so a sync stopped on conflicts, after which a second call has nothing to commit, loses no notice; [workflow.md](workflow.md#branches-and-collisions-adr-0019)); sync (the story's `flai stream sync`, in `internal/storygit`; its refusal for uncommitted changes does not stop the run when those changes are only the paths left, and the close that leaves none syncs the branch; since S-0371 it warns on each path the rebased branch undoes of main's change, found by this sync or held in the story's record, as `flai stream sync` does, and the warning stops nothing, [ADR-0155](../adrs/0155-a-rebased-story-branch-that-undoes-main-s-change-to-a-path-is-recorded-at-sync.md)); move (the task to `done` under `flai move`'s rules, with any story or epic that moves with it; a task already done is not moved again, so the call can be repeated after a stop); log (the narrative entry: the message's subject line, or `--log`; with neither, `Closed T-nnnn: <title>`); touches (the paths the commit changed that the task's or the story's `touches` do not cover are added to each, as `flai touches` records them; a path left is never added; nothing without a commit; since S-0370, [ADR-0144](../adrs/0144-flai-task-done-widens-touches-only-with-the-committed-paths-the-story-s-branch.md), only the committed paths the story's branch still changes against its merge base with the main branch, read after the sync with `git diff --name-only --no-renames <main>...HEAD` in the worktree, so a file the commit reverted to main's version is not added and is listed as `unchanged`; a diff that cannot be read stops the step; it never removes a touch, which stays the agent's with `flai touches`); check (`flai check --strict` scoped to the story as the close-out scopes it, ADR-0085: findings in the story stop the run, findings outside it are notes); inbox (the agent's inbox as the MCP tool `inbox` answers it, in `internal/inbox`, advancing the same cursor). It stops at the first step that fails, answers that step's findings, and leaves the later steps undone. The answer gives `commit` (hash and subject, or none), the paths left for the other open tasks, `told` (each story told, with `story`, `title`, `paths`, and `conversation`; the text output prints `told: S-nnnn (MS-nnnn) of <paths>` for each), `sync` (synced, refused with why, or the conflicting paths and how to continue, with `undoes_main` as `flai stream sync --json` gives it), `move` (the task's new state and each story or epic moved with it), `log`, `touches` (the paths added to the task and to the story, and in `unchanged` the committed paths left out because the branch no longer changes them against main; the text output prints `touches: not added, unchanged against main: <paths>`), `check` (findings and notes; since S-0492, [ADR-0198](../adrs/0198-task-done-s-answer-lists-at-most-ten-of-the-check-s-notes-outside-the-story-and.md), every finding but only the first 10 notes, the rest counted in `notes_omitted`, which `flai check --story` lists), `inbox`, and `stopped` (the step it stopped at, empty when every step ran). Since S-0450 ([ADR-0179](../adrs/0179-task-done-and-story-start-carry-an-inbox-that-lists-the-story-s-threads-and.md)) `inbox` lists in full only the open threads on the story and its tasks, `awaiting_you` counting those, and counts the threads on other items in `threads_omitted`; the whole answer, `--json` and `task_done` alike, is fitted to 40,000 bytes of JSON (`context.PartLimit`): the inbox's oldest changes are left out first, counted in `changes_omitted`, then each listed thread's last entry and pending recommendation text are shortened to a fixed length with an ellipsis, counted in `entries_shortened`, to be read whole with `thread_get`. Exit 0 when every step ran, 3 when the sync stopped the run, 4 when the check did, 1 when another step did. Running the task's tests with `flai test`, fixing what they find, and ticking criteria with `flai criteria tick` stay the agent's: a fix is closed by calling it again, and after a stopped sync the agent resolves the listed paths in the worktree, `git add`s them, runs `git rebase --continue`, and calls it again. Since S-0367 ([ADR-0139](../adrs/0139-a-test-tier-s-fix-command-rewrites-the-files-a-story-changes-before-flai-task.md)) the commit step first runs each test tier's `fix_command` (`verify.CheckoutFix`) over the paths it is about to commit, so the commit holds them fixed; a fix command that fails is logged at warn and the commit goes on. The sync step then fixes the rest of what the branch changes, as `flai stream sync` does. |
| `flai touches <id> [paths...] [--add \| --remove \| --clear]` | Set or show the `touches` list on a story or task; `flai check` warns `wip.overlap` when the claims of two stories in progress overlap (S-0037).
