Since S-0279 ([ADR-0115](../adrs/0115-a-close-out-records-no-wip-overlap-and-notes-only-an-overlap-naming-its-story.md)) it compares each story's claim, as the pull hold does (`Holds.Claim`, `Holds.Overlaps`), and never a task on its own; a pair of stories is one warning, on the item file of the lower ID, naming each overlapping entry of both claims, with both IDs in `stories` in `--json`. Paths given alone replace the list; since S-0254 (I-0067) `--add` adds them to it and `--remove` takes them out of it, refusing a path the item does not touch, and the help says paths alone replace. Replacement stays the default for scripts that rely on it; `flai stream sync`'s widen hint is `flai touches <id> --add <paths outside>`. Since S-0128 it is also a claim: a ready story whose claim overlaps an open story's is held (see `flai board`). Since S-0295 only a story in progress holds, an overlap inside the shared paths (`flai shared`) holds nothing, and a story's folder touch is narrowed in its claim to its tasks' touches inside it ([ADR-0096](../adrs/0096-a-story-in-review-holds-nothing-an-overlap-inside-the-manifest-s-shared-paths.md)). Since S-0468 ([ADR-0188](../adrs/0188-a-task-takes-no-folder-touch-the-planner-s-folder-touch-on-a-story-needs-the.md)) a task takes no folder touch: an entry that, read as a path, names a folder of the repository tree holding at least one file, a path absent from the tree being a file to be made and a folder wholly inside the shared paths not counting. `flai touches`, `flai task new --touches`, `flai edit --touches`, and the MCP `item_new` and `item_edit` refuse one on a task, writing nothing, naming the folder and its file count, and saying to name the files the task changes or to leave the folder to the story's touches with a `### Planning` note. The planner's folder touch on a story, under `FLAI_ROLE=plan` or by an author named `planner-…`, is refused the same way unless the story's Notes name the folder under `### Planning`, read as the body will be after the write; the operator's and the story's agent's are not ([strategic-agents.md](strategic-agents.md#what-the-planner-writes)). `flai task done`'s widening adds the files its commit changed and is not refused (ADR-0144). Since S-0211 a change of the touches leaves an edit notice (`itemedit.Record`, `touches` changed), as `flai edit` does, so agents hear of it and `flai serve` can plan the story again. Since S-0244 (I-0059) a change that grows a claim into another's says so: `itemedit.WatchClaim` reads the story's claim before the write and `ClaimWatch.Grown` compares what it gained with the claim of every other story in progress, only when the written story is in progress itself, and prints `overlaps S-nnnn <title> (in progress) on <paths>: both stories are told, and its agent is messaged in the two stories' conversation: agree there who changes them first` for each (S-0332), returned as `overlaps` (`story`, `title`, `paths`) with `--json`. Since S-0332 ([ADR-0121](../adrs/0121-flai-tells-two-stories-of-a-trial-merge-conflict-or-a-grown-overlap-with-a.md)) `Grown` also sends a message from the written story to each story reached, by the writer, `about` the paths gained, in the pair's open conversation or a new one (`messages.Notify`), saying the claims now overlap and asking which of the two changes them first. Each overlap is noted twice in `.flai-cache/overlaps.jsonl`, once for each story, with `grew` and `reached` and `accepted` empty, so a flai that predates it passes over it; `flai mcp` reports it as an `overlapped` change whose `cause` is the other story. A failure is logged and the write stands. |
| `flai touches suggest <S-nnnn> [path...] [--min 2] [--limit 20] [--json]` (S-0210) | Lists the files most often changed in the main branch's commits together with the story's touches, its tasks' not cancelled, and the paths given, with each file's count and share; writes nothing. `storygit.CommitFiles` reads the history, leaving out merges, the wip folder, and flai's bookkeeping commits; `planning.CoChanged` counts. State is out of reach: ID, type, status, transitions, blocks, owner, dates. A closed or archived item is refused (`RefusedError`, exit 4). A retitle is kept in step in the front matter, the heading, the file's name (`workitem.FileName`), the parent's child line, the story's narrative (front matter `title` and heading), links to the old file name in Markdown under wip, design, and docs (the archive excepted; from a worktree only wip, since one commit cannot span two checkouts), and the generated index. A new parent must exist, be of the right type, and be open; the child line leaves the old parent and `AppendChild` adds it to the new. A story's new epic follows it as though it had entered from backlog, and the one it left as though it had been cancelled out of it (`workitem.Repo.FollowParent`, S-0200, [workflow.md](workflow.md#an-epic-follows-its-stories)), saved with the edit; `Result.Followed` lists the walks, printed, indented, as `E-nnnn → <state>, following S-nnnn`. With `--hash` (`docedit.Hash` of the file as read) a change made meanwhile is a `ConflictError` (exit 3). `check.Run` before and after, as `docedit.Save` does, the second run scoped to the item's story, the story or a task's parent, with the files the edit wrote as the changed paths (`check.ScopeToStories`, as `flai check --story` scopes it; [ADR-0197](../adrs/0197-a-checked-edit-of-a-work-item-refuses-only-a-new-finding-inside-its-story-s.md)): a new finding inside that scope, or an error on the item, puts every file back from the snapshot taken before each write (the rename included) and refuses with the findings (exit 4); a new finding outside the story, such as on another story's task another agent is writing, is in `Result.Warnings` and the edit stands; and one the scoped check leaves out, such as `narrative.state` on another open story's narrative, is neither. An epic has no story: its edit refuses a new finding only on a file the edit wrote, and warns of the rest. A new `wip.overlap`, `touches.folder`, or `wip.after-hub` stays a warning. What is not allowed whatever the repository holds (an empty title, a nature there is not, a list value that is not a word or a path, an epic's touches or parent, a parent of the wrong type or closed, a body with its own heading) is an `InvalidError`, which the command says as `rule:`, so that the channel answers 400; an item or parent that does not exist stays "not found", which it answers with 404. One commit for every file touched, removals included (`git add -A -- files`, `git commit -- files`), subject `chore: [ID] edit <what>`. A story's agent (S-0103) is one of its own words: `--harness`, `--model`, `--provider` (S-0349), and `--agent-config` merge into the agent it has (`key=` removes a key), `--clear-agent` alone removes it, provider and all, and with the others it starts from nothing. `--show` prints the agent and the project's default; another type given them is `InvalidError`. A story's or epic's `topics` (S-0135) are replaced by `--topics` and removed by `--clear-topics`, through `workitem.CleanTopics`; a task given them is an `InvalidError`. A story's `after` (S-0130) is replaced by `--after` and removed by `--clear-after`; entries are made canonical story IDs without repeats, and anything but a story ID, a story naming itself, or `after` on another type is an `InvalidError`, while a story that does not exist and a cycle are the check's to refuse (`story.after`). The planning data (S-0199, [ADR-0074](../adrs/0074-work-items-carry-planning-data-a-story-s-draft-flag-an-epic-s-or-story-s-cost.md)) is one of the item's own words too: `--draft` makes a story in the backlog a draft and `--no-draft` finalizes one, stamping the story's `finalized` block with the editor and the time, as below (S-0201, [ADR-0077](../adrs/0077-a-story-that-was-a-draft-records-who-finalized-it-and-when-and-the-dashboard.md)); `--no-draft` on a story that is not a draft stamps nothing and leaves an earlier block, and `--draft` removes the block; under `FLAI_ROLE=orchestrate`, `--no-draft` is refused, as `rule:`, unless `orchestration.permissions.finalize_drafts` is on, the edit changes nothing else, and `Repo.Finalizable` finds the draft complete, the refusal naming what it lacks (`orchestratorFinalizes`, S-0219), and the MCP tool `item_edit` holds the orchestrator's `draft: false` the same way; since S-0328, under `FLAI_ROLE=orchestrate`, a cost of delay flag is refused unless `orchestration.permissions.plan_backlog_stories` is on, the edit gives a story in the backlog, whose epic has none either, its first inputs and nothing else, and sets no `--cost-of-delay-value`, no empty input, and no `--clear-cost-of-delay` (`orchestratorSetsCostOfDelay`, `Repo.OrchestratorSetsCostOfDelay`), and `item_edit` holds its `cost_of_delay` the same way; the cost of delay flags (a story's or epic's) and the `--forecast-` flags (a story's) change only the keys given, an empty value removes a key, and `--clear-cost-of-delay` or `--clear-forecast` removes the block, contradicting a flag that sets it. A block that changes is stamped `by` (`--by`, else `FLAI_AGENT`, else the config author) and `at`; an edit that changes no value stamps nothing. A cost of delay that removals leave with no input and no value is removed, and so is a forecast left with no duration and no delivery. A draft on another type or on a story past backlog, a cost of delay on a task, a forecast on anything but a story, and an amount that is not a number are `InvalidError`s; what the result leaves invalid (a negative amount, a duration that is not one, a forecast with only a basis) is `Validate`'s to refuse. `Result.Changed` names what changed: `title`, `nature`, `tags`, `topics`, `touches`, `after`, `parent`, `agent`, `draft`, `cost_of_delay`, `forecast`, and for the body `goal`, `criteria`, `notes`, or `body`. Each edit is noted in `.flai-cache/edits.jsonl` (at, by, id, what; stamped when written, bounded to the last 500, mode 0600), from which `flai mcp` reports edits by others as `edited` changes and for which a waiting agent wakes; since S-0211 `flai serve`'s replanner reads it too ([Planning again](strategic-agents.md#planning-again)). It is a log outside git and not a front matter key on purpose: an older flai serving the same repository, as the installed one does for MCP, cannot act on a key it does not know (it refused the item outright until S-0181). Since S-0244 an edit of `--touches` reports a claim it grew into another story in progress's, as `flai touches` does, in `overlaps` with `--json` |

[--hash] [--by] [--autocommit] [--trailer...]`, `flai criteria untick <id> <n>...` (S-0282) | An item's acceptance criteria by number, from 1, in the order of the boxes under `## Acceptance criteria`, nested ones included (`itemedit.Criteria`). `list` reads, an archived item too; `--json` gives `id`, `path`, `hash`, and `criteria`. `tick` and `untick` take numbers as separate arguments or comma separated, change those boxes and no other byte of the body, and save through `itemedit.Apply` as `flai edit` does: a closed or archived item is refused (exit 4), as is a new finding inside the story's scope, while one outside it is a warning ([ADR-0197](../adrs/0197-a-checked-edit-of-a-work-item-refuses-only-a-new-finding-inside-its-story-s.md)), with `--hash` a change made meanwhile is a conflict (exit 3), a number with no box is refused and nothing is written, and the edit is noted as `criteria` for agents. Nothing is committed without `--autocommit`. The story's agent ticks a criterion once it has verified it; the guard lets a sub-agent `list` and refuses it, the planner, and the orchestrator `tick` and `untick` ([ADR-0089](../adrs/0089-acceptance-criteria-are-ticked-through-flai-by-number-by-the-story-s-agent-once.md)). |
| `flai thread new --on <path\|id> [--heading] "<title>" "<text>"`, `reply <id> [--recommend] [--source <path>[#<heading>]] "<text>"`, `confirm <id> [--by]`, `resolve <id> [--reason]`, `list [--on] [--all]`, `show <id>` | Threads under `wip/threads/` (`TH-nnnn`, ADR-0020) anchored to a document, a heading in it, or a work item; anchors are validated; replies from anyone but the opener set `answered`, the opener's follow-up sets `open`, `resolve` closes, and archiving an item resolves those still open on it ([ADR-0109](../adrs/0109-archiving-an-item-resolves-the-threads-still-open-on-it.md)); unresolved threads on a story or its tasks are mirrored into the narrative's `## Open questions` (S-0038), and removing the mirror block leaves one blank line where it stood, never two (S-0179). An entry by the same author in the same second as the last joins that entry under its heading, as narrative log entries and issue instances do, since a second identical heading fails MD024 (I-0043, S-0179). `new`, `reply`, and `resolve` refuse, writing nothing, an entry the project's markdown lint rejects, with the rule and line. An entry carries two optional marks (S-0220, [ADR-0090](../adrs/0090-a-thread-entry-is-marked-a-recommendation-in-its-heading-and-cites-its-source.md)): a recommendation is headed `### <time> <author> (recommendation)`, and an entry that cites a source ends with a one-line paragraph `Source: <path>` or `Source: <path> § <heading>`, the path and heading validated as an anchor's are (`threads.ReplyWith` with `threads.Marks`; `Reply` writes a plain entry). A recommendation leaves the status as it was, so the thread still awaits the operator, and is refused on a resolved thread and from the opener; an answer citing a source sets `answered` as any reply does. A recommendation is pending while the thread is not resolved, it is the newest entry by anyone but the opener, and nothing confirms it; `threads.Confirm` makes it the answer, appending `Confirmed the recommendation of <time> <author>.` with its source by the confirming author and setting `answered`, and is refused with none pending and to the recommendation's author. Each entry in the JSON carries `recommendation` (bool) and `source` (`{ path, heading? }`, absent when none), and the thread's view carries `pending_recommendation`, that entry or null. An older flai reads a recommendation as a plain entry by `<author> (recommendation)` with the source line in its text. `reply --recommend` posts a recommendation and `--source <path>[#<heading>]` cites a source, a file in the repository or an item ID, and after `#` a heading in it; a source with no path, or a path or heading that does not exist, is refused, naming which. `confirm <id>` runs `threads.Confirm` as `--by`, else `FLAI_AGENT`, else the config author, as `reply` does. `show` marks a recommendation `(recommendation)` after its author and names a pending one under the thread's header with the command that confirms it. |
| `flai message send <S-nnnn> "<text>" [--from] [--about <path>]... [--by]`, `reply <MS-nnnn> "<text>" [--from] [--by] [--settled]`, `escalate <MS-nnnn> "<reason>" [--from] [--by]`, `share <MS-nnnn> --paths <path>... "<split>" [--from] [--by]`, `list [--story] [--all]`, `show <MS-nnnn>`, each with `--json` | Conversations between the agents of two stories in progress or in review, under `wip/messages/` (`MS-nnnn`, S-0330, [ADR-0120](../adrs/0120-agents-of-two-open-stories-message-each-other-in-conversations-kept-under-wip.md); `internal/messages`), kept apart from threads: none appears in `flai thread list`, among the threads awaiting the operator in `inbox` or the designer inbox, or in a narrative's `## Open questions`. `send` starts a conversation from the sender's story to `<S-nnnn>` with its first message, written in the main checkout as one file, `MS-nnnn-<slug>.md`, numbered one more than the highest there, titled with the first line of the message cut on a word to 72 characters; its front matter holds `id`, `title`, `from`, `to`, `about`, `status` (`open` or `closed`), `participants`, `created`, and `updated`, and its body the entries under `## Entries`, each headed `### <time> <author> <story>`. The sender's or replying story is `--from`, else `FLAI_STORY`, else the story in an `agent-S-nnnn` `FLAI_AGENT`, else the `story/S-nnnn` branch checked out (`issueStory`, as `flai issue` reads it); with none, the command is refused. Both stories must exist, be stories, not be the same, not be archived, and be in progress or in review; any other state is refused with the state, writing nothing. `--about` names a repository path, a file or folder relative to the root that must exist in the worktree or the main checkout, once per path. The author is `--by`, else `FLAI_AGENT`, else the config author. `reply` adds an entry from one of the two stories and prints whom the conversation now awaits: the story that did not write its last entry written for a story, the addressee until it replies. It is refused from a story not of the two, from a story no longer open, and on a conversation that reads as closed. `--settled` (S-0372, [ADR-0154](../adrs/0154-a-reply-may-settle-a-conversation-which-then-awaits-neither-story-and-flai.md)) marks a reply that asks nothing, such as agreement: its heading ends in `(settled)`, the conversation awaits neither story until the next message from either, a `flai task done` notice among them, and flai serve starts neither story's agent for it; `reply` then prints `MS-nnnn is settled and awaits no one (<n> entries)`. Only a reply can be settled: `send`, `escalate`, `share`, and flai's own messages write unsettled entries. `escalate` (S-0332, [ADR-0121](../adrs/0121-flai-tells-two-stories-of-a-trial-merge-conflict-or-a-grown-overlap-with-a.md), `messages.Escalate`) asks the operator when the two stories do not agree: it opens a thread on the escalating story, one of the two, by its author, titled `S-nnnn and S-mmmm do not agree: <conversation title>`, whose first entry names both stories, gives the conversation's path, and quotes the reason; then it adds a message from that story naming the thread, so the conversation awaits the other story, and leaves it open. It prints the thread, its path, and whom the conversation awaits; `--json` gives `thread` and `conversation`. It is refused, writing nothing, from a third story, from a story no longer in progress or in review, on a conversation that reads as closed, and with an empty reason. `share` (S-0334, [ADR-0134](../adrs/0134-flai-serve-asks-the-agent-of-a-story-in-progress-about-each-ready-story-it.md), `messages.Share`) records, on a conversation between a story in progress and a ready story it holds on overlap, that the story in progress shares the paths `--paths` names with the ready one, split as `<split>` says. It appends to the front matter's `shares` a share with `holder`, `held`, `paths`, `split`, `by`, and `at`, and adds an entry from the holding story quoting the split; it prints the share and whom the conversation awaits, and `--json` prints the conversation as `show --json` does. `--paths` is repeatable or comma separated, each path relative to the root. The story is read as for `reply`; only the holding story's agent, writing for it, or the operator, the holding story's `owner` as the author, may share. It is refused, writing nothing, from anyone else, on a conversation that reads as closed, with no ready story, or whose other story is not in progress, with an empty split or no path, and for a path that does not overlap both stories' claims, the refusal naming their overlap. While a share is in force, an overlap between the two stories whose narrower path lies inside a shared path does not hold (`workitem.Holds.WithShares`, read by `Repo.Holds`), as [workflow.md](workflow.md#branches-and-collisions-adr-0019) says. flai serve asks for a held story first (`messages.AskHold`, see `flai serve agent`): a message by `flai` from the held story to the holding one, `about` the overlapping paths, giving the held story's title and goal and the three answers. A conversation may so have a ready story on one side; a reply written while the other side is ready awaits no one. A conversation reads as closed when its status is `closed` or either story is done, cancelled, or archived, or does not exist (`Conversation.Closed`); a closed one is never reopened. Acceptance and `flai archive` close those of the stories they archive with `Closed: <id> was accepted` or `was archived` (`messages.CloseOn`); a cancelled story's read as closed without an entry until archived. An entry by the same author in the same second as the last joins it, as a thread's does (I-0043), and `send` and `reply` refuse, writing nothing, an entry the project's markdown lint rejects. `list` prints one line a conversation in ID order, its ID, `from → to`, `awaiting S-nnnn`, or `settled` while it awaits neither story, the time of its last entry, and its title; by default only those that do not read as closed, of every story, `--story` only those the story is one of the two of, and `--all` adds the closed ones with `closed: <why>`. `show` prints the conversation's header, its state `open · awaiting S-nnnn` or `open · settled`, the paths it is about, and every entry, a settled one's heading ending in `(settled)`; the ID may be given in any padding. `--json` prints `messages.View`: the front matter, `closed`, `closed_reason`, `awaiting` (empty when closed or settled), `path` relative to the repository, `shares` (S-0334), and `entries` as `{at, author, story, text, settled}`, `story` empty on an entry that closed it. Since S-0331 agents work them through the MCP tools `message_send`, `message_reply`, and `message_get`, since S-0332 `message_escalate`, and since S-0334 `message_share`, `inbox` lists them under `messages`, and `wait_for_events` wakes on them (see `flai mcp`); the dashboard comes in a later story of E-0018 (S-0336). |

[--limit n]` | Rank the sections of the design and docs folders, the conventions among them, by BM25 against the query (ADR-0049, S-0147), as the MCP `doc_search` does: `ctxpack.SearchSections` loads every markdown file under the manifest's `design` and `docs` folders but READMEs, the ADR template, and hidden folders, cut by `Doc.Cuts` as the pack's ranked step cuts them (each section down to the next heading of any level, labelled with its heading path below the title), and `search.IndexSections` ranks them, the pack's candidates and this alike, with the stopwords and single letters `search.Terms` leaves out. At most 20 (`search.MaxSectionHits`), each with path, title, heading path, line, first three lines, size, and score. A query with no words left is refused. |

Without `--heading`, `show` prints the content, its SHA-256, and the edit mode: `full`, `body` (flai owns the front matter: work items, narratives, `board.md`), or `none` with the reason (generated files, threads, issues, archive). `save` reads the new content on stdin; fails with `conflict:` and the current content, hash, and a unified diff when the hash is stale; refuses a front matter change in `body` mode; runs the check before and after writing, and when the edit introduces any finding, or the file has an error, restores the file and fails with `refused:` (exit 4) and the findings; a conflict is exit 3; sets `updated` to today on design and docs files unless the designer changed it; commits that path only (`docs:` or `chore:`, item ID in brackets for items and narratives) unless `--no-commit` or `dashboard.autocommit: false`. Nothing is pushed. With `--story` (S-0378, [ADR-0145](../adrs/0145-a-dashboard-save-may-name-a-story-and-is-then-committed-on-that-story-s-branch.md)), `show` and `save` work in that story's worktree under a repository rooted there (`docedit.locate`), so the hash, the mode, and the check are the branch's, and the commit is on `story/S-nnnn` with the subject `docs: [S-nnnn] <message>`; the main checkout is left as it was. The host methods `doc.show` and `doc.save` take `story` and run in its worktree (`docDir`). `lint` (S-0378) reads content on stdin and lints it as the markdown file at the path, under design, docs, or wip, which need not exist, with the project's markdownlint configuration and the subset of its rules flai implements (ADR-0061); it prints `path:line rule/alias message`, or with `--json` `{path, findings: [{line, rule, alias, message}]}`, and exits 0 clean, 1 with findings, 2 when it cannot lint (no `--stdin`, a path outside the three folders, a configuration it cannot read). The host read method `doc.lint` `{path, content}` answers the same in process (`hostapi.LintDoc`), for the dashboard's editor as the operator types; the markdown tier stays the authority at acceptance. |

Reach (`FileReach`): a file under the manifest's design or docs folder, and the design token file (`design.tokens`) and design stylesheet (`design.stylesheet`) the manifest names, wherever they are. Refused with exit 1 and the reason: any other path, a path under the wip folder, a markdown file, which `doc show` and `doc save` handle, a binary file (a NUL byte in its first 8 KB), and a file over `MaxFileSize`, 1 MiB (1,048,576 bytes). `show` prints the content as it is on standard output and the path and its SHA-256 on standard error, so the content can be redirected and saved again; `--json` prints `{path, content, hash}`. `save` reads the new content on stdin; a stale hash is `conflict:` (exit 3) with the current content, its hash, and a diff. The host methods are `file.get` and `file.save` (`flai hostapi`). |
| `flai adr new "<title>" [--status] [--supersedes N]... [--refines N]... [--body-stdin] [--commit \| --autocommit] [--trailer]... [--print-body]`, `flai adr accept <N>`, `flai adr topics <N> <topic>...` | Record an architecture decision (S-0060, `flai/internal/adr`). The number is one past the highest `NNNN-*.md` in `design/adrs` in the checkout it runs in, the main checkout, every linked worktree (uncommitted files included), the main branch, and every local `story/*` branch, which `storygit.FolderNames` lists for the ADR folder, so stories worked in parallel do not take the same number (S-0245, I-0063); a worktree or branch git cannot read is skipped, and gaps are not filled. `--supersedes` and `--refines` name ADRs in the checkout only. The file is `NNNN-slug.md` with `id`, `title`, `status` (`proposed` by default), `date`, `supersedes`, `superseded_by`, and `refines` when given; the body is the project's `0000-template.md` sections or standard input; the row is added to `design/adrs/README.md` with the index's notes ("accepted, refines 0018"); a superseded ADR gets `superseded_by` and its row noted. `flai check` runs with everything in place, and a finding the ADR introduces, or any failure after files were written, puts every file back (exit 4 with `{ refused }` for a check refusal). `--autocommit` makes one `docs: ADR-NNNN <title>` commit of what was written unless `dashboard.autocommit` is false. The story, the refusals, and the output are `flai issue`'s `--commit`'s; there is no `--story`, and `--json`'s `commit` takes the place of `--autocommit`'s. `accept` sets a proposed ADR to accepted with today's date and updates its row. `flai check` rule `adr.index`: a file with no row, a row with no file. `topics` writes or replaces the `topics` key of an ADR of any status and changes nothing else ([ADR-0047](../adrs/0047-an-agent-is-primed-with-what-its-story-s-topics-claim-and-links-select.md), S-0134), with the same check-and-undo. `flai doc` treats an accepted ADR as not editable, except that `flai doc save` takes a save whose only change is its `topics`. |

[--json]`, `flai digest list [--status active\|absorbed\|compacted] [--topic <t>] [--json]`, `flai digest show <D-nnnn> [--json]` (S-0428, [ADR-0191](../adrs/0191-the-digest-is-one-markdown-file-per-entry-under-the-design-folder-s-digest.md)) | Write and read the digest, what past stories learned, one entry per file under the design folder's `digest` folder (`cmd/digest.go`, `internal/digest`). `new` numbers the entry one past the highest `D-nnnn` it finds in the checkout it runs in, the main checkout, every linked worktree, the main branch, and every local `story/*` branch, as `flai adr new` does, so parallel writers do not take the same number; gaps are not filled. It writes `D-nnnn-<slug>.md`, the slug the title's cut at 80 characters on a word, in the checkout it runs in, with `id`, `title`, `kind`, `sources`, `topics`, `paths`, today's `date`, `status: active`, and the body below `# D-nnnn <title>`. `--kind` is one of `decision`, `answer`, `recommendation`, and `cause`, and at least one `--source` is required, both refused with exit 1 before anything is read; `--source`, `--topic`, and `--path` are repeatable or comma separated. `--body` gives the body as text and `--body-file` from a file, relative to the working directory, or standard input when it is `-`; the two are mutually exclusive. `edit` takes the ID in any padding (`D-7` is `D-0007`); each flag given replaces that field, `--body` or `--body-file` the body and `--topic`, `--path`, `--source`, and `--asked-again` their lists, and an empty value such as `--topic=` empties a list; with no flag it is refused with exit 1. Both run `digest.Validate`, the check's own rules, on what they wrote: `new` removes the file on any problem, and `edit` puts the file back as it was on a problem the edit introduced, leaving one that was there before; each problem is printed as a finding, `<path>:<line>: error: <rule>: <message>`, and the exit code is 4, with `--json` `{"refused": {path, reason, findings}}`. Each prints `<D-nnnn> <title> (<kind>, <status>)`, then the path and `recorded` or `edited`; `--json` gives the result with the entry. `list` prints this checkout's entries in ID order, each one's ID, status, kind, date, and title, and below it its sources, topics, and paths, or `no digest entries`; `--status` and `--topic` keep the entries in that status or bearing on that topic, an unknown status refused with exit 1; `--json` is an array of the entries, bodies included, `[]` when none. `show` prints one entry's title, kind, status, date, sources, topics, paths, `absorbed_by` when it is absorbed, `asked_again`, `cited`, its file, and its body; `--json` gives the entry. | `flai digest absorb <D-nnnn> --by <path> [--heading <heading>] [--json]`, `flai digest cited [--json]`, `flai digest compact [--dry-run] [--json]` (S-0432, [ADR-0199](../adrs/0199-the-curator-runs-after-acceptances-coalesced-and-on-digest-schedule-marks.md)) | Move entries out of `active`, in the checkout they run in (`cmd/digest.go`, `internal/digest/decay.go`). `absorb` sets `status: absorbed` and `absorbed_by` to `--by`, the path from the project root of the design document, convention, or ADR that now records the fact, or `<path>#<anchor>` with `--heading`, which `digestAnchor` finds as `flai doc show --heading` does (`context.Section`, then `context.Slug` of the section's heading): its text, a heading path, or its anchor. The body is kept; an entry absorbed again names the newer document, and a compacted entry may be absorbed. No `--by` is refused with exit 1; a path not from the root or naming no file, and a heading the document lacks, are refused with exit 4 and nothing written. `--json` gives the result as `edit`'s does. `cited` reads every work item, kanban and archive, every narrative in the agents folder and its archive, and every thread for `D-nnnn` in any padding (`digest.Citations`), never the digest folder; the citer is the item, the narrative's story, or the thread, and its date the citing file's `updated`, a file whose `updated` cannot be read an error naming it. It writes each entry's `cited` as its citers in ID order where that changed, checked as `edit`'s write is. Text prints per entry its ID, status, latest citation or `-`, its citers, and `(written)` where `cited` changed, then the counts; `--json` is an array of `{id, title, status, date, path, cited, latest, changed}`. `compact` reads `digest.compact_after` (`Digest.CompactAfterDuration`: `720h` unset, `0` off, a value not a duration or negative refused), runs `cited`, then turns each active entry whose latest citation, or its `date` when later or when it has none, is before now less the window into `compacted`, its body emptied below `# D-nnnn <title>`. Absorbed and compacted entries are left alone, so a second run compacts nothing more. Text prints each entry compacted with its title and its latest citation or date, then the count, the cut-off, the window, and the entries whose `cited` was written; with compaction off it says so and does nothing. `--dry-run` prints what would be compacted and written and writes nothing. `--json` gives `{window, off, dry_run, before, cited, compacted}`. Nothing is deleted. `flai guard` passes all three to the curator and refuses them to every sub-agent and the other strategic agents, as it does `new` and `edit` (see `flai guard`). |
| `flai digest harvest [--since <date>] [--kind decision\|answer\|recommendation\|cause]... [--json]`, `flai digest seed [--since <date>] [--dry-run] [--json]` (S-0429) | List what the digest could record, and seed it (`cmd/digest.go`, `internal/digest`). `harvest` lists every candidate learned on or after `--since` (`YYYY-MM-DD` in UTC, or an RFC 3339 time; with none, everything), of the kinds `--kind` names (repeatable or comma separated; an unknown kind refused with exit 1): each bullet under `## Decisions` of a done story's narrative, archived or not (`decision`, its source the story and its ref the narrative); each entry the manifest's `owner` wrote on a thread someone else opened, but a resolution (`Resolved.` or `Resolved: ...`) and the threads `permission_prompt` opens (`Allow <tool> <path>?`), as an `answer`, a confirmation answering with the recommendation it confirms, or, when it follows a recommendation still pending without confirming it, as an overruled `recommendation` carrying both texts (`digest.Answers`, the one rule S-0433 runs as the operator answers; its ref the entry's time); and each closed issue's cause, its `## Description`, and fix, its `Closed <ts>: <reason>` line's reason or else its `## Remediation` (`cause`). Each carries the topics and paths of the item it comes from (a thread's anchored item, a narrative's story, the story an issue's fix names or that was made from it), its time and date, its title, its text, and `cited_by`, the entries whose `sources` name its source. It is sorted by time, then source, ref, and kind, and writes nothing, so the curator (S-0431) reads the same list on every run. Text prints one line per candidate, `<kind> <date> <source> [<ref>]: <title>`, with `(cited by D-nnnn)`, then the counts by kind; `--json` is the array with every field. `seed` writes, through `digest.New`, one entry per `answer`, `recommendation`, and `cause` candidate, dated the day it was learned, titled from the thread's question and the answer's first sentence or from the issue's title, its body quoting the question, the answer, the overruled recommendation, the cause, and the fix each in a fenced `text` block so the markdown lint passes whatever they hold, and its source the thread or issue. It skips a thread's candidate when an entry citing the thread holds the candidate's time, and an issue any entry cites, so a second run writes nothing; a write the check refuses, such as `digest.secret`, is skipped and named by the rule, never the text, and the rest go on. It never seeds decisions: they are hundreds, and which a design document already records is the curator's judgement. `--dry-run` writes nothing and lists what it would write, skipping a secret's shape as the check would. Text prints each entry written and skipped with its reason, then listed, written, and skipped per kind; `--json` gives `listed`, `written`, `skipped`, and `dry_run`. It was run once on this repository in S-0429. |
| `flai design init [--topics a,b] [--project name]`, `flai design show [--story S-nnnn]`, `flai design proposals`, `flai design css [--tailwind \| --write]` (S-0381, [ADR-0146](../adrs/0146-a-project-s-design-tokens-are-a-dtcg-file-the-manifest-names-beside-its.md); `--write` S-0385, [ADR-0159](../adrs/0159-the-generated-design-stylesheet-puts-the-dark-values-under-data-theme-dark-and.md); `--story` and `proposals` S-0384, [ADR-0151](../adrs/0151-the-operator-and-agents-change-a-design-system-by-edits-on-the-design-page.md)) | The project's design system, its token file and its guidance ([Design system](#design-system)). `init` writes both from the starters embedded in `designsystem`, creating their folders, the guidance's `updated` today and its `topics` from `--topics`, `[all]` by default; a topic the project does not use makes `flai check --strict` warn `doc.topic`, so its help says to name the UI sub-project. Since S-0386, `--project` (repeatable, or comma-separated) names a sub-project the design system governs, which must be one the manifest's `projects` lists; `governedTopics` writes each into the guidance's `topics`, the one list prime reads, with no second record ([ADR-0152](../adrs/0152-agents-build-ui-from-a-tailwind-v4-stylesheet-flai-generates-from-the-tokens.md) decision 4). With `--project` and no `--topics`, the default `all` is dropped; with both, the topics are the `--topics` values, then each sub-project not among them. An unknown name is refused with exit 1, naming the sub-projects there are, before anything is written. It refuses with exit 1 and writes neither when either file is there, and commits nothing. Since S-0385, when the manifest's `design.stylesheet` names a stylesheet, `init` then writes it from the token file it has just written, as `css --write` does, replacing what is there (`initStylesheet`); when that fails, the token file and the guidance stay, and the error says to run `flai design css --write`. It prints each path written, and `--json` adds `stylesheet` when there is one. `show` prints the token file's path and layout, the guidance's heading count, each theme's token count, each problem as `<file>:<line>: <token path>: <message>`, and each theme's contrast table, `FAIL` on a pair below its threshold; it exits 0 whatever it finds, no token file included. `show --json` is `designsystem.Show`, what the host method `design.get` answers, and since S-0384 adds `stylesheet`, the manifest's `design.stylesheet`, left out when unset. `show --story S-nnnn` (`designsystem.ShowStory`) reads the design system an open story proposes from its worktree, at the paths the worktree's manifest gives, and names the story and the worktree first; it refuses with exit 1 an ID that is no story, a story done or cancelled, which proposes nothing, and a story with no worktree, naming `flai stream open`. `proposals` (`designsystem.Proposals`) lists the open stories whose branch, or uncommitted worktree, changes the token file, the guidance, or both, at the paths the main checkout's manifest gives (`mainCheckoutRepo`); each branch is compared with its merge base with main, as `flai stream diff` compares it, and git is read in the main checkout, so the list is the same from any worktree. It prints ID, state, title, and the paths changed, in ID order, or a line naming both paths when there are none; `--json` is `[{id, title, status, paths}]`, `[]` for none. A proposal needs no step of its own to land: accepting its story merges the branch. `css` prints `designsystem.CSS`, and with `--tailwind` `designsystem.Tailwind`; no token file, or one with problems, is exit 1, listing each problem, and nothing is rendered. `css --write` (`docedit.WriteStylesheet` since S-0436, the generator `flai file save` uses) writes `designsystem.Stylesheet` to the file the manifest's `design.stylesheet` names, creating its folders and replacing what is there, and prints `wrote <path>`, with `--json` `{stylesheet, tokens}`; it refuses with exit 1, writing nothing, when the manifest names no stylesheet, saying to set the key, and when the token file is missing or has problems. `--write` and `--tailwind` are mutually exclusive. |
| `flai components list [--project <name>] [--json]`, `flai components config <sub-project> [--include <glob>]... [--exclude <glob>]... [--setup <path>] [--unset] [--json]` (S-0437, [ADR-0162](../adrs/0162-the-component-library-reaches-sub-projects-of-kind-sveltekit-or-svelte-finds.md)), `flai components serve [--project <name>] [--json]` (S-0438, [ADR-0163](../adrs/0163-flai-runs-a-sub-project-s-own-vite-dev-server-on-127-0-0-1-behind-the.md)) | The component library's catalogue, the manifest key that says where it is, and the dev server that renders it (`cmd/components.go`, `internal/components`). [component-library.md § The catalogue](component-library.md#the-catalogue-s-0437) says which sub-projects are supported and how props are read, and [project-manifest.md](project-manifest.md) describes `projects[].components`. `list` takes every sub-project in manifest order, or only the one `--project` names; an unknown name is exit 1, naming the sub-projects there are. A sub-project of kind `sveltekit` or `svelte` gets its components from `Project.ComponentSettings` (the key over the defaults: include `src/lib/**/*.svelte`, nothing excluded, no setup) and `components.List`: `Find` walks the sub-project's folder for the `.svelte` files an include glob matches and no exclude glob does, skipping `node_modules` and dot folders, sorted by path; `ReadProps` then runs the embedded `props.mjs` with `node` once per sub-project, in its folder, within `components.Timeout` (a minute), so `svelte/compiler` is the sub-project's own. No component is run. Any other kind is listed unsupported, with its kind, and needs neither tool. The text is a block per sub-project, blank lines between: `<name> (<path>, <kind>[, setup <setup>]): <n> components`, or `<name> (<path>): unsupported kind <kind>`; each component's path indented two spaces, followed by `(<n> examples)` when its examples file holds any; under it each prop, aligned: name, kind (a union followed by its literals as JSON joined by `\|`, such as `union "sm"\|"lg"`), type as written, `= <default>` as written, and `required` when it has neither a default nor an optional type, a type or default over several lines collapsed to one. A component with no props says `no props`; one whose props could not be read, such as one that does not parse, says `error: <reason>`. With no sub-projects it prints one line saying to add one of kind `sveltekit` or `svelte`. `--json` is `{"projects": [{name, path, kind, supported, setup, components: [{project, path, name, props: [{name, kind, type, default, required, literals, skeleton}], error, examples: [{name, props, theme, width}], examples_path}]}]}`, `components`, `props`, and `examples` `[]` when empty, and `setup`, `type`, `default`, `literals`, `error`, `theme`, and `width` left out when they have no value. Since S-0449 each `array`, `object`, and `unknown` prop carries `skeleton`, and no other kind does: an empty value of its type, which `props.mjs` resolves with the sub-project's own `typescript` in one program per run, or `[]`, `{}`, or `null` where it cannot ([component-library.md § The catalogue](component-library.md#the-catalogue-s-0437)). `hostapi.ListComponents` builds it for both the command and `components.list`. Since S-0443 each component carries its examples ([component-library.md § In the catalogue](component-library.md#in-the-catalogue)): `examples_path` is `components.ExamplesPath`, `<design folder>/components/<sub-project name>/<path, .svelte made .examples.json>`, given whether or not the file exists, and `examples` its file's, parsed by `components.Examples`; a file that does not fit the format gives the examples that parsed, and one that cannot be read fails the command, exit 1. The whole command fails, exit 1 and nothing printed, when a Svelte sub-project it lists cannot be read: `node` not on `PATH` (`components.ErrNodeMissing`, saying to install Node.js), `svelte` not installed there (`ErrSvelteMissing`, saying to run the package manager's install in it), a `svelte` older than 5, or `node` failing, timing out, or answering with what is not the expected JSON. `config` builds the key from the one there, each flag given replacing its field and the rest kept (`--include` and `--exclude` repeatable, each replacing its whole list; `--setup ""` clears setup), and writes it with `manifest.SetComponents`, which keeps every other line, the key order, and the comments. `--unset` writes the key away, so the defaults apply, and takes no other flag. It refuses with exit 1, writing nothing: no flag, `--unset` with another flag, an unknown sub-project (naming those there are), a key that would set no field (use `--unset`), and, as a `manifest.RefusedError` with each field and reason on standard output and `rule:` on standard error, whatever `Manifest.ComponentErrors` finds in the manifest as it would be, which `flai check` reports as `manifest.components`: a glob that does not parse, a setup that names no file under the sub-project's folder, and the key on a sub-project of an unsupported kind. It prints the key it wrote, `include`, `exclude`, and `setup` a line each, or that it was unset; `--json` is `{project, components}`, `components` null after `--unset`, and a refusal `{"refused": [{field, reason}]}`. `flai manifest set` does not reach the key. `serve` runs a sub-project's own Vite dev server with flai's harness in the foreground, as [component-library.md § The dev server](component-library.md#the-dev-server) describes. `components.PickSubProject` takes the sub-project `--project` names, of kind `sveltekit` or `svelte`, or the only such one when it names none, and refuses an unknown name (naming those there are), one of another kind, a manifest with none, and, with no name, one with more than one. `components.Serve` writes the harness with `WriteHarness` into `HarnessDir`, `.flai-cache/components/<project>/<sub-project>/`: the embedded `harness/` files and a `vite.config.mjs` that loads the sub-project's own config, the one `ViteConfig` finds, with the sub-project's own Vite's `loadConfigFromFile`, and merges the plugin into it with the cache dir `node_modules/.vite-flai-components`. `StartDevServer` then runs `<sub-project>/node_modules/.bin/vite --config <it> --host 127.0.0.1 --port <free> --strictPort` in the sub-project's folder, in a process group of its own, and returns once a GET of `HarnessPath`, `/__flai/components/`, answers 200, within `DevServerTimeout` (a minute); one that exits first or does not answer in time is stopped, and the error carries its last 20 lines of output that are not blank. A sub-project without `node_modules/.bin/vite` is refused with an error that wraps `ErrViteMissing` and names the sub-project, its folder, and `npm install or pnpm install`. The command prints the harness's URL on a line of its own, then, indented two spaces, `sub-project <name> (<path>), Vite pid <pid>; Ctrl-C stops it`; `--json` is `{project, url, port, pid}`. Vite's output goes to standard error as it comes. Ctrl-C or SIGTERM stops the process group (`DevServer.Stop`: SIGTERM, SIGKILL after five seconds) and exits 0; a dev server that exits on its own ends the command with exit 1 and its last lines. Since S-0477 ([ADR-0186](../adrs/0186-flai-guard-lets-every-agent-run-flai-components-list-and-a-story-s-task-sub.md)) `flai guard` lets every agent run `list`, and lets a story's task sub-agent run `serve`, but not a read-only sub-agent or the planner's, the orchestrator's, or the analyzer's own call; `config` stays the story's agent's (see `flai guard`). |
| `flai subproject list [--json]`, `subproject add <name> --kind <kind> [--path <path>] [--tags <a,b>]`, `subproject set <name> [--name <new>] [--path <path>] [--kind <kind>] [--tags <a,b>]`, `subproject remove <name>`, each write with `[--autocommit] [--trailer <line>]... [--json]` (S-0491) | The manifest's `projects`, the repository's sub-projects ([project-manifest.md](project-manifest.md)), read and changed without editing `system-flow.yaml` by hand (`cmd/subproject.go`, `manifest.EditProjects`). `list` prints a row per entry, in order: name, path, kind, tags, whether the path names a folder (`present` or `missing`), and whether it sets `components` and `install`; `--json` is `{"projects": [{name, path, kind, tags, folder, components, install}]}`, `tags` `[]` when none and `components` and `install` null when left out. `add` appends an entry, `--path` defaulting to the name; `set` replaces the keys given, `--tags ""` removing them and `--name` renaming, and keeps the rest, `components` and `install` among them, passing them as nil so their lines are kept; `remove` takes the entry out and leaves its folder. Each keeps every other line, the key order, and the comments. An add or set first runs `EditProjects` without the file system, so a refusal for the name or path writes nothing; then, when the entry's folder is missing (`os.Stat` says it does not exist), creates it with a `README.md` holding `# <name>` and a line naming the kind and the command, so git carries the folder; then runs `EditProjects` against `os.DirFS` of the root and, on a refusal, removes the topmost folder it created. A path that is a file, or under one, is left to that check, which refuses it as no folder. A remove is checked without the file system, so another entry's missing folder does not stop it. Refusals print as `flai components config`'s do: each field and reason on standard output, `rule:` on standard error, exit 1, and `{"refused": [{field, reason}]}` with `--json`; an add of a name there is, and a set or remove of one there is not (naming those there are), are plain errors. `--autocommit` commits `system-flow.yaml` and the `README.md` written, and nothing else, with each `--trailer`, unless `dashboard.autocommit` is false; the message is `chore: <action> sub-project <name>`. The text says the entry, the folder created, each path written, and the commit; `--json` is `{action, project, from, created, written, commit}`, `project` the entry as written (as it was for `remove`), `from` the old name after a rename, `created` the folder made, `written` the paths relative to the root, and `commit` only when one was made. `flai guard` lets no sub-agent run it, `list` included, since `cliReads` does not name it. |
| `flai mcp` | MCP server on stdio for agent sessions (ADR-0020, S-0039), built on the official Go SDK. Tools: `inbox` (unresolved threads, `awaiting: you` when the last entry is not the agent's, but `other` on a thread it opened while a recommendation on it awaits the operator's confirmation (S-0220, ADR-0090); since S-0453 ([ADR-0181](../adrs/0181-a-thread-awaits-an-agent-only-when-the-agent-takes-part-in-it-or-it-is-on-the.md)) `you` only when the agent is among the thread's participants or the thread is on its own story or a task of it, and otherwise `operator` when the thread awaits the operator (that opener's thread included) or `other`, with only `you` counted in `awaiting_you` and listed without `all`, and the orchestrator's role listing `operator` too; the stories ready to pull in pull order with `can_pull`, false while the in-progress limit is full or review is at or over its limit, and `pull_hold` saying which, S-0243; and the changes others made since this agent last looked, from a per-agent cursor under `.flai-cache/mcp/`, S-0058; an epic's move that followed a story reads `E-nnnn <title> moved to <state> by <who>, following S-nnnn` and carries `follows`, the story `workitem.FollowedWith` finds moved at the same time by the same actor, S-0200; at most 50 per look, newest kept, the rest counted in `changes_omitted` and never reported later, and a first look with no cursor covers 24 hours of stories and epics only, S-0061; and `unpublished`, the board view's IDs of accepted items no release has covered yet, as information: since S-0195 ([ADR-0067](../adrs/0067-accepted-work-reaches-the-remote-only-when-it-is-published-and-agents-publish.md)) neither `inbox` nor the server's instructions tell an agent to push, the instructions say publishing is the operator's and agents publish only when asked, and the `unpushed` fact of S-0063 is gone; a server that reads no git history says to run the command on the host), each timed as the phase its hostapi read is named for and each in `guard.MCPReads`, so a sub-agent and the planner may call them, since S-0222 the orchestrator's one write there, `release_publish` ([ADR-0094](../adrs/0094-with-publish-on-the-orchestrator-publishes-only-through-release-publish-by-its.md): `{reason?}`, one sentence, needed under `judgement` and the evaluation's reason when left out under `threshold` or `theme`; it refuses any server whose `FLAI_ROLE` is not `orchestrate`, and `orchestration.permissions.publish` off (`Repo.OrchestratorPermits`); it weighs the batch with `release.EvaluateRepo` and refuses, changing nothing (`mayPublish`), when nothing is pending, when `whole_epics` holds it back, naming each story, its epic, and the epic's status, when `threshold` or `theme` is not met, with the evaluation's reason, and under `judgement` without a reason; otherwise it publishes through `Options.Publish`, a `hostapi.Publisher` that `flai mcp` and `flai mcp http` fill (`app.mcpPublisher`), which runs `publish.run`'s `flai release --pending` (`hostapi.publishArgs`), never forced, under the `push` host action read at each call, refusing while it is off and naming `flai serve enable push`, and journals the run as `publish.run`'s with method `mcp.release_publish`, `by` the orchestrator (`orchestratorBy`: `orchestrator`, or `orchestrator (<agent>)` when the server's agent does not already say so), and `<policy>: <reason>` before the outcome in its detail; exit 3, newer remote tags (S-0174) or a moved remote branch, is a `Conflict` whose refusal is flai's message unchanged after `conflict:`, a `push_error` after tagging is a refusal saying the operator puts it right, and an answer with no plan is a refusal with flai's reason; it returns `{ policy, reason, evaluation, versions: [{ component, level, from, to, items }], tags, items, pushed, published?, warnings }`, `items` each ID once; without `Options.Publish`, or a runner to read git, it says it cannot and that publishing is the operator's on the host; `flai guard` gives it to the orchestrator alone, while `publish` is on), since S-0403 the orchestrator's enqueue, `queue_add` (`internal/mcpserver/queue.go`, [ADR-0093](../adrs/0093-with-accept-reviews-on-the-orchestrator-accepts-a-story-in-review-through-flai.md): `{story, verified, evidence}`, enqueued as `flai queue add --by orchestrator` enqueues, through `mcpserver.EnqueueByOrchestrator`; it refuses any server whose `FLAI_ROLE` is not `orchestrate`, and a server that reads no git history, naming the command to run on the host; it answers `mergequeue.Change` as `flai queue add --json` prints it; `flai guard` gives it to the orchestrator alone, while `accept_reviews` is on), and `wait_for_work` (S-0097: `resume` with the agent's own story in progress, known by its narrative's `agent`; `thread` with threads awaiting it written to since the last answer; `pull` with the first ready story in pull order that is not held (`workitem.FirstClear`, S-0128) while `CanPull`; otherwise it waits, re-deciding on every change to thread, kanban, and narrative files, up to `timeout_seconds`, 300 s by default and capped as `wait_for_events` is, and a timeout carries `waiting_for`: `room` (the in-progress limit is full), `review` (review is full and waits on acceptance, S-0243), `held` (every ready story is held while there is room; each ready card says why), or `ready`); `inbox`, `board`, and `wait_for_work`'s ready cards carry `held` as `flai board --json` does; `item_move` of a held story to in-progress moves it with the warning `S-nnnn is held (…)`, as `flai move` does (`Repo.Move`), `wait_for_events` (returns at once when the cursor is behind, otherwise polls thread, kanban, and narrative files, and since S-0331 `wip/messages`, every 250 ms and returns on the first change or the timeout, `timeout_seconds`, 60 s by default; returns events as well as paths and advances the cursor). Since S-0331 a message from the other story of a conversation of the agent's own story is an event of kind `message`, reported once per cursor, with `id` and `title` the conversation's, `cause` the sender's story, `by` its agent, and `to` the paths it is about; the agent's own entries are not reported back. For a settled entry (S-0372) its text says `settled: read it with message_get; no reply is owed`, and `inbox` gives a settled conversation an empty `awaiting` and `last.settled`. Since S-0272 it answers a story's agent that flai serve started (`FLAI_STORY` set, no `FLAI_ROLE`) at once with `end: true` and `why`, after any events already behind the cursor, when a thread on its story or one of its tasks awaits an answer to the agent's own question and no task of the story is in progress (`endWhy`, with a copy of serve's `awaitsAnswer`): the agent writes its narrative's state and ends, and flai serve, whose rule that is, starts it again on the answer. Since S-0335 it answers the same when a conversation of the story awaits the other story's reply (`awaitingReplies`), which a settled one does not, but never while one awaits the story's own reply, since a message it owes is work left: the `why` names each thread `open to the designer` and each conversation `awaiting S-nnnn's agent`, and says flai serve starts it again when it is answered or a new message to the story comes. Otherwise it holds as before; the multi-project server's `wait_for_events` never answers `end`. It does not see a sub-agent finish, since a sub-agent writes no work item, thread, or narrative; its description says so, and that a story's agent waits for one by launching it with the Agent tool's `run_in_background` set to false, and `flai guard` refuses a story's agent the call while a sub-agent of its session runs and no thread on its story is open (S-0285, [ADR-0092](../adrs/0092-a-story-s-agent-waits-for-a-sub-agent-by-launching-it-in-the-foreground-and.md)). Since S-0244 (I-0059) both waits are capped at `mcpserver.LongestWait`, 30 minutes, not five (`holdFor`), because every return is a model turn that rereads the agent's context; while held, `keepAlive` sends a progress notification every minute to a call that carries a progress token, since Claude Code drops a tool call that sends nothing for 30 minutes over stdio and 5 over HTTP (`CLAUDE_CODE_MCP_TOOL_IDLE_TIMEOUT`). Resources: `flai://design/{+path}` and `flai://docs/{+path}`. The agent is `--agent`, else `FLAI_AGENT` (S-0114: flai serve names the agent it started with the flag, because a harness's own settings can put a `FLAI_AGENT` of their own into the server's environment, which merged every started agent into one name, I-0037); every write goes through the same packages as the CLI; stdout carries the protocol only. Started in a folder with no `system-flow.yaml` up the tree (S-0101, [ADR-0036](../adrs/0036-a-folder-that-is-not-a-project-is-served-whole-by-flai-mcp-and-flai-dashboard.md)) it serves every project `workitem.FindProjects` finds in the folder and three levels below (not into hidden folders or build output), found again every 5 s (`Options.Rescan`). A `projects` provider picks the project a call names: `single` for one project, `folder` for several. Every per-project tool is registered through `route`, and its input has `project` (a key, or the project's folder, absolute or relative to the served folder), needed only when there is more than one. A single-project server refuses any other. In a folder, `inbox` answers with a `projects` list (each with `project`, `name`, `folder`, and that project's inbox), `wait_for_work` answers with `project` and `folder` and each project's `ready`, `can_pull`, and `pull_hold`, and `wait_for_events` tags each event with `project` and gives changed paths relative to the folder. Each project keeps its own cursor, and `wait_for_work`'s since-when is the folder's. With no project there it starts, and per-project tools say there is none. |
| `flai mcp http`, `start`, `stop`, `status`, `token [--rotate]` (S-0076; since S-0105 a rotation restarts a server flai serve started with the same `--exit-with`, recorded in its state as `exit_with`, so that it still goes with flai serve; [ADR-0030](../adrs/0030-mcp-is-served-by-flai-on-the-host-over-stdio-and-http-and-the-dashboard-s-api.md), [ADR-0034](../adrs/0034-flai-serve-keeps-each-served-project-s-http-mcp-server-running.md)) | The same server over Streamable HTTP, a process of its own on the host, one per project, for agents that cannot start `flai mcp` themselves; the dashboard serves no MCP. Since S-0106 `flai host` keeps one running for every project `flai serve` serves (see `flai host`; S-0096 had `flai serve` do it): it starts `flai mcp http --exit-with <its pid>` unless one already runs for the project, which it uses and never stops; starts its own again when it ends; stops it when `flai serve` no longer names the project or the host stops; and the hidden `--exit-with` ends the child by itself once that PID is gone (looked at every 2 s), so a host killed outright leaves none behind. A project with no remembered address takes the first free port from 4243 (twenty tried) that the host has not given another project, and that no other project flai serve serves remembers (`.flai-cache/mcp-http.addr`), and keeps it. A remembered address the host has already given another project is not given twice: the project moves to a new port the same way, and the host logs `mcp address moved` with both. Two projects remembering 4243 made one crash-loop on bind (S-0110). A token rotation of a server the host keeps (its `exit_with` is the running host's PID) stops it and waits for the host to start it again on the new token. `flai serve status` names each project's running server (`mcp:` line; `mcp` by root in `--json`). `internal/mcphttp` wraps the SDK's handler and decides only who may ask, under what name, and how many at once; the tools, rules, and cursor are `internal/mcpserver`'s on both transports. `http` runs in the foreground at `/mcp` on `127.0.0.1:4243` unless `--addr` says otherwise (remembered in `.flai-cache/mcp-http.addr`; beyond loopback is allowed and warned about); `start` detaches it and waits for its state file, `stop` signals the recorded PID, `status` prints the address and an agent's configuration and never the token. State `.flai-cache/mcp-http.json` is rewritten every 3 s and stale after 10 s, as `flai serve`'s is; log `.flai-cache/mcp-http.log`; token `.flai-cache/mcp.token` (0600), its own and not the dashboard's, compared in constant time; `--rotate` restarts a running server. A request with an `Origin` header is refused before the token is looked at. The agent is `X-Flai-Agent`, else the client's name (`initialize`, or the request's `_meta` clientInfo), made safe, else `agent`; one `mcp.Server` per agent name is kept and shared by its sessions. Two SDK handlers sit behind the one address, chosen by `Mcp-Protocol-Version`: with sessions for revisions up to 2025-11-25 (`--idle` 30m, `--max-sessions` 16, counted from the servers' live sessions, a loose bound by design) and stateless for 2026-07-28, which the SDK serves over HTTP only that way; 64 requests in flight on both, 503 beyond. Answers are JSON, not event streams; no write timeout, because `wait_for_events` and `wait_for_work` hold a request for up to 30 minutes (S-0244), and the SDK pauses a session's `--idle` timer while one of its requests is open; on a stop, held `wait_for_events` calls are ended as waits that ran out (`mcpserver.Options.Closing`; the SDK does not tie a session's call to its HTTP request), anything else gets 5 s, and then connections are closed, so `flai mcp stop` took 0.3 s with an agent waiting where it took 5 s before. Every answer carries `X-Flai-Project-Key` and `X-Flai-Project-Name`. It serves the main checkout and reads the manifest once, at start Outside any project `http`, `start`, `status`, `stop`, and `token` refuse and say what to run instead: in a project, or `flai mcp` on stdio in the folder (S-0101, [ADR-0036](../adrs/0036-a-folder-that-is-not-a-project-is-served-whole-by-flai-mcp-and-flai-dashboard.md)). |
| `flai self-upgrade [--check] [--list] [--version X.Y.Z] [--dir DIR]` | With `--list`, list the published releases and install nothing; with `--version`, refuse a release that is not published (S-0298, [Listing and installing a published release](#listing-and-installing-a-published-release)). Otherwise resolve the newest `flai/v*` release (or the pinned one), download the platform archive and `checksums.txt` through the release asset API, verify the SHA-256, and replace the running executable atomically (`--dir` installs elsewhere; with no `--dir` it replaces whatever `os.Executable()` returns, wherever that binary was installed). A request the network drops, a connection that fails or a body cut off partway, is made again, up to three attempts in all a second apart; a non-2xx answer, such as a 401 or 404, fails at once with its hint ([I-0086](../issues/I-0086-the-close-out-s-install-smoke-test-failed-once-and-passed-when-run-alone-with-no-cause-in-its-output.md)). Token from `GITHUB_TOKEN`, `GH_TOKEN`, or `gh auth token`. `install.sh` at the repository root is the same operation for a shell one-liner, installing into `FLAI_INSTALL_DIR` (default `$HOME/.flai/bin`, beside flai's own config and cache under `~/.flai`, ADR-0008): always a directory the user already owns, so neither the script nor `self-upgrade` ever needs `sudo`, and an agent or a scheduled job can run `flai self-upgrade` on itself once installed. `install.sh` tries a GitHub call the network drops three times in all, two seconds apart, and never one curl fails with an HTTP error, and it resolves the latest release from pages of ten, asking for the next only while no `flai/v*` tag has turned up, since a large listing is what GitHub drops ([I-0086](../issues/I-0086-the-close-out-s-install-smoke-test-failed-once-and-passed-when-run-alone-with-no-cause-in-its-output.md)). `scripts/install-test.sh` exercises both in the smoke tier, including the default path, against releases built from the tree (`scripts/flai-snapshot.sh --local`) and served on `127.0.0.1` by `scripts/release-server.sh` (`internal/releaseserver`), which answers the release listing, a release by tag, and an asset download as GitHub does, under the stand-in repository `local/flai`: no token, no request to GitHub (S-0340). `scripts/install-published-test.sh` checks the latest published release from GitHub the same way; CI's `install-published.yml` runs it on push to main and daily, and no close-out does. The API base is the hidden `--api`, whose default is `FLAI_RELEASES_API` when set, else `https://api.github.com` (S-0107): a mirror, or a stand-in that answers as GitHub's releases API does. `flai host check` and `upgrade` run `self-upgrade`, so they follow it too; S-0107's trial upgraded a scratch host from a stand-in release that way. With no `--dir`, a running flai inside a system-flow project (`insideProject`: `system-flow.yaml` in a folder above it, as for a checkout's `bin/flai`) installs to `$FLAI_INSTALL_DIR` or `~/.flai/bin` instead of over itself. Its up-to-date answer is about the flai there (`installedVersion`, `version --json`), and the JSON gives `path` and `running`. `flai host upgrade` restarts the host on the `path` the upgrade installed, so it and its children run the installed flai (S-0111). |

Global flags: `--config <path>`, `--json` where output is structured, `--yes` to skip confirmations, and `--verbose` for debug log events, so agents can drive it. stdout is command output; stderr carries structured log events per `design/conventions/logging.md` (`log/slog`, text on a terminal, JSON otherwise, `LOG_LEVEL` and `LOG_FORMAT`), and a failing command logs exactly one fatal event at the boundary.

### Pricing a run from its provider's spend log

S-0357, [ADR-0132](../adrs/0132-a-story-s-cost-comes-from-its-provider-s-spend-log-when-it-has-a-provider-from.md). A story's cost comes from its provider's spend log when its agent ran through a provider, from the harness's own figure when it did not, and is estimated otherwise. Its `usage` says which in `priced_by` ([work-hierarchy.md](work-hierarchy.md#identifiers)).

**The readers.** `usage.Spend` is the contract (`usage/spend.go`). Its `Run` answers what the gateway charged for one run: `Spent`, with the cost in US dollars, the calls where the gateway counts them, and `By`, how the reader joined the spend to the run (`generation`, `key`, `session`, or `window`). It wraps `usage.ErrNoSpend` when the gateway answers but holds nothing for the run yet. `RunRef` gives the run's harness session IDs, its window from its log's first event to its last, the gateway's generation IDs where the harness gives them, the key's usage read when the run started, and the SHA-256 in hex of the agent's key. A reader sends the key it is handed as a bearer and never puts it in an error. `usage.For` chooses the reader by the provider's `base_url`: OpenRouter's for `openrouter.ai` and its subdomains; none for `api.anthropic.com`, whose harness figure stands without a word; LiteLLM's for any other host.

- OpenRouter's (`usage/openrouter.go`) sums `total_cost` over the run's generations through `/api/v1/generation` when it has their IDs. A generation not recorded yet leaves the run unread. Claude Code gives none, so the run is joined by the key's usage instead: `data.usage` of `/api/v1/key`, read when the run starts and again at measurement. Each reading is the highest of three reads a second apart, since OpenRouter answers from copies that lag (S-0351). The run's cost is the rise; no rise leaves it unread. It is exact only when the key is the run's alone: another run on the same key in that span is counted too.
- LiteLLM's (`usage/litellm.go`) reads the proxy's `/spend/logs` rows with `summarize=false`, from the day the run started to the day after it ended, and with `api_key`, the hash of the agent's key, where it is known. It sums the rows whose `session_id` or `end_user` is one of the run's session IDs and that started within the run's window. With none, it sums the rows filed under the agent key's hash within the window. Only the proxy's master key reads the spend log: a virtual key gets 401, so the provider names the master key's variable in `spend_key_env`. An answer the proxy cut short (`x-litellm-spend-logs-truncated`), or one over 64 MB, is an error. No row for the run leaves it unread: the proxy writes spend in batches.

**The spend key.** `spendOf` resolves the provider as a start does, and reads the key in the variable its `spend_key_env` names, else in its `key_env`'s. A variable unset or empty leaves the run unread, and the reason names the variable and `flai serve agent provider <name> --spend-key-env`. A project's manifest cannot name `spend_key_env`, as it cannot name `key_env` ([project-manifest.md](project-manifest.md)). The agents flai serve starts never get a `spend_key_env` variable unless it is also some provider's `key_env` (`withoutSpendKeys`).

**The spend record.** When flai serve starts a story's agent through a provider, it keeps a spend record, `<log>.spend.json`, beside the run's log in its agents folder, mode 0600. Before the agent's first call (`spendAtStart`) it reads the key's usage where the reader joins by it, OpenRouter's, waiting at most 10 s. Once the log is known it writes the record (`keepSpend`): `provider`, and `key_before` when it was read. A read that fails is logged as `key usage not read` (warn), and the record goes without it; a write that fails, as `spend record not written` (warn). Once the gateway prices the run, the record gains `cost`, `by`, `priced_by: gateway`, and `at`. It never holds a key. The newest run whose provider flai serve's state names, with no record, is asked as if it had one without `key_before`.

**When flai serve asks.** At each measurement, when an agent ends, when a task is done while its story's agent runs, and on `flai serve agent usage`, `price` (`serve/usage.go`) prices each run of the story on its own, from what it added to the story's total (`usage.Record.RunTotals`). A run that spent nothing is left out. A run whose record is priced takes the record's cost, and its gateway is never asked again. A run whose record names a provider and has no price is asked once it has ended; the run still going is not asked. flai serve waits at most 10 s for each ask.

**What the price does.** The gateway's cost replaces the run's harness cost. It is spread over the run's models by their share of the harness's cost, or of their tokens when that is nothing; a cost spread over more than one model makes the story `estimated`. Tokens and seconds stay the harness's. A run through no provider keeps the harness's figure, `priced_by: harness`, or `estimate` when any of its cost was estimated. A run whose gateway fails or holds nothing keeps the harness's figure or the estimate, is listed in `Measured.Unread`, and is asked again at the next measurement. flai serve logs it as `spend not read` (warn) with the story, the provider, the log, and the error, and `flai serve agent usage` writes the same on stderr. The story's `priced_by` is the least certain of its runs': `estimate` below `harness` below `gateway` (`usage.LeastCertain`). A task's share scales each model's cost by the ratio of the story's priced cost of that model to the harness's, and takes the story's `priced_by`. Up the hierarchy an item carries the least certain of its children's ([work-hierarchy.md](work-hierarchy.md#identifiers)).

### Reaching the forge

S-0392, [ADR-0165](../adrs/0165-a-project-chooses-local-or-pull-request-acceptance-in-its-manifest-flai-reaches.md). A project whose manifest sets `acceptance.mode: pull-request` is to accept a story through a pull request on its forge, `acceptance.forge` ([project-manifest.md](project-manifest.md)). S-0392 laid the ground: the setting, the contract, and the token. S-0393 pushes a story's branch and opens or updates its pull request ([The forge pass](#the-forge-pass), below). S-0394 reads its required checks and reviews back and sends the story back on a failure ([Reading the pull request back](#reading-the-pull-request-back)). S-0395 has every acceptance wait for them ([The acceptance gate](#the-acceptance-gate)). S-0397 has `flai accept` merge it on the forge by `acceptance.merge_method` ([The merge through the remote](#the-merge-through-the-remote)).

**The contract.** `flai/internal/forge` defines `Forge`, bound to one repository when it is made, so no method takes an owner or a name:

| Method | Answers |
|--------|---------|
| `Access` | Whether the token can read the repository and push to it |
| `FindPullRequest` | The open pull request whose head is a branch, or none |
| `PullRequest` | The pull request by its number, open, closed, or merged, or `ErrNotFound`; `FindPullRequest` sees open ones only, so it cannot tell a merged request from a missing one (S-0397) |
| `OpenPullRequest` | A pull request opened from a head into a base, or the open one for that head updated |
| `Checks` | The checks on the head commit, each with whether the base branch requires it; a required check that has not reported is pending |
| `Reviews` | The reviews on the pull request, oldest first |
| `RequiredApprovals` | The most approving reviews the base branch requires before a pull request into it merges, 0 when none (S-0395) |
| `Merge` | The pull request merged by `merge`, `squash`, or `rebase`, while its head is still the commit given; refused with `ErrMergeRefused` when the forge will not merge or the head moved |

Callers test errors with `ErrNotFound`, `ErrUnauthorized`, `ErrForbidden`, and `ErrMergeRefused`. What is a forge's own stays in its adapter: its API paths, its remote URLs, and, for GitHub, check runs beside commit statuses and rulesets beside branch protection. A GitLab adapter implements `Forge` and adds `gitlab` to the forges the manifest and the host configuration take.

**The GitHub adapter.** `forge.NewGitHub` (`github.go`) speaks GitHub's REST API, `https://api.github.com` unless `GitHubOptions.BaseURL` names another, with the standard library alone, so it needs nothing on `PATH` and is tested against an `httptest` server. Each call has a 30 s timeout. A list follows the `Link` header's next page, up to 50 pages of 100, and refuses a link outside the API, which would take the token with it. Required checks come from the base branch's rulesets and its classic protection; either out of reach (403 or 404, as for a token that is not an admin) leaves its names out rather than failing the read. Required approvals come from the same two places, the rulesets' `pull_request` rule and the protection's `required_pull_request_reviews`: the higher count wins, and a rule out of reach counts as none. `forge.ParseGitHubRemote` reads the owner and name from an https, `git@github.com:`, or `ssh://` remote, with or without `.git`, on github.com only, and never repeats the credentials a URL carries.

**The token.** An adapter gets the token from a `forge.TokenFunc` at each call and keeps nothing it answers. `config.ForgeTokenFunc` reads it from the variable `forges.<forge>.token_env` names in the host's configuration, `GITHUB_TOKEN` when unset, only when flai calls the forge, and refuses an unset or empty variable, naming it and `flai serve forge <forge> --token-env`. The token goes in the `Authorization` header alone. It is never written to the configuration, a log, a work item, an error, or a start record; `flai serve forge` and `flai forge check` name the variable and say whether it is set. `flai serve` reads its own environment, so the variable must be exported where it starts, as a provider's key is.

**The test against GitHub.** `flai/tests/integration/forge_github_test.go` drives the adapter against a real repository, read-only, when `FLAI_FORGE_TEST_REPO` (`owner/name`) and `GITHUB_TOKEN` are set, and skips, naming what is missing, otherwise. `FLAI_FORGE_TEST_HEAD`, a branch with an open pull request, has it read that pull request's checks and reviews too, and `FLAI_FORGE_TEST_API` names a GitHub Enterprise API address.

### The forge pass

S-0393, [ADR-0166](../adrs/0166-under-pull-request-acceptance-flai-serve-pushes-a-story-s-branch-and-opens-or.md). `flai serve` runs one forge pass per served project (`internal/serve/forge.go`), in the background so that a slow push or forge call holds up none of the project's other looks. At start and then once a minute, while the `forge` host action is on for the project and its manifest sets `acceptance.mode: pull-request`, it looks at each story in review and calls `PushStory`, which `flai forge push` calls too.

- **The push.** With the host's git credentials, `story/S-nnnn` goes to `origin` when origin's branch is missing or not the local head: plainly when origin's head is in the local branch, and with `--force-with-lease=refs/heads/story/S-nnnn:<head flai last pushed>` when a sync rebased it. A branch whose origin head is not in it and that flai never pushed is refused, since flai cannot tell those commits are its own. So is a push the lease refuses because someone pushed since. Each reason names how to bring their commits in or put origin's branch back. Nothing but a story's own branch is pushed, and only that branch, with the lease, is ever forced.
- **The pull request.** Found by its head branch, so nothing is written to the story and a clone on another host finds the same one.
